The Engine then checks version, locking, domain rules and resource availability, and returns the receipt after the atomic commit. The Node must not use the “check the version first, blind-write later” pattern, which races after an existence check.

Read requests either name a version or take latest; long-running tasks must first obtain a frozen `snapshotRef` before running. Only explicit admin import and restore commands may replace a whole snapshot; ordinary UI and agents may not submit a JSON Patch to an arbitrary path.

### 4.4 Events, snapshots and reconnection

Every video event contains `videoId`, `eventSeq`, `videoRevision`, `transactionId`, `changedIds` and directly consumable projection changes. `eventSeq` and the video revision are persisted in the VideoStore; the Runtime epoch only identifies the service's run generation. Session sequence numbers, Job sequence numbers and video event sequence numbers are managed separately and do not share one vague cursor.

- A subscription must obtain a snapshot at a given watermark, together with the events after it.
- Video changes and the outbox are written to disk in the same transaction. The Runtime pushes from the committed outbox and allows at-least-once delivery; the client deduplicates by video / eventSeq.
- If the Runtime crashes after commit but before broadcast, it must not lose the recovery path for confirmed edits.
- When the client finds a gap in sequence numbers, it holds back commits and either fills in the missing events or refetches a snapshot; it must not keep editing a mirror that is missing events.
- Candidates and transient UI gestures are stored separately; a failed reconnect must not overwrite the server with an old full snapshot.

### 4.5 Control channel and media channel are separate

WebM playback gets its URL through `media.playback { url, playable? }`. `url` must be a restricted handle that the current media channel has issued and that has not expired; a local path or a URL from another source is not allowed. `playable` lists the WebM codecs the client can decode natively, as measured with `canPlayType` (`vp8`, `vp9`, `av1`, `opus`, `vorbis`). The Runtime uses ffprobe to detect the codecs of the source file's first video and audio streams (remembered per source identity). If both are within that set, it returns the original handle directly, with no copy and no waiting (this is the path for Chromium and Electron). Otherwise it prepares a compatible copy: the Runtime checks the source file's real path again and generates an H.264/yuv420p + AAC MP4 under `<home>/cache/media/playback/`, keyed by the source's real path, size, nanosecond mtime and encoding recipe revision. The longest side is at most 1280; aspect ratio and source timing are kept, and the audio track is kept when present. Silent video and audio-only WebM are supported too. Encoding runs in the background with a single slot; repeated requests are merged; output is published atomically once it is verified decodable. A change in the source triggers a rebuild; on failure no half-finished output is published, and the Runtime stops the child process on shutdown. The cache is for playback only: project assets, downloads and exports still reference the original file.

When the copy is not ready, it returns `{ status: 'pending', retryAfterMs: 250, progress? }` immediately (`progress` is the ffmpeg output time divided by the probed source duration, a 0–1 value; omitted when the duration cannot be probed). When complete it returns `{ status: 'ready', media: MediaHandle }`; failures use the existing media errors. The client polls with short requests and does not hold a whole encode open on one RPC. The standalone player, media previews in chat and editor asset URLs share the same preparation flow and keep the existing seek, volume, playback-rate and pause semantics. Electron and Web share the same UI; Web uses its own restricted-handle table and same-origin URLs, and read-only mode may also generate derived caches. Non-WebM files and WebM that the client can decode natively reuse the original handle directly. `blob:` / `data:` URLs for attachments not yet sent stay in the browser and do not request conversion or upload from the Runtime. `media.resolve` and `?download=1` always serve the original bytes.

Each client has one multiplexed business WebSocket to each Runtime; videos, sessions and tasks are subscribed to per resource. Video and audio bytes, preview frames and thumbnails travel over authenticated HTTP Range, restricted handles or a dedicated media channel; base64 for every frame is not stuffed into conversation events. A single thumbnail under 1 MB may travel in the request's response (the timeline filmstrip's `media.thumbnail` and the Space card's `space.thumbnail`, §5.7): the client requests it on demand; it does not enter the event stream and is not pushed with subscriptions.

`media.resolve`, after the real-path and permission checks of `resolveInside`, reads at most a 64 KiB prefix to identify the content type. When the target is a video entry (`{ entryId }`, where a video is a directory), it returns that video's main asset: the asset version holding the cover frame, the same source as the Space thumbnail. It does not open the video and does not take the write lock. This is the original asset, without the timeline's cuts, subtitles and overlays; it is used for in-place playback of the video card in a conversation (product design §3.2.2). Without a cover it returns `not-found`. The desktop client also has a local-file target `{ localPath }` (only in `MediaTarget`, not `FileTarget`; it cannot be used to open or delete a video): documents or media copies outside the working directory that have no artifact record, referenced in a reply, are used for read-only preview in the file tab. The Runtime grants this only to desktop clients (`principal.kind === 'desktop'`); other principals (browser, CLI, external service) get `forbidden` under their existing access scope. The path must be absolute and goes through the same `resolveInside`, rooted at the file's own directory. Only regular files are allowed: directories, nonexistent paths, and paths reached through a symlink into another directory are all rejected. The content detection and size limits are unchanged, and directories are not granted. `MediaHandle` may carry `contentKind` (text/image/pdf/audio/video/archive/binary) and `textEncoding` (UTF-8, or UTF-16LE/BE with BOM); the HTTP MIME matches the detection result. When the fields are absent, old clients keep their behaviour. Archives are identified by header only and are not unpacked for preview. When loading text, the actual received bytes are limited again, and strict decoding rejects truncated encodings or later binary control characters. Media still goes through the restricted Range channel; sampling does not widen the readable directory. `?download=1` on the same restricted handle returns an attachment response with a UTF-8 file name; large video downloads stream directly and are not read fully into UI memory first.

The playback clock and scrubbing stay local to the UI. `requestGeneration` ensures that stale async frames do not overwrite a newer seek. Streaming text may merge small chunks but keeps message identity and completion boundaries; terminal events and permission events must never be lost to throttling. The client may show cached data, but “connected” does not mean “data is caught up”.

### 4.6 ContextBarrier: sync before sending

Before the user submits a natural-language request, establish an explicit read-your-writes barrier, so that “what I just changed is visible to the agent”.

```text
User submits request
  → Capture the selection, plus the related pending commandIds and text editing session
  → Settle related gestures that have ended and text that can be committed
  → Wait for durable receipts for these commands, or return failure / conflict
  → Catch up this window's related video projection to the required watermark
  → Get the context snapshot and occurrence references for that version from the Engine
  → Bind the ContextSelection, then start the Task / Turn
```

The barrier returns:

```ts
interface ContextBarrierResult {
  contextSnapshotRef: Id;
  videoRevision: Revision;
  eventSeq: string;
  includedCommandIds: Id[];
  selectionHash: string;
  candidateRef?: Id;        // must be given explicitly when referencing a candidate rather than the working draft
}
```

Rules:

- The barrier waits only for the local changes this request depends on; it does not wait indefinitely for all work in other windows or in the background.
- For gestures in progress that cannot safely end, and for IME composition text: prompt the user to wait, cancel sending, or explicitly exclude them. Never quietly commit half a drag in order to send a message.
- When a command fails, do not send old context that looks successful.
- The barrier solves “reading one's own writes”; it does not cancel the version check that follows. If another window commits afterwards, the agent's result must still be checked against the latest version.
- Pure chat that does not involve video may skip the barrier.
- The barrier applies equally to requests from the Home and Space entry points.
- Unpublished changes in the slide editor are also local changes this request depends on. When the message carries slide annotations, first have the editors that have these decks open publish (`editor:flush` / `editor:flushed`, waiting at most 10 seconds). If they have not published, do not send; keep the input and annotations (Slide design §8.2). When the message carries design annotations, do the same with the open design editor; with design-system annotations, do the same with the design-system editor.

For UI behaviour, see Product design §3.2.4.

### 4.7 Time input is part of the business contract

The UI and agents do not compute frames on their own.

- Visual time commands accept `TimelineTimeInput`: `seconds` and `frames` are mutually exclusive; `sequenceId` and `expectedRevision` determine which grid it belongs to.
- Word, sentence and occurrence anchors are the preferred inputs for voice-over and semantic editing.
- The Runtime handles authentication and forwarding; Rust parses, maps and quantizes uniformly and returns `TimeQuantizationReceipt`.
- The protocol distinguishes the requested formatted value, the canonical exact time, and the actual frame coordinate. Commands that carry only a bare frame, with no way to determine the Sequence and Rate, are always rejected.
- Changing the output frame rate uses `ExportSettings` in `exports.create` / `exports.updateDraft`; changing the editing frame rate uses `changeSequenceFrameRate`, which comes with an impact report. The two do not share one vague fps update interface.

Type definitions are in the Video Format Specification §2 and the Command and Protocol Specification §6.

### 4.8 External services

The local gateway (§4.1, §12.2) serves CucCut's own UI, the CLI and the agents it starts. **External services** are the entry points through which the Runtime opens itself to other programs and devices, managed centrally by `ServiceManager`:

| Service | For | Listens on | Contents |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `mcp` | Agents in other apps | Loopback | Tools in the tool catalog whose `surfaces` include `mcp` (§3.5): read videos and grab frames, create videos, derive child videos, edit scripts and translations, cut clips, create caption layers, start transcription / translation / dubbing / export / download from a link, synthesize speech, generate images and music, import and preview code compositions, and read skills and the user library (read-only) |
| `model-api` | Programs that speak the OpenAI API | Loopback | Transcription, speech synthesis, image generation and text generation using the model capabilities already configured on this machine |
| `web` | The local browser | Loopback | The web client and the gateway entry it connects to |
| `node` | Other CucCut instances on the LAN | LAN | Capability sharing (§6.7) |

**Shared lifecycle.** Every service has the same set of states: `off`, `starting`, `on`, `stopping` and `error`. The default is `off`, and the user turns a service on explicitly. Configuration (whether it starts with the Runtime, the port, the access policy and the clients issued) is persisted in `store/services.json` under the Runtime Home (mode 0600, written via temp file and rename) and restored at startup. If the port is already in use, the service enters `error` and explains why. It does not quietly start on a different port, and it does not stop the Runtime from starting. Services stop with the Runtime (§2.4), ahead of tasks in the stop order. `services.list`, `services.start`, `services.stop` and `services.configure` manage them. State changes are delivered on the `services` topic, and the badge on the Rail reads it. The existing `nodes.share.*` methods for the node service are a dedicated form of this set for the node service: the node service's state has only one source, `NodeService`, and what `services.*` reads is a projection of it. Port, capabilities and pairing are still configured with `nodes.share.*`. This version provides all four services. A service that a later version or platform does not provide is still listed, with state `off`, and enabling it is refused with `SERVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE`.

**Service principal.** External requests have no session and no TaskContract. For each authenticated external connection, the Runtime constructs `TrustedPrincipal { kind: 'service', serviceId, clientId }`. Writes then enter the same command entry point with `actor` set to `external:<serviceId>`, with the same version checks, protection scopes, undo and receipts (§4.2, §4.3). `actor.kind` keeps `agent` (the engine's operator kind does not change), and `actor.id` is `external:<serviceId>`. External requests cannot impersonate a UI connection, and they cannot obtain the local gateway's token. A service principal is constructed only by the external service's authentication; the gateway's `hello` cannot declare one. Jobs submitted externally record `submitter: { kind: 'service', id: <serviceId>, clientId }` (§7.9), and a client sees only the Jobs it submitted and their artifacts. The web service is the exception: in the browser, the operator is the user themselves, not an external program. Its principal is `{ kind: 'web', sessionId }`, and writes carry `actor` = `user_local` (the same as the desktop UI, sharing its undo stack). Jobs are submitted per connection, as in the desktop UI. Version checks, protection scopes, undo and receipts are unchanged. The `web` principal is likewise constructed only by the service's authentication, and `hello` cannot declare it.

**Access policy** takes the place of TaskContract in permission checks (the last item in §3.5). It has three parts:

- **Scope**: which videos may be accessed, either all of them or a list of `videoId`s, and only within registered projects. Videos outside the scope do not exist for this service: they are absent from listings, and access by ID returns "not found" rather than "no permission". They also do not trigger an open or a generation approval. The default is all videos.
- **Operation level**: `read` (queries only; the catalog shows only tools with `readOnlyHint`), `ask` (writes, tasks and generation are confirmed one by one in CucCut) and `auto` (executed directly). Tools below the required level do not appear in the catalog at all, rather than failing when called. The default is `ask`.
- **Confirmation**: a request under `ask` creates a service approval that shows the caller, the tool, the target video and a summary of the parameters. The user allows or denies it in CucCut. Approvals have a time limit of 50 seconds, which is shorter than the 60-second request timeout of common MCP clients, so the caller receives a clear denial. A timeout counts as a denial. Service approvals are produced by the `ApprovalService` shared by the session (§3.12), based on the service level and the tool's risk (the tool risk table in §3.12). They have no session to attach to: pending approvals are part of the `services` topic snapshot and are answered with `services.respondToApproval`. The same item also appears in the unified list on the `tasks` topic (principal: services and clients), where answering it with `approvals.respond` has the same effect. A denial, a timeout or a cancellation (the service stopping or the caller disconnecting) all answer the caller with `SERVICE_APPROVAL_DENIED`.

Data egress and budget are still checked separately. When an external request triggers an online provider, that provider must already be enabled by the user (§6.4), and a Grant must cover this egress (§12.5). The `auto` level in the access policy does not mean authorization for any online call: under `auto`, egress not covered by a Grant is refused directly with `GRANT_REQUIRED` (with a remedy) and no approval is generated. Under `ask`, it becomes a `high` service approval that carries the data to be authorized. If allowed, a Grant is issued through the `grant` option of `approvals.respond` (§3.12). When the budget is used up, both levels refuse with `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` before approval and submission. For MCP tools and the model API service, this step comes after the routing check.

**External names are contracts.** Once another program uses the MCP tool names and parameters, the model API service paths, the CLI commands or the Skill names, renaming them is a breaking change. They always use the names from the glossary (the editable entity is `video`, and the catalog is `project`), not names from prototypes or older versions. Each service reports its interface version in the handshake or in its metadata, and breaking changes bump the version.

**The MCP service** and the agent tool bridge (§3.5) share the same tool implementation and MCP endpoint code. The only difference is the principal. The tool bridge's token is bound to a session and its TaskContract, while an MCP service connection is bound to the service principal and the access policy. The difference is kept in a single scope interface (the session's source catalog and TaskContract, or the service's access policy), so tools themselves do not depend on their source. Slow tools return a `jobId` immediately, and status is read with a query tool. The MCP service listens on `127.0.0.1`, default port 47620. It is a stateless Streamable HTTP endpoint, and each request is authenticated separately. Exposed tools are derived from the catalog entries' `surfaces`: only those containing `mcp` are exposed. The protocol constant `MCP_SERVICE_TOOL_NAMES` lists the same set (names, titles and `effect`). Tests verify that the two match, and the UI's tool list reads it.

Queries are `videos_list`, `videos_inspect`, `videos_history`, `videos_shots`, `documents_read`, `edits_ops`, `models_capabilities`, `models_list`, `jobs_inspect`, `jobs_wait`, `jobs_list`, `projects_list`, `space_list`, `space_search`, `skills_list`, `skills_read`, `library_list` and `library_show`. Space entries and cross-video search cover only videos within the access policy scope, and only entries generated or exported by those videos. Task artifacts cover only the ones the caller submitted. Writes, tasks and generation are `videos_create` (requires `project`, and creates the video in that registered project. When the access policy is a video list, the new video is written into the list), `edits_apply` (cut clips, edit scripts and translations, import media from the project), `documents_put`, `assets_import` (media must be a file in the project directory), `edits_undo`, `captions_create`, `videos_frames` (frames are written to `exports/.cuccut-out/frames/<videoId>/` in the video's project), `compositions_import` (import a code composition: validate, bake a pre-render, import two assets and place one composition clip, as two undoable edits; `path` must be a directory inside the project directory), `compositions_preview` (preview a code composition: grab frames at local composition times, written to `exports/.cuccut-out/frames/<videoId>/` in the video's project; `path` likewise must be inside the project directory), `transcribe`, `translate`, `dub` (for `video`; `file` and `outDir` are refused with `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`, and `next` points to `video` and `url`; `transcribe` given a `url` follows the same destination rules as `download`), `speak`, `image`, `export` (exports subtitles, scripts, audio and the final cut; files may only be written to `exports/` in the video's project and to subdirectories that already exist there), and `download` (from a link; the destination must be an existing video `video` within scope or a registered project `project`. Files go into the owning project's `downloads/` (§7.9). `newVideo` must be given together with `project` (as in `videos_create`). A video created by the flow is registered in the service's scope when the parent task records it (written into the list when the access policy is a video list), after which `videos_list` and `videos_inspect` can see it. Without a destination, or with `newVideo` but no `project`, the call is refused with `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`, and `next` points to `projects_list` for a `projectId`. When downloading into an existing video whose timeline is still empty, the media is also placed on the main track (§7.9). If yt-dlp is not installed, or the user has not consented to its use, the call is refused outright. It is not installed or consented to on the caller's behalf (§12.9)), `jobs_cancel` (cancels only jobs the caller submitted) and `jobs_retry` (re-runs only flows the caller submitted). To wait for a task, use `jobs_wait` (at most 50 seconds per call. If it has not finished, call again, §3.5), rather than polling `jobs_inspect` repeatedly. Tools whose `surfaces` do not include `mcp` are not exposed: deleting videos (`videos_delete`), saving artifacts to files (`artifacts_save`), copying to the downloads directory (`downloads_save`), opening portable packages (`videos_import_package`), file transcoding (`transcode`, which has only one input type: a local file), installing and checking local model packs (`models_install`, `models_test`), creating projects (`projects_create`), requesting authorization (`grants_request`), and the task contract tools.

The interface version is reported in `serverInfo.version` from `initialize` and in `_meta['cuccut.interfaceVersion']`. It is now `2`. The exposed list is now derived from `surfaces`, and some tools were renamed: `models_transcribe` → `transcribe` (fixed-flow shape), `exports_create` → `export`, `link_import_start` → `download`, `skill_read` → `skills_read`, `models_synthesize_speech` → `speak`, `models_generate_image` → `image`. The old names are not kept. Tools exposed later (`videos_frames`, `videos_shots`, `videos_history`, `documents_put`, `assets_import`, `edits_ops`, `jobs_wait`, `jobs_list`, `jobs_retry`, `models_list`, `projects_list`, `skills_list`, `library_list`, `library_show`, `compositions_import`, `compositions_preview`) are additions only and do not bump the version.

**Connecting an external agent.** `cuccut mcp install --agent <claude-code|codex|cursor|gemini> [--level ask|auto] [--name <client name>] [--yes]` (CLI management group) does the whole connection in one step. It first reads the host's MCP configuration. If a `cuccut` entry already exists and `--yes` was not given, it refuses and issues no token. It then sets the MCP service to start with the Runtime (if `--level` is given, the level is changed too. If not, nothing changes, and the service defaults to `ask`) and enables it. It creates a new client for this host (the name defaults to the host name) and obtains a token. `services.mcp.connectionInfo` provides the address, which is written into the host's user-level MCP configuration (entry name `cuccut`), and the user is prompted to restart the host. If the Runtime has no registered projects yet (a fresh install, the App never opened), it registers the `CLI` project under the default project directory (`projects.open`, the same one used when creating videos with no project from the terminal) before issuing the token. The result's `defaultProject` states that the service must be given a registered project to create videos, and without one an external agent has nowhere to create them. If writing the configuration fails, the client just created is revoked. When `--yes` replaces an existing entry, the client it used is identified from the old entry's token (the token prefix is the `clientId`). After the new entry is written, that client is revoked, and the result says so. If the client cannot be identified (for example, a hand-written token), the result lists the clients with the same name so the user can deal with them. The token's destination depends on the host. Claude Code stores it in the `env` section of its `settings.json` (`CUCCUT_MCP_TOKEN`), and the `.claude.json` entry holds only the reference `Bearer ${CUCCUT_MCP_TOKEN}` (written via `claude mcp add --scope user --transport http` when `claude` is on PATH). Codex (`[mcp_servers.cuccut]` in `config.toml`, with `url` and `http_headers`), Cursor (`~/.cursor/mcp.json`) and Gemini CLI (`~/.gemini/settings.json`) have no usable environment-variable section, so the token is written in plain text into their configuration (see §12.8 for secure handling). The service is available only while the Runtime is running. `cuccut mcp status` lists service state, address, clients, and whether each host has this entry. It does not display tokens.

**The model API service** maps OpenAI-shaped requests to a single capability call with no video (§7.9). Transcription, synthesis, image generation and text generation each enter the matching capability. Each request is one Job (`submitter: { kind: 'service', id: 'model-api', clientId }`, `videoId: null`). Its record and artifacts are the generation record of that call. Once the Job finishes, its result is returned directly as the response. It listens on `127.0.0.1`, default port 47621. Tokens are issued per client, separate from the MCP service's tokens and not interchangeable. The client and token implementation is shared, and the management methods are `services.modelApi.*`, with the same shape as `services.mcp.*`. Endpoints:

| Endpoint | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/{model}` | Models the service can route to right now: aliases first, then `<providerId>/<modelId>`. Only available models are listed, and the `cuccut` field carries capabilities and provider |
| `POST /v1/audio/transcriptions` | multipart: `file`, `model`, `language` (handled as an assertion), `prompt` (term hints), `response_format` as `json`, `text`, `srt`, `vtt` or `verbose_json`, and `timestamp_granularities[]` as `word` or `segment` |
| `POST /v1/audio/speech` | `input`, `voice`, `instructions`, `speed`, `response_format` as `mp3`, `wav` or `flac`. The response is audio bytes |
| `POST /v1/images/generations` | `prompt`, `n`, `size`, `output_format`. `response_format` accepts only `b64_json`: this service does not host images, so a request for `url` gets 400 `UNSUPPORTED_PARAMETER` |
| `POST /v1/chat/completions` | `messages` (`system`, `developer`, `user`, `assistant`, plain text only), `temperature`, `max_tokens` / `max_completion_tokens` (the latter takes precedence), `seed`, `reasoning_effort`, `response_format` as `json_object` or `json_schema`. Tool calls, `n > 1` and `logprobs` get 400 `UNSUPPORTED_PARAMETER`. `stream: true` is not truly incremental: the full result is returned as SSE with a single content chunk, followed by the end chunk (plus a usage chunk when `stream_options.include_usage` is set) and `[DONE]` |
| `GET /v1/cuccut/info` | Interface version, endpoints, route switches, levels and limits |

Every response carries an `X-CucCut-Interface-Version` header (first version `1`). Responses to generation requests also carry `X-CucCut-Job-Id` (in JSON responses, it is at `cuccut.jobId`). Errors use OpenAI's error body `{ error: { message, type, code } }`, where `code` is a closed set of error codes (command and protocol spec §11.3). 401: missing or wrong token. 403: `Origin` present, `Host` not loopback, a generation request at `read` level, an approval denied or timed out, and egress without authorization or with revoked authorization (`GRANT_REQUIRED`, `GRANT_REVOKED`). 409: the authorized budget is used up or cannot be guaranteed (`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_UNVERIFIABLE`). 404: unknown model. 413: exceeds the upload or request body limit. 429: exceeds the per-client concurrency limit, or provider rate limiting (the `Retry-After` header is passed through as is). 400: invalid or unsupported parameters. 503: this capability has no routable model right now (`CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, with instructions on how to enable it).

Model names are resolved in this order: an alias from the alias table; the canonical form `<providerId>/<modelId>`; a model name from the capability view (when several providers have it, the view's order applies, with the local one first). The alias table belongs to the service configuration. By default it has one entry, `whisper-1` → the local provider's default transcription model pack. `services.modelApi.setAlias` and `removeAlias` change it. Routing: by default this service routes only to `local`. Online providers, LAN nodes and agent providers each have a switch (`routing: { online, nodes, agent }`, all off by default). A category that is switched off does not exist for this service: it is absent from `/v1/models`, a request by name returns 404, and if a capability has no routable model at all, the answer is 503. The switches only decide whether this service may use them. Online providers still require that the user has already enabled them (§6.4). Levels: `read` allows only `GET` endpoints. `ask` creates one service approval per generation request (the tool name is the endpoint, and the summary contains only the model, character count and size, not the body). `auto` executes directly. Video scope has no meaning for this service and is ignored. Each client has a cap on its in-flight generation requests (default 4, configurable), and requests over the cap are answered immediately with 429. If the caller disconnects before receiving the result, its Job is cancelled. Stopping the service does not count as a disconnect.

**The web service** provides the same client protocol as the desktop UI. The browser gets a scope-limited connection whose capability set is given by the service configuration. It excludes settings, credentials and the management of external services. It is within the product scope (product design §1.4). The first-version trade-offs are described in §14.

- **One port, three things.** The service listens on `127.0.0.1`, default port 47622. It serves the static files of the web client (the build output of `apps/web`), the gateway entry `/ws`, the media channel `/media/…`, and the slide editor routes `/slides/<grant>/…` and `/_blob/<id>` (shared with the gateway, requiring a session. In read-only mode the editor is read-only and can only be embedded in this service's own pages. Slide design §6.2). The design canvas editor routes `/design/<grant>/…` are different: canvas scripts are arbitrary JS, so they accept only a grant and no session. The web client places them on a separate loopback hostname (`127.0.0.1` and `localhost` swapped), where there is no session cookie. Cross-site requests allow only GET navigation. Writes must be same-origin, and under read-only configuration, publishing and uploads get 403. The gateway entry uses the same protocol and handlers as the desktop UI. The only difference is that connections are authenticated by session at upgrade, and methods and topics are filtered through an allowlist. Media uses this service's own handle table, with same-origin relative paths, and fetching content also requires a session. The browser cannot obtain the desktop gateway's port or token. The build output is looked up in this order: `CUCCUT_WEB_DIST`, then the packaged `<resources>/web`, then `apps/web/dist` in the repository. If none is found, enabling the service puts it in `error` with an explanation, without affecting the Runtime.
- **Login.** `services.web.createAccessLink` issues a one-time code, placed in the link's fragment (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#code=…`. The fragment is not sent to the server and does not enter access logs or the Referer). `createAccessLink { video }` (a videoId) makes the link open directly into that video's editor. The Runtime resolves the videoId into the file target the UI uses to open it (an opened video, or a location registered in a Space: the project or session working directory plus a relative path). The link's path and query are the in-UI link `/home?video=<JSON of the FileTarget>` (`webVideoHref` in `@cuccut/protocol`. The UI's router and it share one constructor). The fragment still carries only the code. If nothing is found, the result is `not-found` (`VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`). When not logged in, the page shows only a login page (at the root path and for in-UI links. In-UI links are paths whose last segment has no extension and that are not under `/media/`, `/_auth/` or `/assets/`. After login, the client page is served the same way). Its script takes out the code, immediately strips it from the address bar, exchanges it for a session cookie with `POST /_auth/session`, and then returns to the original path and query. On client startup, it goes to the matching UI according to that path, and then changes the address bar back to the root path. The login page also has an input box, and pasting the code (or the whole link) logs you in too. `cuccut web open [--video <videoId>]` prints a link. `--launch` does not hand the link with the code to the browser (opening a browser goes through the process arguments of `open`, `xdg-open` or `cmd`, which other users and programs on the machine can see in the process list). Instead it opens the login page without the code (keeping the path and query), and the code is typed only in the terminal for the user to paste in. The code is valid for two minutes, and the first redemption (successful or not) voids it. The Runtime stores only hashes of the code and session token. A session is valid for 12 hours, lives only in memory, and all sessions are void when the service stops. `services.web.listSessions` lists them, and `services.web.revokeSession` revokes one and immediately disconnects its connection.
- **Why a cookie.** `<video>` and `<img>` cannot send request headers. Only cookies let media requests and WebSockets authenticate with the same session, invalidate together on revocation, and keep the token out of URLs. The cookie is `HttpOnly` (page scripts cannot read it), `SameSite=Strict` (requests initiated from other sites do not carry it), and `Path=/`. Its name includes the port (`cuccut_web_<port>`).
- **The capability set** is a method allowlist (method names or `<namespace>.*`). It is checked before gateway dispatch, and any method outside the list is answered with `forbidden` (`WEB_METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`). The default set is the desktop UI protocol minus: `settings.set`; the model service configuration and credentials, `models.configure`, `models.removeProvider`, `models.enable`, `models.setDefault` and so on, including the write methods for API provider accounts (`models.addAccount`, `models.updateAccount`, `models.removeAccount`, `models.arrangeAccounts`) and balance checks (`models.checkBalance`, which sends requests to the API provider with the account's key); `services.*` (including `services.mcp.*`, `services.modelApi.*` and issuing access links); `nodes.pair`, `nodes.remove`, `nodes.share.*`; installation management of local model packs (`models.install`, `models.repair`, `models.cancelInstall`, `models.remove`, `models.test`, §6.3. Downloading several hundred MB from the network and removing this machine's transcription capability are administration of this machine, so the browser only sees status and progress); adding, importing and removing Agent skills (`skills.add`, `skills.importGithub`, `skills.remove`, §12.9. Reading local folders or fetching content from the network writes guidance into this machine). Queries, sessions, editing, transcription and generation (including `models.generateText`), model usage (`models.usage`, read-only, also in the read-only set; §6.10), tasks and exports (`exports.*`), the tool catalog and candidate inputs (`tools.list`, `tools.candidates`, also in the read-only set), the creation template catalog (`templates.*`, also in the read-only set. Template package spec §6. Handles are issued only for covers, previews and assets listed in the manifest), and viewing and toggling Agent skills (`skills.list`, `skills.get`, `skills.readFile`, `skills.setEnabled`, the first three also in the read-only set; §3.8) are available. Catalog tools whose execution method is not in the allowlist are marked unavailable to the browser, and errors from external tools expose only the error code and the tool name. The allowlist is written out item by item or by namespace, and any method whose name is not in it cannot be called from the browser. Fixed flows (`pipelines.*`, §7.9) are not exposed: file transcoding takes arbitrary absolute paths for its input and output directories, not constrained by the project directory. To expose it, it would first need the same path constraint as `exports.create`. Importing from a link is also excluded. Managed external tools (`externalTools.*`, §12.9) are not exposed at all, not even `list`. Downloading and running third-party programs is administration of this machine, and its state includes local paths. Topics are filtered by the corresponding read method (the `services` topic requires `services.list`, so it is not subscribable by default). A refusal returns `WEB_TOPIC_NOT_ALLOWED`. The service configuration can narrow the default set (`methods`), or set it to read-only (`readOnly`), which leaves only an explicit list of query methods such as `videos.assetStatus`, `exports.get` and `exports.list`. Writes answer `WEB_READ_ONLY`. Configuration cannot add methods outside the default set. When the configuration changes, existing browser connections are disconnected and reconnect under the new allowlist.
- **No file access outside the project directory.** `projects.open` accepts only registered projects (`PROJECT_NOT_REGISTERED`). New projects can be created only under the default project directory, via `projects.create`. The paths in `importAsset` / `relinkAsset` in `edits.apply` must, by real path, lie within the video's project directory (or the working directory of a session without a project) (`PATH_OUTSIDE_PROJECT`). The target directory for `exports.create` likewise must, by real path, lie within that directory. It cannot be the video directory (containing `video.db`) or `.ccut`. When not given, it defaults to the project's `exports/`. A symlink that points elsewhere is also refused. The browser does not expose local file paths. Choosing a directory, importing media by local path and "show in folder" are unavailable. Session attachments allow choosing or dragging in files. They are registered via `attachments.prepare`, and the web service replaces the Runtime upload address with a single-use same-origin address bound to the logged-in session. A PUT must pass Origin, cookie and session ownership checks as well as the current method and read-only configuration checks. Byte size and MIME are still checked by the attachment store. The per-file limit is 20 MiB, up to 8 per message, and the address expires after ten minutes. Image attachments are sent according to the driver's image capability. Ordinary attachments pass only their safe storage path to the agent as file context, and do not widen its permissions. Attachment previews still check the owning session. The browser cannot obtain the desktop upload port or token.
- Write calls are recorded in the service's recent requests (method, target video and result, but not the parameter body).

Security requirements: see §12.8.

---

## 5. Storage and Assets

### 5.1 Storage Layers

| Storage | Writer | Contents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| VideoStore | Rust VideoEngine, single writer | Video entities, document versions, transactions, receipts, undo, outbox, references to assets and code bundles, dependency edges |
| Runtime Store | Node Runtime | Sessions, Task / Run, approvals, authorizations, video memory, Space user marks, project registry, preferences (§5.10), external service configuration (§4.8) |
| User library | Node Runtime | User data reused across videos: glossaries, voices, brand libraries (§5.9) |
| Job Ledger | Node JobManager | Jobs, external task IDs, budget reservations, Applications |
| Artifact / Blob Store | Publish flow (staging → validate → atomic publish) | Content-addressed, immutable bytes |
| Derived cache | Each executor | Proxies, thumbnails, waveforms, frame cache, Space index; all deletable and rebuildable |

VideoStore uses SQLite, and its tables include at least `videos`, `entities`, `document_versions`, `transactions`, `receipts`, `asset_versions`, `bundle_versions` and `dependency_edges`. The Job Ledger manages its job tables separately. Neither may become a separate writer for the same video.

Proposed disk layout (pending review, see §14):

```text
<runtime-home>/
  instance.json / instance.lock     # Supervisor discovery info and instance lock
  store/                            # Runtime Store and Job Ledger: one store, one file. Small stores that are read whole into memory (project registry, Space marks, settings, authorizations, etc.) are JSON rewritten atomically as a whole file; stores that account per record and change only a few entries are append-only JSONL plus compaction: store/conversations/<id>.jsonl (conversations, §3.10), store/jobs.jsonl and store/applications.jsonl (task and application ledgers, §7.3). After importing an old format, the old file is left as *.migrated; unrecognized files are renamed *.corrupt-<time> and kept
  artifacts/                        # Content-addressed Artifact / Blob Store: <sha256>.<extension>; a background sweep removes unreferenced files (§7.3)
  staging/                          # Temporary writes before publish
  cache/                            # Derived cache, can be deleted as a whole; total size is capped (setting cache.maxSizeMiB, default 2 GiB); when over the limit, the oldest by modification time are deleted down to 90%; content-index/ and cuccut-composition-host/ are never deleted
  workspaces/<bundleId>/            # Code authoring workspace (drafts)
  models/                           # Local models
  store/                            # Model service configuration and credentials (§6.8), node and shared state (§6.7), preferences (§5.10), external service configuration (§4.8), registry of external tools (external-tools.json, §12.9), raw links imported from links (link-sources.json, 0600, §7.9), online call usage ledger (usage.jsonl, 0600, §6.10)
  library/                          # User library (§5.9)
  tools/                            # Managed external tools: <tool>/<version>/ and in-progress downloads in .staging/ (§12.9)
  fonts/                            # Fonts downloaded on demand: files/<family>/, index.json and in-progress downloads in .staging/ (§9.1)
  outputs/                          # Generated results and link downloads that belong to no project (§7.9)
  scratch/                          # Where older versions put working directories for projectless sessions; moved to the session directory at startup (the app data directory the system gives the app, §3.10); when Home is not the default location, this is the session directory
  logs/

<project-dir>/                      # Project: one directory. Defaults to <runtime-home>/projects/<projectId>/
  .ccut/                            # Project marker directory
    project.json                    # Project identity: format, schemaVersion, projectId, createdAt
  <video-dir>/                      # Video: one subdirectory of the project, one per video
    video.db                        # VideoStore
    video.lock                      # Video-level OS lock
    blobs/                          # Bytes taken in: assets the user chose to copy, and generated results with no original file
  .ccut-trash/<trashId>/<video-dir>/  # Deleted videos: the whole directory is moved here as is, and moved back on restore (§5.7)
  …                                 # Other files in the project: original assets, scripts, exported renders, etc.
```

**Assets stay where they are by default.** Video files are generally very large, so import by default only registers a location (`linked`) and does not copy bytes. The user copies bytes into `blobs/` (`managed`, video format spec §4.2) only by choosing “copy into the video” at import or later. Content with no original file to point to (model-generated results, recorded and pasted content, code bundles) is taken in directly. Files inside the project directory are linked by path relative to the video directory, so they remain valid after the whole project is moved or renamed. Files outside the project are linked by absolute path (video format spec §4.2). Therefore a video directory is **not** self-contained: if you copy a single video directory elsewhere, linked assets must be relinked at the new location. To carry all content, use “Collect assets” or export a portable package (§5.8). When one file is linked by several videos, there is only one copy of the bytes; no separate deduplication mechanism is needed.

**The project identity lives in the directory.** `.ccut/project.json` stores the project's identity: `{ "format": "cuccut.project", "schemaVersion": 1, "projectId", "createdAt" }` (RFC 3339), written atomically. The Runtime Store registry is only an index of “identity → last seen path”, plus display name, pinning and archiving, and it can be rebuilt from the markers. The marker directory holds only CucCut's own project-level state, never user content; at present it contains only this one file. Space does not list it and does not descend into it.

- **Markers are written only when creating and opening projects** (`projects.create`, `projects.open`), always before the registry entry. Space scans, sessions and tasks do not write them; loading the registry at startup does not write them either, and projects in the registry are listed as usual. If a marker must be written and the directory is not writable (read-only volume, no permission), opening fails (`PROJECT_DIR_READ_ONLY`), and a project without a marker is not registered.
- **Order of decisions on open**:
  1. Marker present and the registry contains this identity: the registered path is the current directory, and it is the same project. If the registered path no longer exists, or the marker there is no longer this identity, the directory was moved or renamed: update the registered path, keep the identity, sessions remain attached and the working directory follows. If the display name was originally the old directory name, it becomes the new directory name; names the user changed are kept. Project-internal assets linked by relative path in the video remain valid; absolute-path links to assets made from the old location are not migrated or guessed, and are reported as missing for the user or agent to relink (video format spec §4.2). If the registered path still exists and the marker there still has this identity: the current directory is a copy, and the Runtime writes a new `projectId` into it and registers it as a new project. The original project and its sessions and Space user marks are unaffected, and the copy does not inherit them.
  2. Marker present and the registry does not contain this identity (registry entry deleted, or a different machine): register using the identity and creation time from the marker. The marker takes precedence: if another older registry entry exists at the same path, it and its sessions are kept and not merged.
  3. No marker: if the registry already has this path (a project registered before the upgrade), keep the identity from the registry; otherwise it is a new project. Write the marker. So deleting the marker and opening from the same path yields the original identity; only a location not in the registry produces a new project.
- **Unrecognized markers** (not JSON, wrong `format`, missing fields) are first renamed and kept as `project.json.corrupt-<time>`, then handled as no marker. They are never silently overwritten. If `schemaVersion` is higher than the known version, opening is refused (`PROJECT_MARKER_UNSUPPORTED`) and the marker is not rewritten.
- **Videos follow the project's identity.** A video's identity lives in its own `video.db`, with no separate marker; videos under a project are derived by scanning for `video.db` in subdirectories. The metadata in `video.db` records the `projectId` it belongs to (this is not revision content: writing it creates no new version and does not enter undo history; video format spec §1.3). When the Runtime opens or creates a video in a project, it hands the project's identity to the VideoEngine: if the library has no owning project (videos from before the upgrade), it adopts that identity and `videoId` is unchanged; if they match, nothing is done; if they differ, the video is a copy or was moved into another project, so the VideoEngine assigns a new `videoId`, appends the old one to `previousVideoIds` (provenance), records the new `projectId`, and content, revision number and history are unchanged. This step completes in one database transaction, so after a crash it is either fully done or not done at all. Videos that belong to no project (in the session working directory) are not claimed.
- **Preprocessing a project's videos on open.** After `projects.open`, the Runtime scans the videos in the project directory in the background and hands each to the VideoEngine for claiming: each video takes the write lock only briefly, once; a video currently being opened is skipped (it was already claimed at open); a video locked elsewhere is left until next time, and opening the video claims it again. Only videos in project directories already in the registry are processed. `videoId` enters the Runtime only through the VideoEngine opening a video, so the same `videoId` never points to two directories at once; if this happens anyway (moved during opening, or indistinguishable copies), the one opened first is kept and the event is logged.
- **Limitation**: when two legacy videos that have no recorded owning project are copies of each other (for example, a project copied before the upgrade), they cannot be told apart: each adopts the project where it sits and keeps the same `videoId`; if both are opened at the same time, only the one opened first is kept, per the previous point.

Invariants: the authoritative state of a video exists only in its `video.db` and the asset versions it references; other files in the project directory are not authoritative video data; no data in the Runtime Home can change video versions independently; after the cache directory is deleted as a whole, the video can still be opened, previewed and exported; if a linked asset is not found, the video still opens and the asset is marked missing, reported by `videos.assetStatus` (commands and protocol spec §4.1).

### 5.2 Writes, Publishing and Crash Consistency

- The video entity, versions, idempotent results, receipts, undo records and outbox are committed by the same Rust database transaction.
- A receipt returned to the user may come only from a commit that succeeded after persistence. On write failure, the old version is kept; insufficient disk space must never return “saved”.
- Time-consuming analysis, downloads and media computation are done outside the transaction; inputs are re-validated at commit time.
- New bytes are first written to staging, and after the digest and format are verified they are atomically published to the immutable Blob Store; only then is the reference added in a database transaction.
- A crash may leave unreferenced blobs, which are cleaned up by a GC with a grace period. GC must account for leases of tasks being built, exports being frozen, historical references and the grace period, and must not immediately delete a just-published product that has not yet entered a transaction.
- Video-level blob GC lives in the engine (`Video::gc_blobs`, internal command `videos.gcBlobs { path }`): it runs only while holding the write lock, and deletes entries in `blobs/` that are not mentioned by the reference set in §5.5; leftover entries in `blobs/.staging/` whose last modification time is older than the grace period (1 hour) are deleted too. If reading the reference set fails, nothing is deleted; an entry that cannot be deleted is recorded only in the receipt. The Runtime decides when to call it: on a normal close of the video (when the last opener has left or it is idle) and with no frozen task lease, it runs once before releasing the write lock; videos that were deleted or whose engine errored do not run it. Export and portable package import are done outside of closing and do not interleave with it.
- Runtime Stores that are whole-file JSON (project registry, Space marks and artifact records, settings, preferences, etc., `store-file.ts`) follow the same rules on read: if the file is not JSON, its top level is not an object, or its structure is unrecognized, it is renamed and kept as `<file>.corrupt-<time>`, logged, and starts from defaults; an empty file is treated as absent; if the version field is higher than known, it is treated as empty but not renamed, and the file is not written afterward (changes stay in memory only), so a downgraded runtime does not overwrite data of a newer version; if the file system cannot be read, startup fails, except for preferences and cache-like stores (treated as empty and read-only). Exceptions that must never be silently lost: if the authorization ledger or external service configuration cannot be read, startup fails; if model credentials cannot be read, every operation reports `unavailable` and the file is left alone; if node sharing state cannot be read, sharing is treated as off and cannot be changed.
- A confirmed video must never point to bytes that are not yet published.

### 5.3 Asset Revisions and Import Information

Each `AssetRevision` must record: the content digest, media type, duration and timebase, for video the display size / rotation / pixel aspect ratio, for audio the sample rate and channels, known color information, and the original or generated source. Variable-frame-rate indexes, proxies, thumbnails, waveforms, and reverse conforms are derived artifacts.

Asset bytes stay in place by default, with only their location recorded (`linked`); the user may also choose to bring them into the video directory (`managed`). A code package is a directory asset. The storage location is not part of a revision's identity: bringing a linked asset in, or giving a moved file a new location, does not create a new revision (video format spec §4.2, §4.3).

A change in source path and a change in source content are two different events:

- Relinking to the same bytes does not change the semantic version.
- New bytes appearing at the same path must produce a new revision and affect the selected instances through an explicit `replaceAssetVersion`.
- Silently replacing an asset in a frozen export with "the file's latest content" is forbidden.

For the record format, see video format spec §4.

### 5.4 Provenance, Generation, and Cost Facts

Generated media records `jobId`, `provider`, `model`, `modelVersion`, `inputHashes`, `outputHash`, the seed when available, and the actual cost and billing status. When the cost is unknown, store `unknown`; do not fabricate an actual payment from an estimate.

Uploaded media records its known provenance. Authorization, licensing, and voice-use confirmations may be linked to the asset, but the system must not assume that "uploading grants every right to third-party material."

External URLs must be resolved into frozen assets before publishing or exporting. An expired signed address cannot serve as a permanent source.

### 5.5 Reference Graph and Resource Cleanup

- Deleting an instance on the timeline does not delete the original asset.
- Deleting an asset must check all sequences, the undo history, frozen jobs, and the published code packages that reference it.
- GC follows the reference graph and the retention policy, not "not shown recently." The reference set for the video directory's `blobs/` is every content digest mentioned anywhere in the library: every version of an asset (not only the current one, since undo can bring a deleted asset back), code package versions, current entities, and digests in the bodies of undo records. It is not filtered by storage mode (version records of linked assets collected by `collectAssets` still say `linked`). Digests counted in excess have no matching entry in `blobs/`. Entries in `blobs/` whose names are not `<sha256>.<ext>` or `<sha256>/` are not managed by the video, and GC leaves them alone. Version records and undo records are never deleted, so this GC only reclaims orphans left by crashes and expired staging. The bytes of a deleted asset are kept alongside its version record (§14).
- Deletion in Space (§5.7) uses the same reference graph.
- Deleting a video does not delete the user's original files. The bytes of linked assets live outside the video directory, and neither deletion nor physical deletion touches them. Physical deletion removes only files the video manages itself (the video database with its logs and locks, and `blobs/`). If the video directory contains files the video does not manage, those files are not deleted and are left to the user.

### 5.6 ResourceResolver, Temporary Grants, and Byte Freezing

A video stores only stable version references to Assets, Bundles, and Fonts. At runtime, the resolver provides controlled handles or short-lived addresses:

```ts
interface ResourceResolver {
  resolve(
    ref: VersionRef,
    purpose: 'interactive-preview' | 'inspection' | 'export' | 'authoring',
    principal: TrustedPrincipal,
  ): Promise<{
    handle: ResourceHandle;         // controlled handle or short-lived URL
    availability: 'ready' | 'missing' | 'denied' | 'expired';
    contentHash: string;
    scope: GrantScope;
    expiresAt?: string;
  }>;
}
```

- Blob URLs, signed addresses, and browser objects are not written into `VideoSnapshot`. The local path of a linked asset is recorded in the asset revision's `storage.locator`; for a video created new from local media, the asset's source also records the original file's path (§7.9). Both are left out when the video is packed into a portable bundle (video format spec §4.2, §4.5, §8).
- A linked media asset is allowed only for the single file the video registered, not for other things in the same directory. A linked asset that is not media does not go through the media channel.
- Querying media metadata is separate from fetching the actual bytes. The timeline loads only visible resources.
- An expired address may be refreshed within a valid grant. When a grant is denied, show a permissions problem; never substitute a file with the same or a similar name.
- A handle is scoped to the video, the asset revision, and the consumer. A URL cannot be used to traverse other videos.
- Path resolution must guard against escape, against symbolic-link changes, and against time-of-check-to-time-of-use races. Logs must not expose sensitive local paths or credentials.

**Freezing semantics.** File size and inode can serve as cheap hints that something changed, but an in-place write of the same size does not change them, so freezing cannot rest on that check:

1. First obtain managed bytes that are guaranteed not to change (a verified copy, copy-on-write, or a controlled object store);
2. verify the hash;
3. publish an immutable `AssetRevision`.

Opening a file descriptor does not make the file's content immutable. A frozen job references only verified managed revisions. Linked assets are the default case. When freezing, create a job snapshot for them: if the volume holding the file supports copy-on-write, use a clone, which takes no extra space; if it does not, check the content digest once when the job starts and once when it ends, and if they differ, fail the job with an explanation, without copying the whole file. Checking the mtime after the run finishes cannot be used to hide the risk that new bytes were mixed in midway.

Whether a running job holds its own independent grant, and whether revoking a grant cancels it, must be stated explicitly in the Grant contract (§12.5). A permission revocation must not be bypassed by an automatically refreshed address.

### 5.7 Space Directory

The Space directory (`SpaceCatalog`) is a derived index in the shared storage layer that unifies videos and user-visible artifacts into one queryable list. It has no authoritative data of its own.

```ts
interface SpaceEntry {
  id: Id;                                  // video entries use videoId; artifact entries use artifactId. In kind, 'video' is an editable video, 'video-file' is a video asset
  kind: 'video' | 'export' | 'video-file' | 'image' | 'audio'
      | 'subtitle' | 'document' | 'slides' | 'design' | 'package' | 'template';
  name: string;
  ref: { videoId: Id } | { artifactRef: VersionRef };
  origin: {
    source: 'generated' | 'imported' | 'exported' | 'edited' | 'derived';  // derived: child video created by videos.derive
    projectId?: Id;                        // owning project; empty when it belongs to no project
    videoId?: Id;
    videoRevision?: Revision;            // frozen revision the artifact corresponds to
    conversationId?: Id;
    taskId?: Id;
    jobId?: Id;
    provider?: { providerId: string; modelId: string };
    cost?: { state: 'estimate' | 'reported' | 'unknown' | 'free-local';
             amount?: string; currency?: string };
    derivedFrom?: VersionRef;              // source entry of a secondary edit
    derivedFromVideo?: { videoId: Id; range?: TimeRange };  // video entry: which video it was derived from (a cut-out clip, a copy)
  };
  status: 'generating' | 'candidate' | 'applied' | 'published'
        | 'source-changed' | 'missing' | 'failed';
  media?: { duration?: MediaTime; width?: number; height?: number };
  deck?: { slideCount: number };           // page count of a slides entry (a directory containing a valid deck.json); name is the deck's title
  canvas?: { boardCount: number };         // board count of a design entry (a directory containing a valid canvas.json); name is the design's title
  previewRef?: Id;
  lastActivityAt: string;
  createdAt?: string | null;              // file / video database birthtime; for generated results use completion time, for placeholders use task creation time
  updatedAt?: string | null;              // file / video database (including WAL) mtime; for placeholders use task update time
  user: { favorite: boolean; displayName?: string; trashedAt?: string };
}
```

Creation and update times are derived by the Runtime, and each client displays them sorted in descending order by the chosen time. When the creation time is unavailable, return null; do not substitute the file's status-change time (ctime) or modification time. An older Runtime may omit both fields, and clients put entries with unknown times at the end. For results outside a source directory, use the task completion time when the file is unavailable; for a missing imported file, keep the registration time as the creation time and leave the update time unknown; trashed video records do not yet store these two times, so both return null.

**Data sources**

| Entry field | Comes from |
| --- | --- |
| Video entry, video name, current revision, cover | VideoStore (queried through VideoService and video events) |
| Artifact entry, bytes, media info | Artifact Store |
| `generating` placeholder, source task, cost state | Job Ledger |
| `candidate` / `applied` | Application records |
| `published` | Publish receipt of the export job |
| `user.*` | Runtime Store |

**Rules**

- **Visibility**. Artifacts are published with a purpose tag. Only deliverable artifacts (final cuts, generated images / audio / video, subtitle files, documents, portable bundles, templates) enter the directory. Proxies, thumbnails, waveforms, frame caches, and inspection screenshots do not.
- **Status is derived**. The directory stores no independent status field, only cached derived results. `source-changed` is determined when an artifact's recorded input fingerprint differs from the fingerprint of the video's current corresponding input. `missing` is determined when `ResourceResolver` reports that the bytes or an external dependency are unavailable.
- **Rebuildable**. After the index is deleted, it is rebuilt from VideoStore, Artifact Store, Job Ledger, and Runtime Store; the result must be identical, and `user.*` is not lost in a rebuild.
- **Updates**. The directory subscribes to video events, job events, and artifact publish events and updates incrementally. It provides its own sequence numbers for client subscription and deduplication.
- **Secondary edits do not overwrite**. A change to an artifact is published as a new Artifact, with `origin.derivedFrom` pointing to the original entry. A change to a video is an ordinary transaction.
- **From a final cut back to the video**. `space.openForEdit` returns the source video, the cut's frozen `videoRevision`, and the video's current revision. When the two differ, the client shows the difference. When there is no source video, it returns the option "create a new video from this asset" without fabricating layers.
- **Deletion**. Moving to the trash only writes `user.trashedAt`. Physical deletion follows the §5.5 reference graph: unique bytes still referenced by a video, its history, a frozen job, or a template are not deleted, and the list of blocking references is returned. Deleting a video is an explicit VideoService command, not a side effect of a directory operation.
- **Permissions**. Directory queries are filtered by principal. The directory offers no way to obtain bytes that bypasses `ResourceResolver`.
- **Project ownership**. An entry's `projectId` comes from the directory it lives in: a video takes it from its project, and an artifact takes it from the project of the video or session that produced it. Entries that belong to no project (results of standalone tools and external-service generations, §7.9) have an empty `projectId`, and their bytes are in the Runtime Home's `outputs/`.
- **Non-video files in a project**. For assets imported directly into a project, and documents written out by a session, the authority for the bytes is the file itself in the project directory. The directory indexes only files the Runtime has registered (via `space.import` or published by a job), and does not scan the whole project directory. When a file is moved or deleted externally, its entry shows as `missing`.
- **Generated artifacts**. Each output of `synthesizeSpeech`, `generateImage`, and `generateMusic` is an Artifact. The entry's `kind` is `audio` / `image` (music is also `audio`), and `origin` takes `source: 'generated'`, `jobId`, and `provider` (from the Job Ledger). Generation parameters (including the original text and prompts) live only in the job record's `generation`. The entry and the asset's source record in a video store only a summary of the parameters and the input hashes, not a copy of the original text. When a video is given, the output is imported as an asset and the entry's status is `candidate`. Without a video, the entry belongs to no video.
- **Failed generations**. A failed generation job leaves a `failed` placeholder in the directory, carrying the reason and a retry entry point. It disappears when the user clears it or a retry succeeds. It has no bytes and cannot be referenced.
- **Derived videos**. A clip or copy cut from a video is an independent video. `derivedFromVideo` only records the source, for filtering and hints. Later changes to the source video do not propagate to it, and deleting the source video does not delete it.

Commands in the `space` namespace: `list`, `get`, `search`, `import`, `rename`, `setFavorite`, `trash`, `restore`, `purge`, `openForEdit`, `continueInConversation`, `rebuildIndex`. `list` can filter by `projectId` (including "belongs to no project"), `kind`, `status`, and source video. `import` registers a file as an asset of a project without putting it in any video. `search`: see §5.11.

**Implementation in this version** (`packages/runtime-core/src/space-catalog.ts` and `space/`; differences from the above are noted in §14):

- **Derivation**. Entries are computed as a whole from all inputs by a pure function (`space/space-derive.ts`), compared with the previous result, and only changed entries are emitted (`entry.upsert` / `entry.removed`). The first scan, rescans, and rebuilds emit one `catalog.replaced`. Incremental updates and rebuilds therefore take the same path and give identical results. The inputs are: scans of source directories (the registered project directories, and the working directories of sessions that do not belong to a project; when a session is bound to a project, its source changes from `conv:<id>` to `project:<id>`, the entry id changes with the source key, and user markers and trash records under the old source key in `space.json` do not carry over); the registrations and user markers from `space.import` (`space.json` in the Runtime Store); the Job Ledger (`JobManager.list()` and its change notifications); and, from the content index (§5.11), each video's videoId, its current revision, the assets used on its timeline, the files that linked assets point to, and the duration and canvas size of the root sequence.
- **Derived child videos**. Child videos created by `videos.derive` (§3.5) are independent video entries. The content index reads the parent video, revision, and range from their derivation source document (the video facts in §5.11). The entry's `origin` is `{ source: 'derived', projectId, videoId: <parent video>, videoRevision: <parent's revision at derive time>, range: { start, end } (the range of the parent taken at derive time, in seconds), capability: 'videos-derive' }`. (In the interface above, this version expresses `derivedFromVideo` as `origin.videoId`.) They are not gathered into the parent video's card and are not in `related` when the parent is deleted. The UI's "Source" column reads "from <parent video name>", and the parent video's viewer lists the short clips cut from it, with each one's range, by `origin.videoId`.
- **Entry `id`**. For scanned files and the video directory, the id is a digest (`sp_…`) of the source key and relative path. Video entries additionally carry `ref: { videoId }` and `media` (the root sequence's duration `durationSec` and the canvas's `width` and `height`, used by the UI's "duration or size" column; once the content index has read them, trashed videos have none). Generated and exported artifacts use the artifactId. `generating` / `failed` placeholders use the jobId. `ref` is `{ videoId }`, `{ artifactId }`, or `{ jobId }`.
- **Source and project**. Entries not in a source directory (artifacts, placeholders, exports published outside a source directory) have an empty `source`. The owning project is recorded in `origin.projectId` (taken from the project of the video that produced it, or of the session that submitted it). `list`'s `projectId` filter matches `source.projectId ?? origin.projectId`.
- **Status**. `generating`: queued and running generations and exports, with progress. `failed`: failed or interrupted, with the reason and error code. It is cleared by `space.purge`, or stops being shown once a task with the same capability and identical input (`inputHash`) later succeeds. `candidate` / `applied`: generated output imported into a video. It is `applied` when any sequence (any timeline) uses that asset. `published`: exported files that were published. A file published into a source directory is merged with the scanned file into one entry (`kind` follows the export settings, and `ref.artifactId` is the export's artifact). A file a session published into its working directory before the session was moved into a project is recognized at its post-move location (§3.10) and is not listed as a separate entry. Exports made for the agent's internal checks (`purpose: 'preview'`, §9.13 "purpose") do not form entries: queued, failed, and completed ones are all left out, and so are the files they publish. (When the same path is later published by a delivered export, it is listed as usual.) `source-changed`: the export's frozen `videoRevision` differs from the video's current revision. `missing`: the artifact file, the published file, or the registered project file no longer exists. Plain files and videos have no status (`null`; filter with `none` in `list`).
- **Deliverable**. Generated images, audio, text, and exports enter the directory. Raw transcription results and frozen export snapshots do not. Proxies, thumbnails, and waveforms were never in the Artifact Store to begin with. The parent tasks and steps of fixed workflows (§7.9) do not enter. Transcoding results published into a source directory appear through scanning.
- **Bytes**. When `media.resolve` is given an `entryId`, the directory locates the file: entries in a source directory are limited to that source directory, and artifacts and exports outside a source directory are limited to that one file. Placeholders and missing entries have no bytes.
- **Slides directory**. A directory in a source directory that contains a valid `deck.json` (`v: 4`, with `order` an array) is a single `slides` entry, and it is not scanned further. The pages and images inside do not become entries of their own (slide design §7.3). `name` takes the deck's title (the directory name when there is no title). `deck.slideCount` is the page count. Size and modification time are the total and the latest of the regular files in the directory and its first-level subdirectories (symbolic links not followed, hidden files skipped), and creation time is taken from `deck.json`. If `deck.json` cannot be read or its version is wrong, the directory is scanned as usual. Project file browsing (`projects.files.list`) likewise marks it `slides` (modification time taken from `deck.json`), and search does not descend into it.
- **Design directory**. A directory that contains a valid `canvas.json` (`v: 3`, with `boards` an object) is a single `design` entry, with the same rules as a slides directory: it is not scanned further, `name` takes the title, and `canvas.boardCount` is the board count. When `canvas.json` is invalid, the directory is treated as an ordinary directory. `projects.files.list` marks it `design` (modification time taken from `canvas.json`), and search does not descend into it. Entering it with `dir` still lists its contents as usual.
- **`space.import`**. A file in the project directory is registered in place. A file outside the project is copied into the project's `imports/` (a sequence number is added on a name clash), and the result returns `copied: true`. Files inside video directories, slides directories, and design directories, files inside hidden and dependency directories, and files of an unrecognized type are rejected (`SPACE_IMPORT_UNSUPPORTED`).
- **`space.openForEdit`**. A video entry returns `video`. One with a source video returns `source-video`, with the frozen revision, the current revision, and `changed`. One without a source video returns `new-video` and a suggested project. It only answers where to go and does not open the video.
- **`space.thumbnail`** (`space/space-thumbnails.ts`). Thumbnails for grid cards and the list's name column. The client fetches them on demand when the entry is visible. It answers `image` (width not over 720, height not over 3 times the width, never enlarged; PNG, GIF, and WebP sources output PNG to keep transparency, others output JPEG), `text` (the UTF-8 text at the start, about 1.5 KB, cut at a character boundary), or `none`. It contains no local paths. If the entry does not exist or is not visible, the answer is `not-found`. The subject rules are the same as for `space.get`, and it stays available in Web read-only mode. How each kind is handled:
  - Video: the cover frame of the working draft (the same rule as the viewer's poster frame, `posterFrame` in `@cuccut/protocol`). The asset revision and time come from the video facts in the content index (§5.11). If the index does not exist yet, let the index read this one first (waiting at most 15 seconds). The asset file is located through the Engine Host's internal read-only query `videos.resolveAsset { path, assetId, revision? }` (not in the gateway's method table). A video that is already open uses the instance that is open. One that is not open is opened read-only, searched, and released right away, without taking the write lock. For an asset copied in, the storage arrangement is an engine-internal convention and is not recomputed in the Runtime. Linked assets are checked against the engine's length and position rules and then admitted under the media channel rules. Videos in the trash, and videos with no picture on the timeline, are `none`.
  - Final cut and video files: the earlier of 1 second and 10% of the duration. If that frame cannot be obtained (the video is shorter), fall back to the first frame. Images use the first frame. Only the demuxer matching the extension is recognized (MP4/MOV, WebM/MKV, AVI; PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF, and BMP are read by their forced format). SVG, HEIC, and AVIF are `none`.
  - Documents and subtitles: for Markdown, plain text, and subtitles (SRT, WebVTT, ASS/SSA), take the text at the start with any BOM removed. Subtitles keep only the dialogue (sequence numbers, timecodes, headers, styles, and tags are removed). UTF-8 and BOM-marked UTF-16 are recognized. Other encodings and binaries are `none`. PDF, Word, and RTF are `none`.
  - Audio, slides, design, packages, templates, and placeholder, missing, or failed entries are `none`.

  Frame extraction goes through media analysis (`media-analysis.ts`): the same demuxer allowlist as the filmstrip, read-only local files, a 20-second time limit, a 1 MB output cap, and 3 concurrency slots. Failures are recorded for 60 seconds. The cache lives in `<home>/cache/media/`. Video covers are keyed by the asset's content digest, time, and width (`<digest>/thumb-<milliseconds>-w<width>.jpg`; on a cache hit the file is not looked up). Files are keyed by a digest of real path, size, modification time, width, and time (`files/<digest>.jpg|png`), and are re-extracted after the file is overwritten. Anything that cannot be read or decoded answers `none` without raising an error.
- **`space.rebuildIndex`**. Rescans all sources and re-queries the files. After the content index's cache is cleared, it is reread in the background. When it returns, the directory has already been rebuilt, and `pendingVideos` is the number of videos still to be reread. User markers and registrations are left unchanged.
- **Permissions**. Principals on the gateway (the UI, CLI, Web) are all the user themselves. Agents in sessions and external services access entries through the tools `space_list` / `space_search` (§3.5, §4.8).
