An agent sees the entries in its session's source directory and the artifacts that belong to the same source. An external service sees only videos within its scope (in registered projects, on the allowlist) and the entries generated or exported by them, while task artifacts and placeholders are visible only to the submitter. Web's `space.import` can only register files in the project directory (checked by real path, `PATH_OUTSIDE_PROJECT`).
- **CLI**. `cuccut space [list]` lists entries by kind, status, source video, favorite, and trash. `cuccut space search` searches across videos (hits carry the video, time, document type, and speaker; the output notes when the index is incomplete). Also: `rescan`, `rebuild`, `trash`, `restore`, and `purge` (when there are references, it lists them and exits with a non-zero status), `delete-video` deletes a video, and `continue [--conversation <id>]` continues a session from an entry. Renaming, favoriting, importing, and returning to a video for editing are UI-only in this version.
- **Deleting a video** (`videos.delete`; entry points also include `space.trash` / `space.update { trashed: true }` called on a video entry). The entire video directory is renamed on the same volume into the source directory's trash, `.ccut-trash/<trashId>/<directory name>`, without copying or deleting. Across volumes it is refused with `VIDEO_TRASH_CROSS_DEVICE`. If the video directory is a source directory itself (the user opened the video directory as a project), or contains one (a session's directory), the rename would move that project's or session's directory. In that case, deletion is refused with `VIDEO_TRASH_SOURCE_ROOT` before the video is closed, the record is written, or the directory is moved. During the move the video's write lock is held; if another process holds a lock, the engine returns `VIDEO_LOCKED`. If another connection has the video open, `VIDEO_IN_USE`. If the Runtime itself is still using it (tasks or export leases), or there are queued or running jobs, `VIDEO_BUSY` (with `jobIds`). Only when the caller itself has it open is it closed first, and subscribers receive `video.closed` (`reason: 'deleted'`). The deletion is first recorded in `space.json` (`trashedVideos`: the original entry id and relative path, the location in the trash, videoId, and name), and then the directory is moved. At startup the record is reconciled against where the directory actually is, so a crash midway leaves no mismatched record. A video in the trash is a video entry whose `id` is determined by its source and its position in the trash (the original position may be taken by another video). User markers carry over, and the `id` from before deletion can still be used for undo and restore. The trash directory begins with a dot and is not scanned as a video. `videos.open` cannot open a path in the trash (`VIDEO_TRASHED`). Entries exported or generated from the video stay where they are, and the result's `related` lists them.
- **Restoring a video** (`videos.restore`, or `space.restore` / `space.update { trashed: false }` on a deleted video). The directory is moved back to its original relative path. If something is already there, a sequence number is added (`renamed: true`). Restoration is impossible if the project or session no longer exists.
- **`space.purge`**. A video entry in a source directory is refused with `invalid-request` (`SPACE_PURGE_VIDEO`; delete the video first). An in-progress placeholder is refused with `conflict` (`SPACE_PURGE_RUNNING`; run `jobs.cancel` first). Failed placeholders are cleared directly. Anything else only deletes what is in the trash (otherwise `SPACE_NOT_TRASHED`), and slides and design entries are deleted together with their directories. Before deletion, references are checked: video assets that link to this file (`video-asset`), and in-progress jobs that use this artifact (`job`). If a video currently cannot be read, or the index has not caught up, the reference cannot be confirmed and is treated as referenced (`unverified`). For a deleted video, additionally check: other videos that link to files in its directory (`video-asset`); its queued, running, and awaiting-reconciliation jobs (`needs-reconciliation`, where the result may still have to be written into it) (`job`); and files in its directory that the video does not manage (`user-file`). If none exist, only the files the video manages are deleted, and then the empty directory itself (§5.5). When there are references, nothing is deleted and `{ status: 'blocked', references }` is returned. Deleted artifacts and exports leave a purge marker, and afterwards do not reappear as `missing`.
- **Trash retention period**. Preference `space.trashRetentionDays` (1–3650, default 30). It runs once after the first Space scan, then every 6 hours, with the day count taken from the setting at that moment. Entries that have been in the trash longer than the retention period go one by one through the same path as `space.purge`. Those with references are kept, logged, and retried next time. Videos in source directories that only have a trash marker written (records from before this version) are not included.
- **Artifact records** (`store/space-artifacts.json` in the Runtime Store). The Job Ledger keeps only the most recent several jobs (§7.5). After a pruned job's artifact bytes are still in the Artifact Store, but its entry would disappear. Space keeps a separate record of the tasks that produced artifacts (generation, export, and file-to-file workflows such as file transcoding, translation of subtitle files, and downloads from links, recorded only by workflow name; files handed off by `downloads_save` are also recorded here under the workflow `downloads-save`, §3.5), with the facts needed for derivation (`SpaceJobFacts`: kind, status, videoId, Provider and model, input hash, submitter, times, error, output list, the summary of export settings and the published file name, and the last applied status). It is written when a task ends with a result, and also backfilled from the Ledger at startup. It is merged with the Ledger during derivation, and anything the Ledger still has takes precedence. Generation parameters (original text, prompts), warnings, grants, and cancellation details are not in it. A record is removed after all of its artifacts have been physically deleted or purged (and if the Ledger no longer has the task either, its purge marker is removed too). It is not a cache, and `space.rebuildIndex` does not delete it. Unreadable lines are skipped. If the whole file cannot be read, it is renamed and kept, and the store starts from empty. The same file separately stores **references kept only for sweep** (`references`: when a task ends, the IDs of all artifacts in its result, in its workflow step outputs, and in its applied artifacts are recorded. Tasks whose output does not enter the directory, such as dubbing, translation, speaker diarization, and transcription written into the video, are also recorded) and the sweep starting point `sweepSince` (the moment from which references begin to be kept; for an old file read in, it is filled in and saved, not moved later). These references do not take part in derivation, are not shown as entries, and are not currently released.
- **`space.continueInConversation`**. Which session to put it in: when `conversationId` is given, use it, and it must be visible to this entry (the same rule as the agent's `space_list`), otherwise `SPACE_CONVERSATION_MISMATCH`. When it is not given: an entry belonging to a project creates a new session in that project (sessions within a project each have their own topic, so it does not guess which one to pick up, §3.10). An entry not in a project returns to the session where it is or which produced it, and if that session no longer exists, a new session not belonging to a project is created. Only the entry's reference is carried (`SpaceEntryReference`: `entryId`, kind, name, project, relative path in the source directory, videoId, artifactId, status, the capability that produced it, jobId, session, and frozen revision). It does not read file contents and does not carry generation parameters. The reference is recorded in the session's `pendingReferences` (one per entry at most, up to 20), without starting any task. On the user's next `conversations.send`, it is attached after the text sent to the agent (in a `<cuccut-space-references>` section, metadata only; for content, the agent fetches it by id through a tool), recorded on that user message's `references`, and then cleared. `conversations.update { pendingReferences: null }` removes them. A trashed entry is refused (`SPACE_ENTRY_TRASHED`). When retried with `commandId`, it answers the same session.
- **Entry points**. The desktop connection and the CLI can both delete and restore videos and continue sessions from entries. Web is in the default set (`videos.*`, `space.*`). In read-only configuration these are all writes and are refused (`WEB_READ_ONLY`, including `space.update` / `space.trash`, so they cannot be used to bypass it). The agent in a session has the tool `videos_delete` (risk `high`, asks depending on the access mode; it can only delete videos in the session's source directory, and out-of-scope or nonexistent ones both answer `VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`). There is no restore or physical-delete tool. The MCP external-service tool catalog does not include video deletion.

For UI behaviour, see product design §4.

### 5.8 Portable package

A portable package is a self-contained `.cuccut` file: a snapshot of the video, the body of every document version and the bytes of every asset version, together with each file's length and sha256. CucCut on another machine opens it and gets a new video with the same content. The format is specified in the video format spec §8.

- **Form.** Settled: only a single-file `.cuccut`, an uncompressed POSIX ustar archive (regular files only; paths no longer than 255 bytes; individual files under 8 GiB; no pax extensions). The system `tar` can list and extract it. The folder form is not offered (§14).
- **Freeze.** An export is the `portable` kind of `exports.create`. Like other exports, it is frozen, preflighted, staged and published (§9.11). In one request the engine returns the current snapshot, the raw body stored for each document version, and where each asset version is right now. The engine host processes requests in order, so they all correspond to the same video version. The working database is not copied when it has a WAL with uncheckpointed changes; later edits do not affect the frozen export.
- **Contents.** All assets in the snapshot are converted to embedded (`managed`): linked assets are copied in after being checked against their recorded digest, and embedded blobs are copied as-is. Keys, authorisations, the job ledger, the app ledger, sessions and the user library are not included (a voice's reference recording is included only if it is already a video asset).
- **Local paths.** The package must not contain absolute local paths. The locations of linked assets in the snapshot are replaced. In other fields, any string containing a local root directory (the home directory, the video directory and its parent, the source directory, the directory holding assets, the original-file directories recorded in asset provenance, and their real paths) is replaced with a placeholder, and the warning `PACKAGE_LOCAL_PATH_REMOVED` is recorded. Document bodies are collected verbatim (their digests are computed on the original and cannot change); if these root directories appear in them, preflight rejects each occurrence (`EXPORT_PACKAGE_LOCAL_PATH`). A test opens the exported package and checks it file by file.
- **Preflight.** Checked at submission. Each kind of problem is listed item by item (`details.items`), not just the first one: assets that cannot be read (missing, unreadable, or no longer matching their recorded content; `details.items` of `ASSET_MISSING`); files that cannot be written to ustar (`EXPORT_PACKAGE_UNSUPPORTED`); insufficient space on the target disk (`EXPORT_INSUFFICIENT_SPACE`, computed as the total size of all files plus a 64 MB margin); a given file name that already exists (`EXPORT_DESTINATION_EXISTS`; a default file name that collides gets a sequence number). With `missingAssets: 'skip'`, unreadable assets are not included in the package; the manifest marks them honestly as `missing`; in the snapshot they become linked assets with only a file name, each with a `PACKAGE_ASSET_MISSING` warning. After the package is opened, `videos.assetStatus` lists them as missing, and `relinkAsset` recovers them.
- **Write-out and publish.** The archive is first written to a hidden temporary file in the target directory; the manifest is written last. After writing, the file is read back from the start using the same rules as on input: each file's length and digest are checked, and the manifest is checked against what was written. Only after this passes is it published (same hard-link publish as finished renders, §9.11). If a linked asset changes during copying, the export fails with `ASSET_CHANGED`; if the disk fills up, it fails with `EXPORT_INSUFFICIENT_SPACE`. Cancellation and failure both delete the temporary file, leaving no half-written package. If the process is force-killed, a hidden temporary file may be left in the target directory (`.<output file name>.<8 hex digits>.tmp`). Space does not list entries starting with a dot, so later exports are unaffected, and on next startup it is deleted by the "leftover cleanup" below.
- **Open.** `videos.importPackage` creates the package as a new video in the project directory (for a session with no project, it is created in the session's working directory, §3.10). It checks and extracts the package in a hidden staging directory in the source directory (format and packaging version, each file's length and sha256, no path escaping the package, and entry kinds limited to regular files); then the engine builds the new video. The new video gets a new `videoId`; entity IDs and revision numbers are kept from the package. The engine does not trust the manifest: it recomputes documents and assets from the recorded digests and checks the snapshot under the same rules as at submission. A newer packaging version, a digest mismatch, path escapes (`..`, absolute paths, symlink and hard-link entries), or a manifest that does not map one-to-one to the archive are all rejected, leaving no video directory behind. The staging directory (`.cuccut-import-<8 hex digits>`) is deleted in every outcome; if the process is force-killed, leftovers are deleted by the leftover cleanup on next startup. The directory name is taken from the video name in the package; duplicates get a sequence number.
- **Leftover cleanup.** After Runtime startup and Space's first scan, a background pass clears what a previous force-kill left behind. It deletes only items that meet all three conditions: the name matches CucCut's own naming; the modification time is more than 1 hour old (exports and opens that have just started, including those in other processes, are never deleted); and no running job is using it. Hidden export temporary files are looked for only in the target directories recorded by export jobs that still exist in the Ledger, and only the output file names of those jobs are matched; file names used by queued or running exports are left alone. Temporary files left by exports that the Ledger has already pruned cannot be found, so they are kept. Staging directories for opening portable packages are looked for only at the first level of the source directory, skipping opens in progress in this process. Kinds are determined by `lstat` (temporary files are regular files, staging directories are directories); symbolic links are never touched, and other directories are not searched deeper. The trash (`.ccut-trash`) does not match user file names and is never deleted. A deletion failure is only logged and does not affect startup. The publish of all exports, such as renders and subtitles, uses the same hidden temporary file (§9.11), and these are cleaned up too.
- **Entry points.** Export: `exports.create` (the UI, the CLI `cuccut export --kind portable`, and the export tools for Web and agents; risk tiering is the same as other exports). Open: `videos.importPackage` is exposed only to the desktop UI, the CLI (`cuccut import-package`) and Web (the file must be inside the project or session working directory, checked by real path, `PATH_OUTSIDE_PROJECT`); agent tools and external services have no such entry point.
- **Space.** An exported portable package is an entry produced by an export (kind `package`, source `exported`, with file size, §5.7). A `.cuccut` exported elsewhere or placed in the source directory is also recognised as `package` by its extension.

### 5.9 User library

Some data belongs to the user rather than to any one video and is reused across all videos. It is kept centrally in `library/` under Runtime Home, and is read and written only through Runtime:

| Library | Entries | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Glossaries | Transcription terms (canonical spellings and common mishearings); translation terms (source, target, language direction) | Transcription prompts, script polishing, translation |
| Voices | A reference recording, its transcript, language, origin and the speaker's declaration | `synthesizeSpeech` voice parameter, translation dubbing |
| Brand library | Overlay templates, stickers and animated stickers, image and video assets, brand colours, fonts, caption styles | The editor's picker panel |

The rules are the same for all libraries:

- **Copied on entry into a video.** When a video uses a library entry, its content at that moment is copied into the video: assets go through the normal asset import and are embedded in the video (`managed`: library files are not the user's original files and cannot be linked, §5.1); styles and templates become entities in the video, and Provenance records which version of which library entry they came from. Later changes to or deletion of a library entry do not change any existing video. “Update this video with the new library version” is an explicit editing transaction. The copy is `library.applyToVideo`, an ordinary editing transaction whose actor is determined by the originating connection (the UI and CLI act as the user): images, videos, stickers and fonts are imported via `importAsset` as `managed` into the video directory, with Provenance `origin` set to `library`, and `source.library` recording `library`, `entryId`, `version`, `contentHash`, `name` and `kind`. Caption styles become a `caption-style` document via `putDocument`, with the extension `cuccut.library` recording the same provenance; if `captionItemIds` is given, `setCaptionStyle` is applied within the same transaction. Glossaries, voices and colours are not entities in a video; copying them is rejected with `LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE`; the steps that use them freeze them by reference (see below).
- **Enabling is up to each video.** The library stores only content. Which glossaries a video's step uses, and which voice a speaker uses, are recorded in the video; a new video's default ticks are a marker on the library entry (`defaultEnabled`). The record lives in a `library-selection` document in the video (video format spec §4.6; at most one per video): it lists the enabled glossaries per step (`transcribe` for transcription only, `translate` for translation only, earlier ones take priority), and the speaker voice bindings (which speaker in which transcript uses which voice). It stores entry IDs, not content: when used, the version at that time is read and frozen. Without this document, nothing is enabled.
  - **New video** (`videos.create`, excluding opening a portable package) adopts glossaries marked `defaultEnabled` in the library: once it is created, this document is written with the actor `system:library`, outside the user's undo stack. If no entries are enabled by default, nothing is written; videos created by external-service clients do not adopt them. A failure to adopt does not affect creation; it is only logged.
  - **Read and write.** `library.getVideoSelection` reads; `library.setVideoSelection` changes it, as an ordinary editing transaction (the actor is determined by the originating connection; it can be undone). Fields given are replaced wholesale; fields not given are left as they were. On write, only what is given in this call is validated: the glossary must exist in the library, and its kind and steps must match (otherwise `LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE`); a `library:` voice must exist in the library and have no `providerId`; the speaker must exist in that transcript. Entries deleted from the library later remain in the document and are handled when used: transcription skips them; translation skips them and lists them in the summary; dubbing does not synthesise that speaker's sentences and reports each one (§7.9).
  - **Who uses it.** Transcription calls that do not pass `glossaries` use the video's enabled transcription glossaries (the gateway's `models.transcribe` and the transcription flow, and the agent's `transcribe`, behave the same); translation and dubbing usage is in §7.9. External-service clients do not read this document.
- **Entries are versioned.** Each content change produces a new integer version number and a content digest `contentHash` (SHA-256 of canonical JSON with sorted keys; files take part through their own digest); a `put` with unchanged content does not produce a new version; a mismatched `expectedVersion` gives `LIBRARY_VERSION_CONFLICT`. A job freezes the entry's version and content digest (§6.2), not the "current library": `JobRecord.library.entries` records each entry it uses. While the job runs, these versions are pinned (pin), and older versions remain readable. When the job ends the pin is released, and older versions are cleaned up on the next write to that entry or at next startup. Pinning is in memory only: after a restart, no job is running, so only the current version remains.
- **Exchangeable.** Each kind of entry has a single-file exchange format for import and export (see below). Import determines the type from the file's content and validates it, without trusting the extension; export does not overwrite existing files.
- **Outbound transfer needs separate authorisation.** Uploading a voice's reference recording to an online Provider to create a clone is an outbound data transfer: it is authorised per Provider under §12.5, and voices without the speaker's declaration are not uploaded. The only check before upload is `assertVoiceUploadAllowed(voice, providerId)` (`@cuccut/runtime-storage/library`), which throws `VOICE_CONSENT_REQUIRED` if there is no declaration (`consent.declared` and `declaredAt`). The Provider-side clone identifier is recorded on the entry (`clones`: `providerId → { voiceId, state: valid | stale, referenceHash, createdAt }`, where `referenceHash` is the digest of the reference recording that was uploaded). When the reference recording is replaced or the entry is deleted, clones are marked `stale`; after a voice with clones is deleted, an entry header containing only the clone records is kept, for use by the deletion request. `synthesizeSpeech` with `voice: 'library:<id>'` resolves at submission to this entry's valid clone on the chosen Provider, and the job freezes the voice entry. If there is no clone, or the clone is stale, it is rejected with `VOICE_CLONE_REQUIRED` (`details.reason`: `missing`, `stale`); it is not substituted with another voice.
- **Creating and deleting clones.** `library.createVoiceClone { id, providerId, name? }` submits an ordinary job (`kind: 'voiceClone'`); progress, cancellation and failure are all reported in the job. If a clone of the same version of the same voice on the same Provider is already queued or running, that job is returned. Checks run in order at submission; if any fails, no job is created: external-service clients cannot use it (`LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE`); a speaker declaration must exist (`VOICE_CONSENT_REQUIRED`); the Provider must have a clone interface (`VOICE_CLONE_UNSUPPORTED`; the first release only has ElevenLabs instant cloning, §6.4) and be enabled (`CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`); if a valid clone already exists for an unchanged reference recording, `VOICE_CLONE_EXISTS`; uploading the reference recording is an outbound transfer of data kind `audio`, which requires a covering Grant (§12.5; no approval is initiated). On execution, the version at submission time is pinned (if it no longer exists, `LIBRARY_ENTRY_GONE`); the declaration is then checked once more against the entry's current version, and if it was withdrawn while queued, nothing is uploaded. The job reserves against the Grant, uploads (only once, never retried, since a retry might create a second clone at the vendor), and settles. Once created, the clone is recorded in `clones[providerId]`; if the reference recording was replaced during upload, the recorded clone is marked `stale` directly. The replaced old clone is then sent a remote deletion request; if deletion fails, the job does not fail, and the warning `VOICE_CLONE_OLD_NOT_DELETED` is left (with the old `voiceId`). The result is a receipt artifact (`cuccut.voice-clone/1`: the frozen voice version, Provider, remote `voiceId`, reference recording digest and adapter version). If Runtime stops or crashes, running clone jobs are interrupted and not re-queued (§7.5). `library.removeVoiceClone { id, providerId, localOnly? }` first requests remote deletion, and only clears the record once the clone is deleted or the remote no longer has it (`remote`: `deleted`, `not-found`); if the remote fails, the record is kept and the Provider's error code is reported; `localOnly` clears only the local record (`remote: 'skipped'`), for use when the Provider is no longer available. Deleting an entry does not automatically delete its remote clones.
- **Not in Space.** Library entries are not artifacts and do not appear in the Space directory; “save to library” from a Space artifact is a copy (`source: { artifactId }` of `library.put`).
- **Not exposed to external programs.** The library is the local user's data; under minimal exposure, it is given only to the local UI, the CLI and agents within a session. The MCP server has no `library.*` tools. External-service clients (MCP, the model API service; submitter `kind: 'service'`) that reference glossaries in transcription or use a `library:<id>` voice in synthesis are rejected in JobManager with `LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE` (`details.reason: 'service-client'`), and no job is created. The model API service rejects a `voice` starting with `library:` with 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` before approval.

**Storage.** Each entry has its own directory. JSON is written as a temporary file followed by rename; a directory without an entry header (left by a crash mid-write) is deleted at startup. Large files are named by content digest, and the versions of the same entry share them.

```
library/
  <glossaries | voices | brand>/<entryId>/   # entryId prefixes: gls_, voc_, brd_
    entry.json                               # entry header: current version, retained versions, deletion time, voice clones
    versions/<n>.json                        # content and digest of each version
    files/<sha256 hex>.<ext>                 # reference recordings, images, videos, stickers, fonts
```

**Entries.**

| Library | Content | Validation and limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Glossary | `name`, `kind`, `defaultEnabled`, `terms`. For transcription (`transcription`): optional `language`; each item is a canonical spelling `canonical` and a list of mishearings `misheard[]`. For translation (`translation`): optional `sourceLanguage`, required `targetLanguage`; each item is `source`, `target` and an optional `note` | Language must be a valid BCP 47 tag; canonical spellings or source texts must not repeat and must not contain line breaks |
| Voice | `name`, `language`, the reference recording's transcript `transcript`, `origin` (`recorded`, `imported`), `consent` (`declared`, `declaredAt`, `statement`), the reference recording | wav, mp3 or flac, ≤ 20 MiB; file header must match and ffprobe must decode audio; `declaredAt` is updated when the declaration text changes |
| Brand library | `name` and `kind`: `image`, `video`, `sticker` (image or Lottie), `font` (ttf, otf, woff2), each with one file; `color` is `#RRGGBB` or `#RRGGBBAA`; `captionStyle` is a style object with a string `schema` (≤ 64 KiB), which becomes the document body when copied into a video | Images, stickers and fonts ≤ 50 MiB; Lottie ≤ 10 MiB; videos ≤ 4 GiB; `overlayTemplate` only reserves the kind, and `put` rejects it with `LIBRARY_KIND_RESERVED` (§14) |

**Exchange formats.** On import, the type is determined from content: a text with `format: cuccut.glossary` in its front matter is a glossary; JSON with `format` `cuccut.voice-package` is a voice package; JSON of `cuccut.library-item` is a brand-library colour or caption style; Lottie JSON and `.lottie` archives (a zip whose central directory contains `animations/*.json` or `a/*.json`) are stickers; png, jpeg, gif and webp are images (a sticker's kind is changed after import); mp4, mov, webm and mkv are videos; ttf, otf and woff2 are fonts. Standalone audio files cannot be imported (a voice also needs a transcript and a consent statement); anything else is rejected with `LIBRARY_FORMAT_INVALID`.

- **Glossary** is UTF-8 Markdown, ≤ 4 MiB. Front matter has `format: cuccut.glossary`, `version: 1`, `name` (a JSON string or plain text; the file name is used if omitted), `kind`, the optional `language` for transcription, the optional `source` and required `target` for translation, and `default: true | false` (false if omitted). The body contains exactly one table; explanatory text may precede it. Header matching is case-insensitive: for transcription it is `| Term | Misheard |`, for translation `| Source | Target | Note |`. In cells, `\|` and `\\` are escapes; mishearings are separated by `,`, `，` or `、`, and if a spelling itself contains a separator, escape it with `\`.

  ```markdown
  ---
  format: cuccut.glossary
  version: 1
  name: "产品名"
  kind: transcription
  language: zh-CN
  default: true
  ---

  | Term | Misheard |
  | --- | --- |
  | CucCut | 宝卡特, 包剪 |
  ```

- **Voice package** `.bcvoice` is a UTF-8 JSON file with the reference recording embedded as base64: `format: "cuccut.voice-package"`, `version: 1`, `name`, `language`, `transcript`, `origin`, `consent`, `reference: { fileName, mediaType, sha256, byteLength, data }`. The whole file is ≤ 32 MiB, and the recording ≤ 20 MiB. On import, the format and version, fields, base64, the decoded length and digest, and the file header (wav, mp3 or flac, matching `mediaType`) are checked; finally ffprobe decodes it. The declaration time in the package is kept. Clones on each Provider are not exported: moving to another machine or account requires re-cloning.
- **Brand library** entries with files export the original file; colours and caption styles export `{ "format": "cuccut.library-item", "version": 1, "library": "brand", "content": { … } }`.

**Transcription glossaries.** The `glossaries: [{ id, version? }]` of `models.transcribe` (at most 20; must be transcription glossaries, otherwise `LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE`) are resolved and frozen at submission. Resolution happens in JobManager; the gateway's `models.transcribe` and the transcription flow (the agent's `transcribe` tool, which uses the video's enabled glossaries) take the same path, and synthesised `library:<id>` voices work the same way. When the model accepts a hint (`acceptsHint`), the canonical spellings from each table are de-duplicated in order, joined with “、”, and placed on the line after the user's hint, up to 1200 characters in total; spellings that do not fit are dropped and counted. When the model does not accept hints, glossaries are ignored without error. A user-supplied `hint` is likewise not passed to the model; from submission the job carries a `hint-ignored` warning, which is combined with the result's warnings on completion. `JobRecord.library.glossaryHint` records `status` (`sent`, `unsupported`), the number of items sent and the number dropped. Mishearings are not included in the hint; they are left for script polishing.

The `library` namespace provides `list`, `get`, `put`, `remove`, `import`, `export`, `applyToVideo`, `getVideoSelection`, `setVideoSelection`, `createVoiceClone`, `removeVoiceClone`, and the short-lived file handle `openHandle` (command and protocol spec §4.1). Changes are delivered on the `library` topic (the snapshot is a summary of all entries, followed by `entry.upsert` and `entry.removed`). The CLI is `cuccut library` (cloning is `cuccut library voice-clone <id> --provider <p>` and `voice-clone-remove <id> --provider <p> [--local-only]`; the entries enabled in a video are `cuccut library video-selection <videoId>`, changed with `--transcribe-glossaries`, `--translate-glossaries`, and `--speaker-voice <transcript>:<speaker>=<voice>[@<Provider>]`).

Statistics derived from measurement (for example, the speaking-rate distribution of each synthesis model in each language) are derived data. They are kept in the cache, can be deleted and rebuilt, and do not belong to the user library.

### 5.10 Preferences

Preferences are stored in two places, divided by "who needs to read them":

- **Held by Runtime**: settings that affect tasks, the CLI and agent results. For example, the target line length for captions, the follow-up action after transcription completes, the default save location, the default Driver and model (§3.11), capability defaults (§6.8), and switches for updates and diagnostics. Stored in the Runtime Store, read and written through `settings.get` and `settings.set`; changes are delivered on the `settings` topic. A task freezes the values it uses when it is created.
- **Held by the UI**: settings that only affect how this one interface is presented. For example, UI language, appearance, keyboard shortcut bindings, panel layout. Stored in the UI's own storage; they do not go through Runtime.
- **New caption style preferences** are remembered by each client's own storage (product design §5.9); the desktop app and the Web client each keep their own. Only successfully committed style changes are recorded; overrides to the appearance of the source text and of the translation are recorded separately. Opening an existing video does not change the preference, and does not overwrite its style document. When generating, translating or importing new captions, the preference is written into the video's `caption-style` document, in the same transaction as the caption instance. When the CLI and agents do not specify a style, the default preset is still used.
- **UI language** is held in both places. The UI stores its preference in its own storage (`system` or one shipped language), and resolves the current language from it together with the system language. The desktop app also writes the preference into Runtime's `ui.language`, and the text Runtime and the CLI generate (errors, task descriptions, notifications) is localized according to it. On connection, the UI's preference takes precedence; if `ui.language` is later changed elsewhere (for example in the CLI), the desktop UI follows it. The Web client only changes its own UI. When the value is `system`, Runtime checks `CUCCUT_SYSTEM_LANGUAGES`, which the desktop app passes when it launches Runtime, and then the POSIX locale settings. When the system language has no matching shipped language, English is used. `CUCCUT_LOCALE` takes precedence over everything (for testing and troubleshooting).
- **Text already stored also follows the language**: besides the text in the language used at generation time, the human-facing text Runtime produces also carries message references (`key` + `params`, with a field `fooRef` beside the field `foo`), stored together with records and events. The UI and the CLI regenerate it from the reference in their own current language. A reference they do not recognize, or an old record without a reference, uses the stored text as is. Convention: [repo conventions §5](../repo-conventions.md#5-copy-and-multilingual-support).

The settings registry is part of the contract (`settings.ts` in `@cuccut/protocol`; the validation schemas are in `schemas.ts`). Every key has a stable name (a lowercase dot-separated name, not renamed after release), a schema, a default value and a one-sentence description.

- **Validate the whole batch, apply atomically.** If `settings.set { values }` contains one unknown key or one value that does not match its schema, the whole batch is rejected with `invalid-request`, and nothing is saved. After validation passes, the new file is written to a temporary path and then renamed. Only after the write to disk succeeds is the in-memory state swapped and the notification sent. Writes run serially, so a crash never leaves a half-written file.
- **`null` restores the default.** A value equal to the default is not stored separately; the file records only values that differ from their defaults, so "is the default" means "has never been changed". `settings.get { keys? }` returns `{ settings, defaults }` (effective values and defaults; all keys when `keys` is not given). `settings.set` returns the new complete view.
- **`settings` topic.** On subscribe, a snapshot of all keys is sent first. After that, each change to an effective value sends one `settings.updated`, carrying only the changed keys and their new effective values. Changes that do not alter any effective value send nothing.
- **File**: `<runtime-home>/store/settings.json`, with `schemaVersion`. A value read that does not match its schema (hand-corrupted) is treated as the default. A key read that is not recognized (written by a newer version) is kept as is and has no effect. If the whole file is not valid JSON, Runtime fails to start, consistent with the other Store files.
- **Freeze.** When a task or session is created, it takes a copy of each key's value together with its source (set by the user or the default). Later setting changes do not affect it. A new session freezes the default Driver; if that Driver is not registered, creation is rejected with `driver-unavailable` and does not fall back to another Driver. A new task freezes its access mode, recorded on the task card (`autonomy.source`: `request` when given explicitly at send time, `conversation` when switched in the session, `setting` from `agent.defaultAccessMode`, `default` for the built-in default). A mode given explicitly at send time takes precedence over the session's mode, and the session's mode takes precedence over the setting. If the session's mode is switched while a task is running, the task switches to the new mode (§3.12).
- **Not here**: credentials (§6.8). The registry holds no keys, tokens or passwords. The default Provider and model for model capabilities are managed by `models.setDefault` (§6.8).

First batch of keys:

| Key | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `agent.defaultDriver` | Driver id or `null` | `null` (built-in default `codex`) | Driver used by new sessions (§3.11) |
| `agent.defaultModel` | model id, `__agent-default__` or `null` | `null` (recommended model) | Model for new sessions. Applies only to sessions of the default Driver, and only when that Driver's Agent preferences have no default model. `null` uses the recommended model (Claude Code's Sonnet, Codex's `-sol`). `__agent-default__` passes no model and follows the Agent's own CLI configuration. Frozen at session creation; handed to the Driver on every turn (§3.11 "Default model and recommended model") |
| `agent.defaultEffort` | reasoning effort or `null` | `null` (Driver's own default) | Reasoning effort for new sessions; same rules as above |
| `agent.defaultAccessMode` | `plan` `ask` `autoAcceptEdits` `auto` `fullAccess` | `auto` | Used by sessions that have not switched mode (§3.12). Writes also accept the legacy values `controlled` and `authorized`, saved as `ask` and `fullAccess`; legacy values stored in the file are likewise replaced when read |
| `ui.language` | `system` or one shipped language (`LOCALES`: `zh-Hans` `zh-Hant` `en` …) | `system` | Language for text generated by Runtime and the CLI; the desktop app writes it along with the UI language preference (see above) |
| `captions.maxLineLength` | `{ cjk, other }` (character count) | `{ cjk: 16, other: 42 }` | Target line length for automatic line breaks. CJK text and other text are set separately |
| `transcribe.afterComplete` | `open-video` `notify` `nothing` | `open-video` | Follow-up action after transcription completes |
| `downloads.directory` | absolute path or `null` | `null` (the run host's Downloads folder, §7.9) | Default save location. Results from tools that have no video, media downloaded from links, and files handed over by `downloads_save` all go here; when set, it is always used (§7.9 "Save location") |
| `models.downloadEndpoint` | `http(s)://` base URL or `null` | `null` (public model repository `https://huggingface.co`) | Download source for local models (a mirror). `https://www.modelscope.cn` downloads from ModelScope (following its path rules, §6.3). No credentials, query parameters or fragments. The environment variable `CUCCUT_MODELS_ENDPOINT` takes precedence (§6.3) |
| `models.dir` | absolute path or `null` | `null` (`<runtime-home>/models`) | Local model directory. Can only be changed through `models.setDir`; `settings.set` rejects it with `invalid-request`, `SETTING_MANAGED`, because the change must first check what is in use, unload Workers, and choose between moving the files or only relocating the path. The environment variable `CUCCUT_MODELS_DIR` takes precedence, and in that case the setting is read-only (§6.3) |
| `tools.downloadEndpoint` | `http(s)://` base URL or `null` | `null` (official release address in the manifest) | Download source (a mirror) for managed external tools. The address is `<base>/<tool>/<version>/<filename>`. No credentials, query parameters or fragments. The environment variable `CUCCUT_TOOLS_ENDPOINT` takes precedence (§12.9) |
| `fonts.autoDownload` | boolean | `true` | When preview or export needs a family that is in the font catalog but not installed on this machine, it is downloaded automatically (§9.1). When off, rendering uses fallback fonts and export gives a `FONT_NOT_DOWNLOADED` warning |
| `fonts.cssEndpoint` / `fonts.fileEndpoint` | `https://` base URL or `null` | `null` (`https://fonts.googleapis.com` / `https://fonts.gstatic.com`) | Stylesheet endpoint and font file host (a mirror) for on-demand font downloads. Only `https` is accepted; no credentials, query parameters or fragments. File addresses must sit under the file host's base URL (§9.1) |
| `updates.autoCheck` / `updates.autoDownload` | boolean | `true` / `true` | Preferences for app updates (§2.6) |
| `cache.maxSizeMiB` | integer 256–1048576 (MiB) | `2048` | Total size limit for `<runtime-home>/cache/`. Checked once after startup and then every hour; when over the limit, the oldest entries by modification time are deleted until it drops to 90% (§5.1) |
| `space.trashRetentionDays` | integer 1–3650 (days) | `30` | Retention period for the Space trash. Entries moved to the trash for longer than this many days are physically deleted when nothing references them (§5.7) |
| `resources.capacity` | `{ memoryMiB, gpuMemoryMiB, cpuThreads }` (each item may be `null`) or `null` | `null` (detected from this machine) | Advanced: overrides the capacity that resource scheduling sees. An item set to `null` stays automatic (§7.6) |
| `runtime.idleExitMinutes` | integer 1–1440 (minutes) | `10` | A Runtime launched by the CLI exits on its own after being idle for this long: no active local gateway connection, no queued or running task, and no open external service. CLI connections that only query status do not count as active (§2.2). The desktop app and manually started Runtimes are not affected |
| `diagnostics.enabled` | boolean | `false` | Anonymous usage statistics and performance summaries (§12.6) |
| `offline.strict` | boolean | `false` | Strict offline mode. Currently blocks downloads of local models (§6.3), managed external tools (§12.9), fonts (§9.1), and imports from links (§7.9). Online calls are not yet blocked (product design §12) |

The CLI's `cuccut settings` (lists keys, current values, and whether each is default), `settings get <key>`, `settings set <key> <value>` (the value is parsed as JSON; if it cannot be parsed, it is treated as a string) and `settings reset <key>` use the same set of methods.

### 5.11 Cross-video content search

`speech.search` searches one open video (§3.7). To look across all videos by transcript, translation, chapter and speaker, it uses a content index maintained by Runtime:

- It is a derived cache (§5.1) that stores, per video, the video version at indexing time. After a video submits a new version, that video's entries are rebuilt incrementally. The whole index can be deleted and rebuilt; during a rebuild, search results are marked as incomplete.
- The index is generated by Runtime through VideoService's read-only queries; it does not read `video.db` directly. Videos that are not open can also be searched. Searching does not open a video and does not take its write lock.
- `space.search` returns videos, document kinds, time positions and snippets, filtered by the scope of the subject and external services (§4.8). Time positions in a hit are based on the version at indexing time; after the video is opened, the client re-locates them against the current version.

**Implementation of this version** (`packages/runtime-core/src/space/content-*.ts`):

- **Reading.** The Engine Host's internal read-only query `videos.readContent` (not in the gateway's method table) provides a video snapshot and the current text of text documents (transcript, captions, translation). Transcript and captions also carry a timeline projection on the root sequence (the same `plan_text` as export). A video that is already open uses the open instance. A video that is not open is opened read-only, read, and released immediately, without taking the write lock.
- **Cache.** One SQLite database, `<home>/cache/content-index/index.db` (Node's built-in `node:sqlite`, WAL mode, with `-wal` / `-shm` sidecar files). The `videos` table has one row per video (the real directory path is the primary key; it records videoId, the version at indexing time, the directory's modification time, and the video facts as JSON). The `segment_rows` table holds the searchable segments (source text, NFKC-lowercased body, time, kind, speaker; indexed by directory). `segments` is an FTS5 external-content table (trigram tokenizer) built on the body and kept in sync by triggers. Each video's segments are replaced as a whole in one transaction. Only one video is read at a time. The format version is recorded in `PRAGMA user_version`. On a mismatch, when the file is not a database, or when it cannot be opened, the connection is closed, the database (including its sidecar files) is deleted and rebuilt, and everything is re-read. There is no migration. Old `<digest>.json` files are deleted at startup. `space.rebuildIndex` does not delete the database: it marks everything as pending rebuild and queues a re-read. Until the re-read finishes, old segments remain searchable, and results are marked incomplete. If the database cannot be opened, it degrades: records and facts are kept only in memory, search returns no hits, and a warning is logged.
- **Matching.** NFKC and lowercase, split on whitespace. A hit requires all terms to appear in the same segment (AND). Chinese is not word-segmented. No relevance ranking: results follow the caller's video order (most recent activity first), and within a video, time order. Words of three or more code points go through FTS5 MATCH (each word as a quoted phrase). Shorter words (common two-character Chinese words such as 剪辑, "edit") cannot use trigram, so they fall back to `instr` substring filtering. `kinds` uses `IN`, and speakers match a normalized substring. Candidate segments are finally re-checked with the same substring rules and snippets are generated, so the results are equivalent to substring matching.
- **Video facts.** These cover what the Space directory needs (material used on the timeline, linked material, §5.7) and what the tool candidate inputs need (§7.9). Both are taken from the text, projection and snapshot already provided by `videos.readContent`:
  - Transcript (`speech` document): document ID, language, whether it has word-level timing (has visible words, and no word whose `timingQuality` is `estimated` or `missing`), and whether it is on the timeline (the projection on the root sequence has content).
  - Translation: document ID, target language, the transcript it was translated from (`sourceDocumentId` in the document header, or the transcript in the body's `sourceBasis`), number of units, and number of stale units. Staleness follows the dubbing rules (§7.9): marked stale, source sentence gone, fingerprint mismatch, or empty translation. `/1` translations have no fingerprint, so only units marked stale and empty are counted. When the source transcript is not in the video, all units count as stale. If the glossary has changed (`glossary-changed`), that requires reading the user's library, which the index does not judge; dubbing still checks it.
  - Dubbing group: group ID, language, dubbing plan, the translation used and the transcript it was translated from, and the number of dubbing instances on the timeline. The `dubbing-plan` document (`groupId` in its summary) together with instances carrying the `cuccut.dub` extension; separated background and vocal tracks are not counted as instances.
  - Cover (used by `space.thumbnail`, §5.7): the material ID of the cover frame, its version and source time, plus that version's content digest, media type and duration. File paths are not recorded. Null when there is no visible picture.
  - Duration and canvas size (`media` of the video entry, §5.7): the duration of the root sequence (the latest-ending instance, converted to seconds with the sequence frame rate; same `sequenceDurationFrames` as the video summary in the agent tool, regardless of `durationPolicy`), and the canvas width and height. Null when the snapshot is incomplete.
  - Derivation source (`origin` of the video entry, §5.7): the parent video, parent video version, idempotency key and range from the summary of the current version of the `derivation` document. Null if the video is not derived. `videos.derive` also uses it to recognize existing child videos.
- **Incremental.** The video directory comes from scanning the Space directory. A newly appearing video is read once. When a modification time changes, `videos.inspect` checks the version first; if the version is unchanged, only the new modification time is recorded. For an open video that submits a new version, a change notification from VideoService triggers a re-read. Videos that disappear from the source directory are deleted together with their cache files. To get a video that has no current index yet (`space.thumbnail`), it is moved to the front of the queue, and only that one is awaited.
- **Rebuild.** `space.rebuildIndex` deletes the cache and queues all videos for re-read. Until the re-read finishes, old records remain searchable but count as pending indexing. Videos that cannot be read (engine unavailable, an older engine without `videos.readContent`, a corrupted database) also count as pending indexing, and are retried at the next change or rebuild. When there are pending videos, the result is `complete: false`, and `pendingVideos` gives the count.
- **Segments.** Transcript and captions are split into the sentences that export produces (`segmentsOf`), with times on the sequence timeline. When a document's material is not on the timeline (projection empty or errored), it falls back to source time (`clock: 'source'`). Translations borrow the time of the transcript sentence they correspond to: `cuccut.translation/2` matches by `sourceSentenceId`; when the transcript has no stored sentences, it matches by the first word of the aligned words or sentence IDs. `/1` matches by unit ID. Stale units are not included. Chapters are the titles and descriptions of the `kind: 'chapter'` markers on the root sequence. Speakers are taken from the names in the transcript or captions.
- **Matching.** No search library is introduced. Text undergoes NFKC and lowercasing, then each segment is matched by substring; several whitespace-separated terms must all appear in the same segment. Search can also filter by speaker alone. Chinese is not word-segmented; substring matching is itself character-based, so searching 剪辑 ("edit") finds 视频剪辑 ("video editing"), at the cost of no word boundaries (剪 "cut" also matches 剪刀 "scissors"). There is no relevance ranking: segments are ordered by the video's recent activity, and within one video, by time. Each search linearly scans all segments in scope. With thousands of videos of a few hundred segments each, this takes tens of milliseconds. At larger scale, switch to a bigram inverted index; the interface stays unchanged.

---

## 6. Models

### 6.1 Capability × source

The Models module organizes model services along two independent axes: **capability** says what to do, and **Provider** says who does it. A single capability is not bound to one vendor.

**Capability.** Each capability has a capability contract: input parameters, `outputContract` (§6.4), and how it is applied to the video.

| Capability | Output contract | Applied to the video |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `transcribe` | `cuccut.asr-result/v1` (§6.6) | Writes a `speech` document |
| `synthesizeSpeech` | Audio file, with duration, sample rate, channels and optional word timings | Imported as an audio asset, as a candidate; not automatically placed on the timeline |
| `generateImage` | Image file, with dimensions and available seed | Imported as an image asset, as a candidate |
| `generateMusic` | One WAV (song or background music), with duration, sample rate and seed | Imported as an audio asset, as a candidate; not automatically placed on the timeline. The only executor is the local Provider |
| `generateText` | Plain text, or structured text conforming to a given JSON Schema (translations, polish results, chapters, title and synopsis candidates) | Validated by the initiating flow and written to the corresponding document, or offered as a candidate (§7.9) |
| `separateAudio` | Two audio tracks, vocals and background, with the same length and sample rate as the input | Validated by the dubbing flow, then placed on the timeline as background and vocal tracks (§7.9). The only executor is the local Provider |
| `analyzeVision` | Time-coded annotations by purpose: person position, speaker judgment, content region | Serves as analysis output for flows such as smart cropping to read; does not modify the video directly |

Forced alignment, speech analysis, speaker diarization and video generation follow the same pattern: one capability contract, plus the Provider that implements it. The model must never be allowed to fabricate exact video frames.

- **`generateText`** is a call to a text model: the input is an ordered list of messages plus an output format (plain text, or structured output constrained by a JSON Schema). Structured output must pass schema validation (§6.4). Work done sentence by sentence or paragraph by paragraph, such as translation, polishing and chaptering, is handled with it, without creating a full AgentSession for each sentence. It has two entry points that share one execution path: `models.generateText` submits a Job, and the result is published as a text artifact and leaves a generation record (§7.9); a flow inside the Runtime calls it directly through the in-process `TextGenerator`, without creating a Job, shares each Provider's concurrency cap and retry policy with Jobs, and accumulates counts of calls, retries and failures. Its Providers are initially only online APIs; LAN nodes do not share it (§6.7). The default model, reasoning effort and concurrency cap are configuration for this capability (§6.8). Who completes the same task depends on the entry point: when initiated from a session, the agent completes it; when initiated from the tool entry point, `generateText` completes it (§7.9). Both paths write to the video using the same editing transaction.
- **`separateAudio`** is used only in the dubbing flow. The executor interface is `DubSeparator` (input: the file of the source material being transcribed; output: two files, vocals and background). Its implementation is a local Provider: the model is the `separate` local model pack (currently only HTDemucs-FT, §6.3), chosen per §6.2. That means the user's default, otherwise the factory default (the first one that is installed, available and not disabled, by ID). If no usable one can be chosen, it is treated as having no executor. When dubbing is submitted, the model pack (`separatorModel`) and its weight estimate are chosen and frozen, and retries reuse them. At execution, if that model pack is no longer available, this step fails with `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED` and does not switch to another. The whole separation runs in its Model Worker (Model Worker protocol spec §2.5.4); the output sample rate follows the input track's. Provider failures are mapped to this step's error codes (`MODEL_LOAD_FAILED`, `MODEL_WORKER_CRASHED`, `INPUT_UNREADABLE`, `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID`; failure to write the output into staging is `STAGING_WRITE_FAILED`, which does not count as a crash). The capability view lists `separateAudio` (local Provider only), and the default is set via `models.setDefault`. The online separation service is not integrated, and there is no separate separation command. The API providers (§6.4), models declared by self-hosted endpoints and external model interfaces (§4.8) do not involve it. Output contract: vocals and background each form one audio track decodable by ffprobe, with duration differing from the input by no more than 20 ms, and the same sample rate as the input. Container duration is determined by the longest stream. If the two tracks the Worker writes differ from it by more than 20 ms, the executor uses ffmpeg to pad both to the container duration (silence if short, trimmed if long). Non-conformance is `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID` (`details.problems` lists each item) and is not retried. When no usable separation model pack is available, dubbing that requests separation is still accepted; the separation step is recorded as `skipped` and reported honestly, without switching to other processing (§7.9).
- **`analyzeVision`** chooses models separately for each purpose: each purpose (person localization, speaker judgment, content region) has its own default model, and they do not substitute for one another.
- **Voice for `synthesizeSpeech`** can be specified in three ways, and the Provider description declares which are supported: a preset voice ID; a reference recording plus the original text (cloning, from a voice in the user's library or a segment of the video, §5.9); or a text description. A model that does not support a given method rejects the request at submission and does not substitute a different voice. The model description's `voiceModes` declares whether it accepts `preset` (built into the model, voices listed in `voices`) or `custom` (voice IDs in the vendor's account, passed through as-is for the vendor to verify). A model whose `defaultVoice` is null must be given a voice. Reading annotations (for polyphonic characters) are part of the input text (`<字|读音>`, where the reading is written as tone digits or pinyin with tone marks; parsing normalizes it to tone digits; syntax is in Model Worker protocol spec §2.5.2). The adapter converts them to each model's own notation. If a model does not support them, this is reported honestly, not silently dropped.
- **Local synthesis** (`provider: 'local'`): the model is a `synthesize` local model pack (§6.3), and the model description additionally carries `local`: model family; native sample rate; whether it is slow; whether built-in voices are recordings or descriptions; suggested duration of the reference recording and whether it reads the original text; whether the description is free text or a closed vocabulary; whether it accepts tone instructions and emotion; the range of knobs (`speed`, `cfg`, `steps`); the duration limit; the notation for reading annotations (homophones, inline pinyin, annotated or unsupported); and the license. A reading annotation it cannot pronounce does not fail the task. It is synthesized from the surface text, and the result carries a `reading-dropped` warning. The three methods map to the request's `voice` (the model's speaker or built-in voice), `reference` (a reference recording file plus optional original text; `library:<id>` uses the recording of a voice-library entry directly, without needing online cloning) and `voiceDescription`; at most one may be given. The voice is decided and frozen at submission. If unspecified, the cloning model uses its built-in voice (a recording shipped with the app, chosen to match the requested language, or the first one if none exists in that language), and the speaker model uses its default speaker. The reference recording (including built-in voices) has its digest and length read and frozen into `generation.reference` at submission, and is re-checked against the digest before execution. If it changed or cannot be read, the task fails with `ASSET_MISSING` rather than switching voices. Output is WAV. Non-commercial models (OmniVoice's weights are CC-BY-NC) state this in `license.commercialUse: false`, and the UI prompts before selection.
- **Local image generation** (`provider: 'local'`): the model is an `image` local model pack (§6.3). Model description: one image at a time, outputs PNG only, does not accept reference images, accepts `seed`, and is free of charge. Sizes are limited to the tiers the model pack lists (Qwen-Image-2.1 offers 1:1 with long side 1024, 16:9, 9:16, 4:3, 3:4 and 2.35:1, default 1:1; plus 512×512, which does not fit the frame menu and is offered to the settings page's test draw). A request may give `size` or an aspect ratio; anything else is rejected at submission. The prompt is at most 1024 code points. `notes` tells the user it generates on this machine and takes several minutes (candle on CPU is stated honestly as taking several hours, §6.5). `seed` is frozen at submission: if the request doesn't give one, one is drawn (0..2^32-1) and written into `generation`, so retries and re-executions yield the same image. The number of denoising steps is the request's `steps`, with the range given by the model description's `local.steps` (8–40 for Qwen-Image-2.1, with the UI adjusting by 4 at a time). When not given, the model's default (20) is used. If given, it is frozen into `generation`, and an online model receiving `steps` rejects it at submission. Non-commercial models (Qwen-Image's license permits only research and evaluation) likewise state this in `license`.
- **Local music generation** (`generateMusic`, `provider: 'local'`): the model is a `music` local model pack (§6.3), executed by the audio.cpp engine (`audiocpp_cli`), not through the Model Worker. Each task starts one engine process, which reads the GGUF in the model pack directory, writes the WAV into staging, and exits when done (engine lookup, command line and output contract are in Model Worker protocol spec §4.4). Model description: two methods, `song` (by style and lyrics, with vocals) or `instrumental` (by style, no vocals); outputs WAV only; accepts `seed` and diffusion step count `steps`; free of charge. `maxStyleChars` and `maxLyricsChars` are the code-point limits for style and lyrics. `maxDurationSec` is the maximum duration that can be generated in one go (300 seconds for YuE2 3B), and `defaultDurationSec` is the duration used when no budget is given (120 seconds). `local` additionally carries the model family; the engine and the executable it currently finds; the sample rate; the lyrics language (`unverified` if upstream has not been checked); orchestration options (`planning`: `off` generates directly, `melody` plans the melody first, `full` plans fully; YuE2 defaults to `melody`); the step range (1–50, default 8); `slow`; and the license (YuE2 is CC-BY-NC-4.0, not commercially usable, `license.commercialUse: false`). The request's `maxDurationSec` is a compute budget (converted to the engine's semantic token limit), not a promised duration. When not given, `targetDurationSec` is used, and failing that, the default budget; `targetDurationSec` is only a creative intent. A result that hits the budget carries an `output-truncated` warning. `seed` is frozen at submission (drawn if not given), and retries on the same machine yield the same track. There is no online Provider and no factory default (§6.2). The engine is a shared component of the model pack (`audiocpp-engine`, §6.3), downloaded with the model pack. If the model pack is not installed, or the weights are present but the engine component is missing, both report `not-installed` with an install remedy; the `detail` differs (the latter names this component). When the current host platform has no engine package and there is no `CUCCUT_AUDIOCPP_CLI`, it reports `unsupported`.

**Provider**. Sources fall into three categories. They implement the same per-capability interface and produce the same output contract; validation, publishing and application after JobManager do not differ by source (§7.1).

| Source | `providerId` | Where it runs | Implementation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Local | `local` | This machine's Model Worker (§6.5); music generation is one audio.cpp engine process per task (§6.1) | TypeScript local Provider plus Rust Worker (music generation adds the external audio.cpp engine) |
| LAN node | `node:<nodeId>` | Model Worker on another machine (§6.7) | TypeScript |
| Online API | `openai`, `anthropic`, `google`, `deepseek`, … in the API provider catalog (§6.4), plus user-defined compatible endpoints | The vendor's service | TypeScript (§6.4) |
| Agent | `agent:<driverId>`, only `agent:codex` in the first version | Agent runtime installed and logged in on this machine | TypeScript (§6.9) |

Online Providers are organized by **API provider**: one `providerId` is the access point of one model vendor, one relay platform or one self-hosted endpoint, and its account (key) is shared across all capabilities that this provider implements (§6.4, §6.8). Capability and Provider are still two axes: the same OpenAI key is used for `generateText`, `transcribe`, `synthesizeSpeech` and `generateImage` alike, and each capability's default is still set separately.

**Provider description**. Each Provider declares to the registry: which capabilities it supports; which models exist under each capability; each model's limits and features (input size and duration limits, languages, whether word-level timing is returned, whether it accepts a prompt, voices, image sizes, cost status); and whether it is currently available and, if not, why. Before routing, the description is checked for language, timing marks, voice, format, image input, output size, permissions and budget. Anything unsatisfied is rejected at submission rather than failing midway through execution. Differences between Providers appear only in the description. For example, when online transcription does not return word-level timing, the result's `timingQuality` is marked `estimated`, the same as when there is no local aligner (§6.6).

Returns a unified domain artifact, and archives the auditable raw response.

### 6.2 Selection, defaults, and failure policy

**Selection order**. The provider and model for a call are determined in the order below; take the first one that is given:

1. The `provider` and `model` given explicitly in the call parameters;
2. The user's default provider and default model for this capability (§6.8);
3. Factory default: only `transcribe` and `separateAudio` have one. If a local model pack is installed and usable on this machine, use `local` (for `separateAudio`, take the first by ID). `synthesizeSpeech`, `generateImage`, `generateMusic`, and `generateText` have no factory default, and still have none once a local provider offers them;
4. None of the above: the call is rejected with `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`.

There is no automatic local-first, cloud fallback, or failover. The factory default does not point to any online provider: material may leave the machine only through the user's configuration or an explicit choice.

Details: If only `provider` is given, use the default model when the user default points to that provider; otherwise use that provider's default model. If only `model` is given, first check whether the default provider (user default or factory default) offers it, then `local`, then the sole provider that lists it; if several providers offer it, `provider` must be given as well. A selected provider or model that is unavailable is rejected outright: it does not fall through to the next step and does not switch provider. An explicitly given provider or model that does not exist is `not-found`. A node is written `node:<nodeId>`; `node:<alias>` is also accepted, and after selection it is replaced by the `nodeId` and frozen. A model pack that a node does not list is still allowed through; it is left to the node's preflight (§6.7).

The actual task freezes `providerId`, `modelId`, the version or manifest, parameters, inputs, and policy decisions. At submission, a generation task checks the model description and fills in all parameters (voice, language, format, speaking rate, size, count, seed); they are frozen in the job record's `generation`, and execution and recovery read only this copy.

- Retries must not silently change the model or voice. Generation jobs are not retried automatically: a failure that persists after the bounded retries for online providers (§6.4) is a failure, and generating again is a new job.
- Moving from free to paid, or from local to cloud, requires re-verifying authorization (§12.5).
- An unavailable capability states its exact reason: not configured, missing key, missing installation, not connected, insufficient permission, or unsupported. Manual editing and the other local capabilities remain available in this case.

**When nothing is configured**. `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED` is a structured result, not a generic error: `details` gives the capability, a reason (`no-default`, `missing-credential`, `not-installed`, `signed-out`, `outdated`, `not-paired`, `not-connected`, `disabled`, `unsupported`; `signed-out` and `outdated` come only from the agent provider, §6.9), and a feasible remedy (`remedy.action`: `configure-provider`, `enable-provider`, `set-default`, `install-model`, `pair-node`, `setup-agent`, with a one-sentence human-readable `hint`). It is returned at submission and creates no job. An agent uses it to explain the situation to the user; the UI provides an entry point into settings. When there is no default (`no-default`): if a usable provider already exists, the action is `set-default`; for `transcribe` and `synthesizeSpeech`, if a local model pack is not installed, it is `install-model`; for `generateImage`, even if an uninstalled text-to-image model pack exists, it is `configure-provider` — when the page is opened, the UI falls back only to a connected cloud model, and local image generation requires the user to choose it or set it as the default.

Settings show the Agent Driver configuration and each model capability's configuration separately, and guide the user according to what the task needs.

### 6.3 Local model management

Node manages the model catalog, versions, download plans, verification, and resource admission; the Rust Worker holds the weights and the device context.

States: `not-installed`, `downloading`, `installed`, `loading`, `ready`, `busy`, `unloading`, `error`. Do not fabricate a percentage when the actual progress is unknown. Pack status in `models.list` and in the `models` topic additionally carries `estimatedBytes` (for required components, the download size from the built-in manifest, given whether or not installed, so the size can be shown before download; null if unknown), `components` (for each component: repository, revision, installed or not, size, and which packs share it; components with a registered license also carry the license), `install` (installation progress: `queued`, `downloading`, `verifying`, or `paused`, with bytes received and total bytes, total null if unknown), and `selfTest` (the most recent check). The snapshot for the `models` topic is `{ capabilities, bundles }`, and changes in pack status emit `bundle.updated`.

**Catalog and components**. The model catalog lists installable packs by capability: `transcribe` recognition packs (Qwen3-ASR 0.6B and 1.7B, MLX; Whisper large-v3 and large-v3-turbo, Core ML; MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize, MLX 8-bit); `synthesize` speech packs (five variants of Qwen3-TTS, IndexTTS2, IndexTTS 2.5, GPT-SoVITS v2, VoxCPM2, OmniVoice); `image` text-to-image packs (the Qwen-Image-2.1 MLX 4-bit pack, one repository of about 9.8 GB holding the text encoder, DiT, and VAE; on platforms other than Apple Silicon the catalog lists the candle variant of the same repository, `qwen-image-2.1@candle`, which is dequantized at load time, §6.5); `separate` separation packs (HTDemucs-FT; the upstream `facebookresearch/demucs` code and weights are MIT; on platforms other than Apple Silicon the catalog lists the candle variant of the same repository, `htdemucs-ft@candle`, whose fp16 weights are upcast to f32 at load time, §6.5); `diarize` speaker-diarization packs (`speaker-diarization@mlx`; on platforms other than Apple Silicon the catalog lists `speaker-diarization@candle` from the same repositories; Pyannote segmentation-3.0 for segmentation, upstream MIT, plus WeSpeaker ResNet34-LM for speaker embeddings; both components are required); and `music` generation packs (`yue2-3b@audio-cpp-q8`: the YuE2 3B Q8 main model, F16 VAE, and config files from the `audio-cpp/Yue2-3B-GGUF` repository, about 4.6 GB; the entire repository is a single `music` component, and other quantizations in the repository are not downloaded; there is also a shared component, `audiocpp-engine` (the audio.cpp engine, see the next section); backend `audio-cpp`, listed for all platforms, run by the audio.cpp engine and not through the Model Worker; if the engine component is missing, the pack is reported as `not-installed` / `incomplete` and the engine is named; license CC-BY-NC-4.0, not for commercial use; Model Worker protocol spec §4.4). Synthesis is MLX only and is available only on macOS with Apple Silicon; on other platforms it is reported as `error` / `unsupported`. A pack's status carries its name and license (`label`, `license`). A pack consists of its own weights plus several shared components (for example, the VAD and aligner shared by several recognition models, the speech codec shared by the five Qwen3-TTS variants, and the IndexTTS2 repository borrowed by IndexTTS 2.5). Shared components are installed once and retained by reference count; when the last pack that uses one is deleted, it is reclaimed too. A pack with missing components is reported as incomplete (`not-installed`, `reason: 'incomplete'`, with `detail` naming the missing components); installation fetches only the missing parts and does not re-download the whole pack. Components shared by several packs that were added later (the aligner, speaker models) can be registered as **optional**: installation and repair still download them, but the pack still counts as installed without them (the component status carries `optional: true`); they are omitted when handed to the Worker, and an already-installed pack does not become incomplete because the registry now lists an extra optional component.

All five recognition packs carry an optional forced aligner (the MLX 4-bit pack of `Qwen3-ForcedAligner-0.6B`): if it is installed, local recognition word timings are aligned and the `local` provider reports native word-level timing; if it is not installed, the provider reports no word-level timing. MOSS aligns only lines longer than 5 seconds; even with the aligner installed, it reports no word-level timing; it has no channel for recognition hints, so it reports that hints are not accepted. A component may carry a different license from the main model: the aligner takes upstream's Apache-2.0; the speaker model (WeSpeaker ResNet34-LM, an optional component of the MOSS pack and a required component of the speaker-diarization pack, stored once when the same repository is used) takes upstream pyannote's CC-BY-4.0 and must credit WeSpeaker and pyannote.audio — the model card of the MLX conversion lists MIT, but upstream governs; the attribution (work `wespeaker-voxceleb-resnet34-LM`, source WeSpeaker and pyannote.audio, license, MLX format conversion) is written in the `summary` of the component license. The component status carries its own license (`license`); in the model details' "License" row, the UI lists the weights' license, and adds a separate row for each component that requires attribution (CC-BY) or whose license differs from the weights. The Apache-2.0 aligner license and the MIT license of the whisper.cpp GGML conversion require a license and NOTICE only when redistributed; CucCut does not redistribute weights (they are downloaded from upstream), and the aligner's repository has no NOTICE file, so neither needs an additional notice. A component may take only one subdirectory of a repository (`subdir`, for example when one repository holds several Whisper variants): installation, verification, and the installed check still operate on the whole repository, and what is handed to the Worker is that subdirectory and the files beneath it. References are computed from the install records in the model catalog (`.ccut-installs.json`): a component's holders are the packs that use the same repository and revision and that are either listed in the record or have all their files present; if the repository directory holds a different revision, it is not deleted.

**Audio engine component**. The second required component of the music-generation pack is the audio.cpp engine (`audiocpp-engine`, command `audiocpp_cli`, §6.5). Like the shared VAD, aligner, and Whisper tokenizer of the recognition models, it is downloaded with the pack: confirmed once, the displayed size already includes it, installed once, retained by reference count, and reclaimed along with the last pack that uses it. When the weights are present but the engine is not, the pack reports `not-installed`, `reason: 'incomplete'`, with `detail` naming this component; repair downloads only this component. There is no music-specific state. Its source is not a Hugging Face repository but the GitHub release page of audio.cpp (the component status and install plan carry `origin: 'github-release'`; without this field the source is Hugging Face; `repo` is `0xShug0/audio.cpp` and `revision` is the release tag `v0.9.1`).
