It is therefore a **platform-specific** component: the version is pinned (currently 0.9.1); the file name, size, and sha256 of each platform's archive are registered in `music-engine.ts` in `@cuccut/models` (each checked against the digests on the upstream release page); and the model catalog resolves which package to download from the host running the Runtime: macOS arm64 and x64 use the Metal build (about 30–32 MB); Windows and Linux x64 use the Vulkan portable build (about 62–72 MB, requiring a Vulkan GPU driver); other platforms have no package. On such hosts the pack reports `error` / `unsupported` (`estimatedBytes` is null, and `models.install` rejects with `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_SOURCE`), unless `CUCCUT_AUDIOCPP_CLI` is set and points to an existing file. **The environment variable override satisfies this component directly**: when it is set and the file exists, the component reports `installed` (`bytes: null`), the install plan is `keep` with nothing downloaded, and the `unsupported` for lacking a platform package is no longer reported; when it is set but the file does not exist, the component counts as missing and the pack is `incomplete`. Status looks only at this variable and the installed components, not at `PATH` (`PATH` is used only as a fallback when a task runs, see §6.5), so on a machine where the engine is only on `PATH`, the pack stays `incomplete` and is not routed to. License Apache-2.0, listed in the component licenses in the model details; it is not distributed with the app, so it is not listed in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. Download rules: verify size and sha256 (on mismatch, delete the bad file, `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY`); extract in-process into a staging directory, taking only regular files at the package root (the executable, dynamic libraries in the same directory, and licenses), skipping subdirectories, links, and entries whose names escape the directory; if extraction fails or no executable is found afterward, delete the staging directory and fail with `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY`, leaving no half-finished directory; set the executable bit (0755), atomically rename the whole thing into the version directory, then run `audiocpp_cli --version` once: the first line must parse as `audio.cpp <version>`; if it cannot be read, delete the just-published directory and the downloaded archive and fail with `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY` (a retry downloads again). Refused in strict offline mode (`OFFLINE_STRICT`). **Download address and size**: the archive address follows the tool download source (`tools.downloadEndpoint` setting or `CUCCUT_TOOLS_ENDPOINT`, §12.9): if a mirror is set it is `<base>/audiocpp/<version>/<file name>`, otherwise it is the registered official release address; in the install plan, the component's `source` is the mirror base or the release page. The size is the registered per-platform archive size; no HEAD probe is sent, so `estimatedBytes` and `confirmBytes` already include the package for this platform. **Staging and manifest**: the archive, the `.part` file, and the extracted `unpacked/` directory live in `<runtime-home>/tools/.ccut-staging/audiocpp@<version>/`; on cancel, the partial download is kept for resume. After publishing, the version directory gets a `.ccut-manifest.json` (the path, size, and sha256 of each extracted file), which is authoritative for checks, verification, and repair. Migration: a version directory that has an executable but no manifest (installed by the old external-tool path) counts as installed, and verification passes directly; repair re-downloads the archive. When the last pack that uses it is deleted, `tools/audiocpp/<version>/` is removed (and `tools/audiocpp/` too if it becomes empty), but `tools/` itself is left alone. Location: `<runtime-home>/tools/audiocpp/<version>/` (future audio.cpp-based packs share the same copy). This directory is where the component is stored; it is owned by the model catalog's component reference counting, not by `externalTools` (§12.9).

**Speaker diarization pack**. It has no check (`models.test` rejects with `unsupported`), and no separate job is submitted; only the `speakers` workflow (§6.6), which adds speaker diarization to an existing transcript, starts a Worker that installs only this pack. The recognition packs for Qwen3-ASR and Whisper register which one to use (Core ML Whisper uses the MLX one; GGML Whisper and candle recognition packs use the candle one; MOSS does its own diarization and does not use it). When its required components are all installed, the components handed to these recognition-pack Workers gain `segmentation` and `speaker`, and the Worker's resident-memory estimate counts them too; when they are not fully installed, they are omitted. After it is installed, an idle Worker of a recognition pack that uses it is unloaded first, and the next task reloads with the component included; a running task is not interrupted and is handled as if the component were absent. When it is deleted, if a recognition pack that uses it has a task in use (queued or in progress), the deletion is rejected with `MODEL_IN_USE`; idle Workers are unloaded before deletion. The `local` provider reports for each transcription model whether it can tell speakers apart (`TranscribeModelInfo.speakers`: `native` means it diarizes on its own, `pack` means the speaker-diarization pack is installed, `none` means it cannot); when transcription is not given `diarize`, this decides (§6.6). A transcription model registered with this pack also reports `diarizationPack` (its `bundleId`, whether installed or not), and the UI uses it to offer a download when the pack is missing.

**Install**. `models.install`, `models.cancelInstall`, `models.repair`, `models.remove`, and `models.test` manage local models (commands and protocol spec §4.1):

- **Size first, then confirm**. Without `confirmBytes`, only a plan is returned: for each component, whether it is downloaded or kept; the files to download and their byte counts (null if the size is unknown, with an estimate given separately); bytes already in staging; free space; and the source. No job is created. The caller passes back the `confirmBytes` from the plan unchanged to submit; if it does not match a new plan, it is rejected with `conflict` (`MODEL_INSTALL_SIZE_CHANGED`, with the new plan). If already installed, nothing is submitted; if the same pack is already being installed, that job is returned.
- **Installation is an ordinary job** (`kind: 'modelInstall'`, no Task, §7.9), queued in a serial queue: progress is in the `jobs` topic (`progress.unit: 'bytes'`), and the pack's status (`downloading` and progress) is in the `models` topic.
- **Source**. The base address is taken first from the environment variable `CUCCUT_MODELS_ENDPOINT`, then from the setting `models.downloadEndpoint` (§5.10); if neither is set, it is the public model hub `https://huggingface.co`. A file's address is `<base>/<owner>/<repo>/resolve/<revision>/<file path>`, URL-encoded segment by segment with `/` preserved; a mirror only needs to serve files at the same path. The base must be `http(s)://`, must not carry credentials, query parameters, or fragments, and download requests send no auth header.
  - **ModelScope**. If the base host is `modelscope.cn` or `www.modelscope.cn` (use `https://www.modelscope.cn`), paths follow ModelScope's layout: `<source>/models/<owner>/<repo>/resolve/master/<file path>`. Its commits do not share SHAs with the public hub, so it uses `master`; files are still verified one by one against the sha256 in the built-in manifest, and a mismatch is `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY`. Repositories renamed there are looked up through a rename table (`openai/whisper-large-v3` → `openai-mirror/whisper-large-v3`, `OpenMOSS-Team/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize` → `OpenMOSS/MOSS-Transcribe-Diarize`). Repositories absent there (the whisper.cpp GGML weights `ggerganov/whisper.cpp` and GPT-SoVITS's `PJMixers-Dev/lj1995_GPT-SoVITS-safetensors`) are downloaded from the public hub instead; the plan's `source` lists all bases used (`https://www.modelscope.cn + https://huggingface.co`), and the repository manifest's `source` records the actual base. ModelScope's HEAD does not give a size, so the plan instead sends a GET for only the first byte and reads the total length from `Content-Range`. The implementation is in `packages/models/src/download-source.ts`; ModelScope's base and per-host recognition (`MODELSCOPE_ENDPOINT`, `isModelScopeEndpoint`) are in `packages/protocol/src/models.ts`, and the settings page uses it to recognize the selected source. The repositories in the table and the sha256 values in the built-in manifest were each checked against ModelScope's file listings.
- **Download, verification, and publishing**. Following the built-in manifest (Model Worker protocol spec §4), each file is downloaded to the staging area `.ccut-staging/<owner>/<repo>@<revision>/` inside the model directory, and each file is checked for size and sha256. Once a component's files are all present, its manifest is written and the whole directory is atomically swapped into place. A component counts as installed only after it is published: if a failure occurs midway, components already published are kept and the next run fetches only what is missing. Optional components (the aligner, speaker models) that are missing do not stop the pack from counting as installed; reinstalling downloads only the missing ones (the UI calls this "top up"). Before starting, free space is checked against the bytes to be downloaded. After an install or repair completes, idle Workers of this pack that already have it loaded are unloaded first, and the next task reloads with the new files (newly added components take effect only at that point); running tasks are not interrupted.
- **Pause and resume**. Cancelling an install (`models.cancelInstall`, or cancelling its job) is a pause: the staging area is kept, and the pack reports `install.state: 'paused'`; reinstalling resumes from the break point with HTTP Range, and files already verified are not downloaded again. `discard: true` deletes the staging area. Connection failures, 5xx responses, and dropped streams are retried with backoff a limited number of times, and the count resets when progress was made before the disconnect; if a resumed file does not match, it is downloaded again from the start. If a resume request gets a 200 back (the source ignored Range), or if the partial data is no more than 64 MiB and the response is 5xx, that response is discarded and the file is downloaded from scratch without Range (ModelScope's small files return 200 for `bytes=n-` but send only a fragment, or return 502).
- **Failures**. Each error code carries a remedy (`details.remedy`): `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_NO_SPACE`, `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_NETWORK`, `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_INTEGRITY` (the bad file is deleted), `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_SOURCE` (the source does not have this file or denies access), and `MODEL_MANIFEST_INCOMPLETE` (the built-in manifest lacks a trusted hash; it will not download first and compute one afterward).
- **Repair** follows the same two steps as install, but reads each file's sha256 again, re-downloads only the bad and missing files, and reuses good files as they are.
- **Strict offline** (`offline.strict`): install and repair are rejected with `conflict` (`OFFLINE_STRICT`).
- **Removal**. If a job is using the pack (a queued or in-progress transcription, check, or install), the removal is rejected with `conflict` (`MODEL_IN_USE`); if the Worker is idle, it is unloaded before deletion. After deletion, defaults pointing to the pack for capabilities without a factory default are cleared (`transcribe` and `separateAudio` keep theirs, §6.8).

**Check**. Each installed pack can be checked once: a fixed sample bundled with the pack runs through the full Worker flow and the output is verified. A check is an ordinary job (`kind: 'modelTest'`), queued in the same queue as this pack's transcription; the result and time are recorded in the model status (`selfTest`). It proves the pack runs on this machine, not that its quality is good. For a transcription pack, the sample is an English recording; the result must contain a fixed phrase (English single-digit words and Arabic numerals are treated as equivalent, since Whisper renders numbers as digits). A speech pack synthesizes one fixed short sentence with the default voice; the output must decode, have a reasonable duration, not be silent, and not clip (criteria in Model Worker protocol spec §4.3); the synthesized WAV is kept as an artifact. A text-to-image pack paints one image from a fixed prompt at 256×256, 4 steps, and a fixed seed; the output must be a PNG of the correct size and not a single solid color; the PNG is kept as an artifact. A music pack starts the engine once with a fixed style, `instrumental`, no arrangement, an 8-second cap, a fixed seed, and 4 steps; the output is judged by the same criteria as synthesis, and the WAV is kept as an artifact (if the engine is not found, the pack is unavailable and the check is rejected with `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`). A separation pack splits the same sample used for transcription (vocals only) into two stems; both must decode and match the sample's length, and the vocals must not be silent and must be at least 6 dB louder than the background (Model Worker protocol spec §4.3); the vocals WAV is kept as an artifact. Only usable packs can be checked. A failed check yields a conclusion with a stable code (`MODEL_SELF_TEST_FAILED` in command protocol spec §11.3) and a fact line for technical details; the code is determined by the kind of Worker failure and by which verification step failed. Whether repair can help is determined only by the code (`modelCheckRepair` in `@cuccut/protocol`), so the UI and CLI do not each decide this on their own. A missing bundled sample or recording (`APP_FILE_MISSING`) is a problem with the CucCut installation itself; it is not recorded in `selfTest`, and repairing the pack will not help. The UI attaches wording only to recognized codes; unrecognized codes show as a generic failure, with the Runtime's original wording placed in the technical details.

**Model catalog**. The effective directory is taken first from the environment variable `CUCCUT_MODELS_DIR`, read when the Runtime starts, then from the setting `models.dir` (§5.10); if neither is set, it is `<runtime-home>/models`. `models.getDir` returns the path, its source (`env`, `setting`, `default`), the default path, whether it exists, whether it is writable, used and free space, the number of recognized packs, and any move in progress. `models.inspectDir` inspects a folder read-only: whether it exists, whether it is writable, whether it contains or is contained by the current directory, which models inside it are recognized by manifest (`<owner>/<repo>/.ccut-manifest.json`), the bytes a move would write, and whether it is on the same disk. `models.setDir` changes the directory (commands and protocol spec §4.1):

- **Two modes**. `switch` only changes the location: it writes the setting and changes the root of the model directory; models already in the new location become usable immediately, and the original directory is left untouched. `move` moves the recognized models from the current directory to the new one: it is a job (`kind: 'modelsMove'`, queue `models:dir`, `progress.unit: 'bytes'`). Only after the move completes and is verified does it write the setting and change the root, and then it deletes the moved files from the original directory. If there is nothing to move, it is equivalent to `switch`.
- **Only recognized items are moved**. Only the repositories and revisions used by registered packs (with a valid manifest) and the install records (`.ccut-installs.json`, merged into the target's record; for packs present in both, the original entry is kept) are moved. If the target already has the same revision, it is not moved again; a same-named repository at a different revision stays where it is. Partially downloaded staging content and files placed by other programs stay in the original directory.
- **Same disk**: each repository is moved by renaming it, one by one. **Across disks**: first copy into `.ccut-moving/` in the target, verify each file's size and sha256 against the manifest, and once complete, rename into place. Before starting, a log `.ccut-move.json` is written in the original directory.
- **Rollback**. On a mid-move failure, cancellation, or inability to write the setting, the move is undone: on the same disk, renames are reversed; across disks, the copies created are deleted. The setting and model directory remain unchanged, and the job fails with `MODELS_DIR_MOVE_FAILED` (or `MODELS_DIR_NO_SPACE` when the disk is full). If the Runtime exits in the middle of a move, the next startup rolls back according to the log. After switching, files in the original directory that cannot be deleted are kept, and the job carries the warning `MODELS_DIR_SOURCE_KEPT`.
- **Stop first, then change**. If any task is using local models (queued or in-progress tasks with a pack, and installs, checks, and moves), the change is rejected with `conflict` (`MODEL_IN_USE`, with `jobIds`); otherwise all idle Workers are unloaded, and if any are busy, the same rejection applies. During a move, every pack reports `error` / `relocating`; no new local tasks can be submitted, and installs, removals, and checks are rejected with `MODEL_IN_USE`. After the root changes, pack status is recomputed against the new directory; the `models` topic emits `bundle.updated` for each pack, and the capability view is recomputed.
- **Rejections**. If the directory is set by environment variable: `MODELS_DIR_ENV_LOCKED`; if the folder does not exist (an external drive not connected): `MODELS_DIR_MISSING`; if it is not writable: `MODELS_DIR_NOT_WRITABLE`; if it contains or is contained by the current directory: `MODELS_DIR_NESTED`; a cross-disk move without enough space: `MODELS_DIR_NO_SPACE` (with `requiredBytes`, `availableBytes`, and a remedy). All are `conflict`. If the effective directory is missing, installs and repairs are rejected with `MODELS_DIR_MISSING`, and no same-named directory is created elsewhere.
- **Entry points**. The model directory card at the top of the "Local models" section on each capability page in settings (product design §7.6), and the CLI `cuccut models dir [--set <path> --move|--switch | --reset]`. The Web service allowlist and the external MCP service include none of these three methods: changes move several GB of files and unload local Workers, so they are local-machine administration. The browser settings page does not show this card.

**Entry point**. When a model is missing, an agent can submit “download model” as a prerequisite step of a task (`models_install`): it first makes a plan and gets confirmation according to the `command` risk (§3.12). The confirmation states the size and source, and the result carries the size. The audio.cpp engine of the music model bundle (backend `audio-cpp`) is one of its components (§6.3); it is downloaded in the same confirmation and the same task, and the planned size and source already include it. If the machine has no engine bundle and `CUCCUT_AUDIOCPP_CLI` is not set, `models_install` is refused with `MODEL_DOWNLOAD_SOURCE`; in strict offline mode it is refused with `OFFLINE_STRICT`. Checking an installed model bundle (`models_test`) likewise requires confirmation per `command`, and `jobs_wait` is used to get the result. The MCP external service provides no install, check or delete; it can only use `models_list` to see status. The Web service allowlist also excludes install management (§4.8): downloading several hundred MB and deleting this machine's transcription capability is administration of this machine, while the browser sees only status and progress. `jobs.cancel` can stop an install (equivalent to pausing). CLI: `cuccut models install <bundleId> [--yes]` (shows the size first; only `--yes` skips confirmation), `models cancel`, `models repair`, `models test`, `models remove`. `models bundles` shows components, progress and the most recent check.

Models are reused by backend / device / model-version / compatibility; each video does not load its own copy. The resource budget is a soft constraint based on estimates and measurements; external programs may still occupy the GPU. When memory is insufficient, work is queued or safely unloaded; the quality of the final result must not be silently lowered during generation.

### 6.4 Online adapters and output contract

Online providers are all implemented in TypeScript. Each API provider has a set of adapters (one per capability), handling parameters, authentication, submission, polling, cancellation, result download and error normalization. They do not go through the Model Worker and do not occupy the local model queue; each provider has its own concurrency cap. Vendor input limits are handled by the adapter: audio exceeding a single request limit is sliced by the adapter, submitted piece by piece, and its times are merged back into the material clock. Slice boundaries fall at silence.

**API provider catalog**. Online providers are defined by the catalog below. The catalog exists only once in the Runtime: the provider registry, the settings UI and the prototypes all read the same table, and `providerId` must not get another alias (the old names `gemini`, `dashscope`, `zai`, `volcano` and `volcano-ark` are no longer used; Volcano's speech and Ark are merged into `volcengine`). “Catalog declaration” is the capability this provider opens and the UI may show; “implementation” is the subset the Runtime can actually call. Provider descriptions (§6.1) report only implemented capabilities: a capability marked P1 must not be reported as available before it is implemented, nor become a selectable default. Each provider's built-in model table (2–4 current flagship models, taken from official docs) is maintained with the catalog and is not listed one by one here. The adapter table below lists only models that have a dedicated adapter.

| `providerId` | Display name | `kind` | Catalog-declared capabilities | Implementation | Base URL (international / China) | Auth | Icon |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `openai` | OpenAI | `vendor` | `generateText`、`transcribe`、`synthesizeSpeech`、`generateImage` | All | `https://api.openai.com/v1` | Bearer | `openai` (monochrome) |
| `anthropic` | Anthropic | `vendor` | `generateText` | All (native Messages adapter) | `https://api.anthropic.com` | `x-api-key` and `anthropic-version` | `anthropic` (monochrome) |
| `google` | Google Gemini | `vendor` | `generateText`、`transcribe`、`generateImage` | All | `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta` | `x-goog-api-key` | `gemini-color` |
| `elevenlabs` | ElevenLabs | `vendor` | `synthesizeSpeech` (including voice cloning) | All | `https://api.elevenlabs.io/v1` | `xi-api-key` | `elevenlabs` (initial-letter avatar if missing) |
| `deepseek` | DeepSeek | `vendor` | `generateText` | All (compatible) | `https://api.deepseek.com/v1` | Bearer | `deepseek-color` |
| `moonshot` | Kimi（Moonshot） | `vendor` | `generateText` | All (compatible) | `https://api.moonshot.ai/v1` / `https://api.moonshot.cn/v1` | Bearer | `kimi` (monochrome) |
| `qwen` | 阿里云百炼（Qwen） | `vendor` | `generateText`; `synthesizeSpeech` and `transcribe` are P1 | `generateText` (compatible) | `https://dashscope-intl.aliyuncs.com/compatible-mode/v1` / `https://dashscope.aliyuncs.com/compatible-mode/v1` | Bearer | `qwen-color` |
| `zhipu` | 智谱 GLM（Z.ai） | `vendor` | `generateText` | All (compatible) | `https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4` / `https://open.bigmodel.cn/api/paas/v4` | Bearer | `zhipu-color` |
| `minimax` | MiniMax | `vendor` | `generateText`; `synthesizeSpeech` is P1 | `generateText` (compatible) | `https://api.minimax.io/v1` / `https://api.minimaxi.com/v1` | Bearer | `minimax-color` |
| `volcengine` | 火山引擎（豆包） | `vendor` | `generateText`; `synthesizeSpeech` and `generateImage` are P1 | `generateText` (compatible, Ark) | `https://ark.cn-beijing.volces.com/api/v3` | Bearer | `volcengine-color` |
| `xai` | xAI（Grok） | `vendor` | `generateText`; `generateImage` is P1 | `generateText` (compatible) | `https://api.x.ai/v1` | Bearer | `xai` (monochrome) |
| `mistral` | Mistral | `vendor` | `generateText` | All (compatible) | `https://api.mistral.ai/v1` | Bearer | `mistral-color` |
| `groq` | Groq | `vendor` | `generateText`; `transcribe` is P1 | `generateText` (compatible) | `https://api.groq.com/openai/v1` | Bearer | `groq` (monochrome) |
| `openrouter` | OpenRouter | `relay` | `generateText` | All (compatible) | `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` | Bearer | `openrouter` (monochrome) |
| `siliconflow` | 硅基流动（SiliconFlow） | `relay` | `generateText`; `synthesizeSpeech` and `transcribe` are P1 | `generateText` (compatible) | `https://api.siliconflow.cn/v1` | Bearer | `siliconcloud-color` |
| `custom:<名字>` | user-chosen name | `custom` | user-declared | user-declared (compatible) | user-provided | Bearer, key optional | initial-letter avatar |
| `agent:codex` | Codex | `agent` | `generateImage` | All (§6.9) | — | no key | `codex-color` |

- **`kind`** is `vendor` (model vendor), `relay` (relay platform), `custom` (self-hosted endpoint) or `agent` (agent, §6.9). An `agent` provider has no account, is not in the API provider list, and appears only in a capability's model selection.
- **One adapter set per provider.** Text providers marked “compatible” in the table share one OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions adapter (the same implementation as `custom:<名字>`), differing only in base URL and built-in model table. Anthropic uses its own Messages adapter. `custom:<名字>` works as before: the user gives an address, key and model name to connect another vendor or a self-hosted service, the capability description is user-declared, and it is treated at the `declared` layer (§3.6). Voice cloning (§5.9) connects only to ElevenLabs; `separateAudio` has no online adapter (§6.1).
- **Base URLs.** Providers with both international and China base URLs use the one matching the account's `region` (`global` or `cn`, §6.8). Both the provider and the account may rewrite the base URL; the account's rewrite takes priority, then the provider's, and finally the catalog's preset.
- **Icons** are taken from lobe-icons (MIT), renamed by `providerId` and distributed with the UI. The license and manifest are in the repository's `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. Each vendor's logo is used only to identify its API provider. Providers without an icon get an initial-letter avatar.

A `JobSpec` fixes an `outputContract`, for example language, sample rate, channels, alignment quality, required entity IDs and image dimensions. Returned format, bytes or duration that fail validation cannot be registered as a ready artifact.

**Outbound data and credentials**. Online calls send material or text off this machine. The first time a given type of data is sent to a provider requires authorization (§12.5), and paid calls pass budget admission (§7.8). An API key appears only in request headers sent to that provider's endpoint: it is not written to logs, task records, events or error details, and it is not passed to arbitrary redirect addresses when results are downloaded.

Source records keep provider / model / version / input hash / output hash, the seed where available, and a cost status of `estimate`, `reported` or `unknown`. Without a trustworthy report, no falsely precise actual payment amount is written. In a transcription result, cost is `reported` only if every request reported usage (`usage` is the vendor's usage as returned); if any chunk did not report, it is `unknown`.

**Requests and errors**. All online requests go through the same HTTP layer. Each request has a deadline (estimated from audio duration). Connection failures, timeouts and 5xx responses are retried with backoff, 3 attempts in total; if it still fails, the error is `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`. 4xx responses are not retried: 401/403 is `PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED`, 429 is `PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, and the rest are `PROVIDER_REJECTED` (command and protocol spec §11.3). Redirects are followed manually, at most 3 hops. Auth headers are sent only to the origin of the original request and are dropped on cross-origin redirects. Requests with a body do not follow cross-origin redirects, so material is never forwarded elsewhere. Vendor error bodies have the key removed and are truncated before being written into error descriptions. Cancellation aborts in-flight requests immediately. Each online provider defaults to concurrency 2; custom endpoints count separately. Text requests are also rate-limited per provider, with the cap set by the `generateText` capability parameter (default 4, §6.8).

When a response carries `Retry-After`, the runtime waits for that long (at most 30 seconds). For 5xx it replaces backoff. For 429, it retries after waiting only if the value is 30 seconds or less (counted against the same 3 attempts); if it is absent or longer, the request fails immediately. Quota exhaustion (OpenAI's `insufficient_quota`) is never retried. On a 429 failure, `details.retryAfterSec` records the vendor's stated seconds. `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` carries `details.reason`: `unreachable`, `timeout` or `server-error`. Text adapters subdivide 4xx further: context too long is `INPUT_TOO_LONG` (`details.reason: 'context-length'`) and is not retried. `PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` has `details.reason` of `rate-limited` or `insufficient-quota`, and `PROVIDER_REJECTED` has `content-filter` or `model-not-found`.

**Audio preparation for transcription**. Online transcription in the Runtime uses ffmpeg to decode the selected audio track into a 16 kHz mono master (only the same demuxer allowlist as the Worker; if a range was requested, only that segment is decoded). If there is no audio track, the result is directly `no-audio-track` and no provider is queried. When the master exceeds the model's single-request limit (bytes or duration, whichever is tighter, with a 10% margin), silencedetect finds silences and the master is cut at the silence midpoint that is latest within the second half of each chunk. If no silence is found, it is hard-cut. Each chunk is encoded in the format the adapter needs (OpenAI uses AAC/M4A, compatible endpoints use WAV, Google uses FLAC) and submitted in order. Progress is reported in seconds processed. Slices are encoded from the master on demand and deleted right after submission. The master and slices live in the task's staging and are deleted when the attempt ends. Uploads use a file stream (multipart) and do not read the whole material into memory. Google's request body requires inline base64, so only the current chunk (not exceeding 12 MB) is buffered.

**Transcription adapters in the first batch**.

| Provider | Endpoint | Models | Word timing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `openai` | `POST {base}/audio/transcriptions` (multipart, Bearer) | `whisper-1` (default), `gpt-4o-transcribe`, `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` | `whisper-1` takes segment and word timings from `verbose_json` (`provider`); the other two return only text, are chunked at 120 seconds, and word timings are interpolated (`estimated`) |
| `google` | Gemini Interactions API: `POST {base}/interactions` (`x-goog-api-key` header, never in the URL) | `gemini-3.5-transcribe` (default, word-level timestamp annotation), `gemini-3.8-flash` (general model, returns segments per JSON Schema) | former `provider`, latter `estimated` |
| `custom:<名字>` | user-given OpenAI-compatible base URL; key optional | user-declared (`declared`), limits take conservative defaults | requests declared `native` use `verbose_json`, otherwise `estimated` |

**Generation requests**. One attempt of `synthesizeSpeech` or `generateImage` is one synchronous request (for images, one request per image count), with a 5-minute deadline. The response body is read by bytes, up to 64 MB. All parameter checks happen at submission (§6.2). Text or prompt is counted in Unicode code points; exceeding the model limit is rejected outright, not truncated, chunked or rewritten. A voice, language, format, size, count, speaking rate, `instructions` or `seed` that is not in the model description is rejected. The adapter converts frozen parameters into the vendor's format and writes output into staging per the requested format (`output-<n>.<extension>`), declaring media type, length and sha256. A response whose type or count differs from the request is a protocol error. A vendor that returns only text and no image is `PROVIDER_REJECTED`. Only bytes in the response body are trusted; addresses given by the response are not downloaded.

**Generation adapters in the first batch**.

| Provider | Capability and endpoint | Models | Key points |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `openai` | `synthesizeSpeech`: `POST {base}/audio/speech` (JSON, Bearer), response body is audio | `gpt-4o-mini-tts` (default, 13 voices, default `marin`, accepts `instructions` and account voice `{ id }`), `tts-1-hd`, `tts-1` (9 voices, default `alloy`) | 4096 characters; mp3, wav, flac; speaking rate 0.25–4; no seed |
| `openai` | `generateImage`: `POST {base}/images/generations`, reads `data[].b64_json` | `gpt-image-2` (default; also 16:9, 9:16 and 2K sizes), `gpt-image-1.5`, `gpt-image-1`, `gpt-image-1-mini` | sizes 1024x1024, 1536x1024, 1024x1536; if not given, the vendor decides; 1–10 images per request; png, jpeg, webp; prompt 32000 characters; no seed |
| `elevenlabs` | `synthesizeSpeech`: `POST {base}/text-to-speech/{voice_id}?output_format=…` (`xi-api-key` header) | `eleven_multilingual_v2` (default), `eleven_v4`, `eleven_v3`, `eleven_flash_v2_5` | voices belong to the account; there are no preset or default voices, so one must be given; character limits by model (10000, 10000, 5000, 40000); mp3 (`mp3_44100_128`), wav (`wav_24000`); `language_code` is sent only to models that accept it; accepts seed |
| `google` | `generateImage`: Gemini Interactions API `POST {base}/interactions`, `response_format.type: 'image'` | `gemini-3.1-flash-image` (default), `gemini-3-pro-image`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite-image`, `gemini-2.5-flash-image` | sizes come from each model's aspect ratio × resolution table and are sent as `aspect_ratio` and `image_size`; one image per request, up to 4 per task; only the last image in `model_output` is taken, not intermediate `thought` images; png, jpeg; no seed |
| `custom:<名字>` | same two endpoints as `openai`; image requests add `response_format: 'b64_json'` | user declares models with `capability` `synthesizeSpeech` / `generateImage` | limits follow the declaration; undeclared limits take conservative values: speech 4096 characters, mp3; if voices are declared, the first is the default and other voice IDs are passed to the endpoint as-is; if none are declared, a voice must be given; image 1024x1024, 1 image per request, png, prompt 4000 characters |

**Voice cloning requests**. ElevenLabs instant cloning is `POST {base}/voices/add` (multipart: `name`, one reference recording `files`, optional `description`; `xi-api-key` header), and the response's `voice_id` is taken. It is sent only once, since resending may create an extra clone, with a 2-minute deadline. Deletion is `DELETE {base}/voices/{voice_id}`; a 404 is treated as already gone on the remote side. Details not verified offline are listed in §14.

**Generated output contract**. Before publishing, each output is validated: the file is in staging, its length and sha256 match the declaration, the media type is the requested format, and the type recognized from the file header matches it. Then ffprobe fully decodes it once with that format's demuxer: audio must have a positive duration, sample rate and channel count; images must have positive width and height. If any check fails, the whole task fails as `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID`. It is not published and not retried, and the raw file is moved to `logs/diagnostics/<jobId>/` (§6.5). Media facts from validation are written into each output of the result. Outputs with identical content are the same Artifact.

**Text requests**. One attempt of `generateText` is one synchronous request with a 10-minute deadline. The request is an ordered list of messages (`system`, `user`, `assistant`; passed through as-is for Chat Completions, while Gemini moves `system` into system instructions), an output format (`text`, or `json` plus a JSON Schema whose root is an object), `maxOutputTokens` (if not given, the model's limit), optional reasoning effort, `temperature` and `seed`. Checks at submission: there must be at least one non-empty `user` or `assistant` message. Input that clearly exceeds the context by a character-based estimate is rejected as `INPUT_TOO_LONG` (the exact judgment is made by the vendor). `maxOutputTokens` above the model limit, a `temperature` or `seed` the model does not accept, structured output the model does not support, or an invalid schema are all `invalid-request`. Reasoning effort has four levels: `minimal`, `low`, `medium` and `high`. When the model does not support the requested level, the nearest level is used (on a tie, the higher one); if the model cannot adjust reasoning, it is ignored. Both cases are written into the result's `notes`.

**Text adapters in the first batch**.

| Provider | Endpoint | Models | Key points |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `openai` | Chat Completions: `POST {base}/chat/completions` (JSON, Bearer) | `gpt-6.1-sol` (default), `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-luna` | output cap uses `max_completion_tokens`; reasoning effort is `reasoning_effort`; structured output sends `json_schema` with `strict: false` (strict mode accepts only a schema subset, so validation is done by the Runtime); does not accept `temperature` or `seed` |
| `google` | `POST {base}/models/{model}:generateContent` (`x-goog-api-key` header) | `gemini-3.8-flash` (default), `gemini-3.7-flash`, `gemini-3.6-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` | system message goes into `systemInstruction`; structured output uses `responseMimeType: 'application/json'` with `responseJsonSchema`; reasoning effort is `thinkingConfig.thinkingLevel`; the `thought` part is not taken; accepts `temperature` and `seed` |
| `anthropic` | Messages: `POST {base}/v1/messages` (`x-api-key` and `anthropic-version` headers) | the catalog's built-in model table | system message goes into top-level `system`; output cap `max_tokens` must be given (if not, the model's limit); structured output and reasoning effort are declared per the model description, and unsupported ones are rejected or adjusted by the rules above |
| Compatible text providers (`deepseek`, `moonshot`, `qwen`, `zhipu`, `minimax`, `volcengine`, `xai`, `mistral`, `groq`, `openrouter`, `siliconflow`) | same endpoint as `custom:<名字>`, base URL from the catalog | the catalog's built-in model table | limits follow the built-in model table; structured output, `temperature`, `seed` and reasoning effort are sent only when the model description declares support |
| `custom:<名字>` | same endpoint as `openai`, output cap uses `max_tokens` | user declares models with `capability` `generateText` | limits follow the declaration; undeclared limits take conservative values: context 32768, 4096 tokens per output |

**Text output contract**. The executor writes the full text to staging (`output-1.txt` or `output-1.json`), declaring length and sha256. Finish reasons are normalized to `stop`, `length` or `content-filter`. Content filtering is `PROVIDER_REJECTED` (`content-filter`) and is not published. `length` is not a complete success: the text is still published, with an `output-truncated` warning; structured output that hits the cap always fails. Structured output is validated against the schema before publishing. Output that is not JSON, or does not match the schema, is `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID` (`details.reason` is `schema`, `length` or `empty`, and `details.problems` lists the first few), and it is not retried. Before publishing, JobManager re-checks the digest and length and decodes strictly as UTF-8. Structured output gets its schema validated once more. The product is `.txt` (`text/plain`) or `.json` (`application/json`). The result also carries a preview of the first 2000 code points, usage, model and version, finish reason and `notes`.

Base URLs can all be rewritten (proxies, gateways). Tests use a local fake service and never connect to real providers.

### 6.5 Model Worker (local inference process)

**Granularity.** One Model Worker process hosts one **resident model package**: a primary model (a build of one model version for one backend) together with its fixed dependencies (VAD, forced aligner, speaker embedding). At most one process runs per model package on a given machine; each different model package gets its own process. There is no per-device or per-video/per-job process. Crashes in the inference backend's device context (Metal, CoreML, CUDA) are process-level. Isolating by model package means a single SIGABRT loses only one model, does not clear the other capabilities, and does not take down the Engine Host or the Runtime. No video code runs inside the Worker.

**Code layering and backends.** All local inference shares one library crate, `model-runtime`, and one binary crate, `model-worker`. We do not write a separate process program for each capability. The library holds the capability-independent parts: the IPC protocol and message types, parent-process supervision and exit, weight loading and manifest validation, the backend abstraction (device context and tensor runtime), audio decoding and resampling, the conversion from seconds to ticks, and writing segmented results. Capabilities such as `transcribe`, `align` and `synthesize` each implement only their own model and pipeline. Shared submodels such as VAD, the aligner and speaker embedding are modules in the library, and they load only when a model package declares them as dependencies. Backends are split by Cargo feature: `backend-mlx` (Apple Silicon only); `backend-candle` (CPU on all platforms), with `cuda` adding CUDA on top of it; `backend-coreml` (macOS on Apple Silicon only), for the directory-loaded Core ML models (Whisper), whose device is recorded as `ane`. The recognition pipeline's VAD still runs on MLX, so it depends on `backend-mlx`. `whisper-ggml` (all platforms) is for the single-file GGML weights of whisper.cpp (Whisper); its VAD and aligner use candle, so it depends on `backend-candle`. `whisper-ggml-cuda` adds ggml's CUDA backend on top of it, and `whisper-ggml-vulkan` adds the Vulkan backend; both can be enabled together, and at runtime CUDA takes priority. Building them requires CMake and a C++ compiler (CUDA also needs the CUDA Toolkit, Vulkan also needs the Vulkan SDK). `cuda` (candle) and `whisper-ggml-cuda` (Whisper) are two independent features. `model-worker` enables only MLX and Core ML by default. Builds for Windows, Linux and Intel Mac use `--no-default-features --features backend-candle,whisper-ggml`; add `cuda,whisper-ggml-cuda` when an NVIDIA GPU is present, and `whisper-ggml-vulkan` when AMD or Intel GPUs should run Whisper on Vulkan. `npm run build:engine` picks the right set for each platform, and `CUCCUT_WORKER_FEATURES` appends features. The model catalog records the backends each model package supports, and the Runtime chooses one per platform. Every backend must compile on every platform; it is simply unavailable where the matching hardware is absent.

**Backend matrix.** The same model on different backends is a different model package (`qwen3-asr-0.6b@mlx-4bit` and `qwen3-asr-0.6b@candle`). They share the repository, component and human-facing name. Whisper's weights for Core ML and for GGML are different conversions, so their repositories differ. On Apple Silicon, Whisper also has MLX model packages (`whisper-large-v3@mlx` and `whisper-large-v3-turbo@mlx`, the `whisper-mlx` family). These read mlx-community's fp16 conversions and share the tokenizer with Core ML. The Worker can load them, but they are not listed for now: whether to keep Core ML is undecided (§14, "Whisper backend on Apple Silicon"). The Runtime lists only one backend per platform. The default transcription model package also depends on the platform, and both are MOSS Transcribe (it identifies speakers itself and accepts no recognition hints). If it is not installed, the local Provider's default model falls back to the first installed recognition model package:

| Platform | Listed model packages | Device | Default transcription model package |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| macOS (Apple Silicon) | MLX: Qwen3-ASR 0.6B / 1.7B, MOSS, speech synthesis; Core ML: Whisper | `metal`; `ane` | `moss-transcribe-diarize@mlx-8bit` |
| Windows, Linux, Intel Mac | candle: Qwen3-ASR 0.6B / 1.7B, MOSS (all with an optional aligner, MOSS also with an optional speaker model), speech synthesis; GGML: Whisper large-v3 / large-v3 Turbo (with an optional aligner) | `cuda` or `cpu`; `cuda`, `vulkan`, `metal` or `cpu` | `moss-transcribe-diarize@candle` |

candle reads the same quantized weights as MLX and dequantizes them at load time: on CPU it computes in f32, and on CUDA it computes in the weights' half precision. Silero VAD always runs on CPU. GGML Whisper loads the quantized weights as they are (whisper.cpp), and its VAD and aligner run on candle's CPU. In `worker.hello` the Worker reports its devices by backend, with the preferred one first. The device that candle and GGML model packages register is `cpu`. The Runtime passes the Worker's preferred device to `model.load` and shows it on the model package's status. On Windows the installer comes in three variants by graphics card (§14, "Windows installer"). NVIDIA (Ampere / RTX 30 and newer) uses the CUDA installer, and both candle and GGML Whisper run on CUDA (`cuda` plus `whisper-ggml-cuda`). AMD and Intel GPUs, and NVIDIA GPUs that do not support CUDA 13, use the Vulkan installer: Whisper runs on Vulkan (`whisper-ggml-vulkan`), while candle and the other models run on CPU. Where neither applies, the standard build is used and everything runs on CPU. Choosing between CUDA and Vulkan at runtime within a single GPU installer is not possible yet (§14, "Single GPU installer chooses its backend at runtime"). The GGML device rule at runtime has only one clause: take the first GPU device that ggml enumerates, which is the same device whisper.cpp uses. ggml registers CUDA before Vulkan, so when both backends are compiled in, an NVIDIA machine selects CUDA, and a machine without an NVIDIA driver (where CUDA reports 0 devices) selects Vulkan. The device name reports `cuda`, `vulkan` or `metal` according to the GPU's backend, and `model.load` accepts only `cpu` and that one device. CUDA and Vulkan for Whisper have not yet been tested on real hardware (§14). The environment variable `CUCCUT_GPU` set to `off`, `0`, `false`, `cpu` or `no` stops the Worker from initializing the GPU (candle and GGML are both forced to CPU). If CUDA or Vulkan initialization fails, the Worker falls back to CPU and explains this once on stderr. If GGML was built with a GPU backend but no GPU is enumerated, the message names the backend that was compiled in. CUDA out-of-memory errors include a hint to use a smaller model or set `CUCCUT_GPU=off`. On Apple Silicon, candle and GGML still compile and run (the comparison tests use candle), but they are not listed. The six speech synthesis engine families sit behind the `synthesize::tensor` facade (the same adapter layer as v2). Builds for Apple Silicon with MLX use MLX, and other builds with `backend-candle` use candle. A build compiles only one of them. So when candle is also compiled in on Apple Silicon, a candle synthesis model package is rejected at `model.load` (`engine-unavailable`). candle's synthesis pins its tensors to the process's preferred device, and a model package whose device is not the preferred device is rejected in the same way (`unsupported-device`). On CPU, synthesis converts both float and quantized weights to f32. On CUDA, float weights keep the checkpoint's precision, and quantized weights are dequantized to the precision of their scale factors (half precision). System calls such as memory-pressure signals and process priority are concentrated in one platform module, and non-macOS platforms get conservative implementations first. Weights are not shared across processes. If the ASR and TTS model packages depend on the same auxiliary model, each loads its own copy. Resource sharing happens only at the lease layer of `ResourceScheduler`.

**Intel Mac local package.** `package:mac -- --local --target x86_64-apple-darwin` builds only on a local Intel Mac. It turns off MLX and Core ML, and enables candle CPU plus `whisper-ggml-metal`. Whisper's preferred GPU is Metal (on a usable AMD graphics card), while VAD, the aligner and the other models stay on CPU. Without a compatible GPU, or with `CUCCUT_GPU=off`, Whisper falls back to CPU. The Metal kernels are embedded in the Worker. The product is ad-hoc signed, not notarized, and does not produce a public update feed. The official Mac release is still Apple Silicon. For the build entry point, see the [desktop README](../../apps/desktop/README.md#intel-mac-local-build). On startup, the Intel Worker re-executes itself with the same PID and sets `GGML_METAL_CONCURRENCY_DISABLE=1` before starting any threads. This avoids a computation error in the current ggml concurrent Metal dispatch on discrete GPUs without unified memory. Chinese and English transcription was tested on a Radeon Pro 5500M with Whisper large-v3 Turbo q8_0. Other GPUs and models have not been tested.

**ggml CPU instruction-set baseline.** `whisper-rs-sys` 0.15 statically compiles whisper.cpp into the Model Worker with CMake. ggml's `GGML_NATIVE` defaults to ON (it is OFF only when cross-compiling or when `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is set). On MSVC, `FindSIMD.cmake` actually runs a probe program on the build machine. If it detects AVX-512, it adds `/arch:AVX512` to the whole ggml-cpu target, and if it detects AVX2, it adds `/arch:AVX2`. On GCC and Clang, the flag is `-march=native`. The installer is built on CI runners. When a runner's CPU is newer than the user's (often with AVX-512), the ggml-cpu code crashes with an illegal instruction on the user's machine, and ggml has no runtime instruction-set check here. So the packaging script sets `GGML_NATIVE=OFF` and `GGML_AVX2=ON` for all three variants (`whisper-rs-sys` passes `GGML_*` environment variables to CMake), so ggml-cpu is compiled to a fixed baseline. On MSVC that is `/arch:AVX2` plus FMA, F16C and BMI2, roughly Intel Haswell (2013) and AMD Zen and later. Setting `GGML_AVX2` explicitly avoids depending on ggml's default derivation (when `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is set, it falls back to SSE2). The trade-off: machines with AVX-512 lose the AVX-512 kernels, so Whisper is somewhat slower on CPU. CPUs without AVX2 (Sandy Bridge and Ivy Bridge, and many Pentium, Celeron and Atom parts) still crash. Covering them would require lowering the baseline to `GGML_AVX2=OFF` (`/arch:AVX`) or lower, which slows everyone down, so we are not doing that now. The `GGML_CPU_ALL_VARIANTS` option, which selects a kernel per CPU at runtime, requires `GGML_BACKEND_DL`, which in turn requires `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS`. But `whisper-rs-sys` hard-codes `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF` and statically links `ggml-cpu`, and environment variables cannot change that. Using it would mean modifying `whisper-rs-sys` and shipping each variant's `ggml-cpu-*.dll` with the package. `whisper-rs-sys` does not re-run its build script when `GGML_*` environment variables change, so switching the baseline requires clearing its build output (the CI cache key includes the baseline). The Rust code does not set `target-cpu`; it is compiled to rustc's default x86-64 baseline, so it is unaffected. The development build from `npm run build:engine` is still native. The above was checked against whisper-rs-sys's build.rs and ggml's CMakeLists, but it is not yet verified: a Windows build with this set of variables, confirmation that the build log shows `/arch:AVX2`, running on a machine that has only AVX2, and whether the workflow's actual arguments match. To verify, inspect the CMake-generated files under `target/<triple>/release/build/whisper-rs-sys-*/out/build/`. In `CMakeCache.txt`, `GGML_NATIVE` should be OFF and `GGML_AVX2` ON. The compile flags for ggml-cpu should be `/arch:AVX2` (with the Visual Studio generator, this is `EnableEnhancedInstructionSet` in `ggml-cpu.vcxproj`), and there should be no `/arch:AVX512`. The verbose output enabled by build.rs goes to the `output` file one level up in the same directory, and cargo does not print it to the console unless `-vv` is passed. Linux does not have a packaging flow yet. When it gets one, it will need the same set of variables (on GCC, `-mavx2 -mfma -mf16c -mbmi2`, and so on).

**MLX build.** `backend-mlx` uses `pmetal-mlx-rs` and `pmetal-mlx-sys`. The whole workspace uses one version of them and links a single libmlx. The deployment target, macOS 14.0, is set centrally by `.cargo/config.toml`. During its build, `pmetal-mlx-sys` calls the Metal compiler (`xcrun -sdk macosx metal`) to produce `mlx.metallib`, and it expands the source preamble used by runtime JIT kernels. On the build machine, `xcode-select` must point to a full Xcode (the Command Line Tools do not include the Metal compiler), and that Xcode's Metal Toolchain component must be installed (`xcrun -sdk macosx metal --version` must run). Xcode 26 and Xcode 27 are both built as upstream ships them, with no patches to MLX. The preamble script does not check whether the compiler succeeded. When `metal` is unavailable, the build still passes, but the resulting library fails when it compiles JIT kernels at runtime (for example, `unknown type name 'bfloat16_t'`). CMake also treats these preambles as up to date, so they are not regenerated after the toolchain is installed. Therefore, after switching Xcode, changing `xcode-select`, or installing the Metal Toolchain, run `cargo clean -p pmetal-mlx-sys` in every target directory that was used before, and then do a fresh build.

**Source.** The local inference implementation was ported from v2 as is (speech recognition, forced alignment, speaker diarization, vocal separation, speech synthesis, text-to-image), together with its offline-runnable tests, and it was not rewritten. v3 changes only the boundaries: one model package per process, the Worker reads only the files listed in the manifest (the engine does not scan model directories), and manifests and hashes are managed on the Node side (§6.3). Online Providers are all still in TypeScript (§6.4). The cloud Rust code from v2 serves only as a reference. The batching plan is in §14.

**audio.cpp engine (not a Model Worker).** The weights for local music generation (§6.1) are GGUF, read by the external audio.cpp engine `audiocpp_cli`. They are not compiled into `model-runtime`. For each job, the local Provider starts one engine process through `process-host`, with `cwd` set to the job's staging directory. The process exits when it finishes. It is not resident, and it does not take part in the Worker's idle or unload logic. The engine is not bundled with the app, and it is not a managed external tool. It is instead the shared component `audiocpp-engine` of the music model package (§6.3), and the package downloads a pinned precompiled build from the upstream release page. The binary is located in this order, re-checked for each job, so an install takes effect without a restart: `CUCCUT_AUDIOCPP_CLI` (a developer override; when set, only it is used), then the installed component (`<runtime-home>/tools/audiocpp/<registered version>/audiocpp_cli`), then `PATH`. `PATH` is only a fallback at job execution time. The model package status and capability view recognize only the override variable and the component. So if the engine exists only on `PATH`, the model package remains `incomplete` and is not routed to. When the engine component is missing but the weights are present, the model package is `not-installed` or `incomplete`. The engine selects its device at build time (Metal on macOS; the downloaded packages on Windows and Linux use Vulkan; self-built ones can use CUDA or CPU), and `CUCCUT_AUDIOCPP_BACKEND` can specify one. The model package registers its `device` as `auto`. Cancellation ends the whole process group (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after a grace period; on Windows the entire process tree is terminated). The command line, the output contract and the failure classification are in §4.4 of the Model Worker protocol specification.

**Startup.** The Worker is launched on demand by `process-host`. It is not warmed up when the Runtime starts.

1. The `models` module on the Node side resolves the model package: the explicit weight file path, the manifest hash, the backend, the device, and the Worker contract version. The Worker does not scan directories itself, does not go online, and does not download anything.
2. It starts over the same stdio JSON-line channel as the Engine Host. The message shapes are in the Model Worker protocol specification. The Runtime's first request is `worker.hello`. The reply gives the Worker version, contract version, available backends and capabilities. If the contract versions do not match, the Worker is closed immediately. The handshake waits 5 seconds by default. On Intel Mac it waits 60 seconds, to allow time for compiling the embedded Metal kernels the first time (the cache may miss when installing to a new path). If it times out, the process is still terminated and reported as a load failure. A synthesis model package requires the Worker to declare `synthesize`, text-to-image requires `image` (with the model family listed in `imageFamilies`), and separation requires `separate` (with the model family listed in `separateFamilies`). If these are missing, the model is not loaded, the job fails with `MODEL_LOAD_FAILED` (`unsupported`), and the model package is not disabled.
3. Loading weights is an explicit `model.load` request, not part of startup. Loading reports only its stages (`loading-weights`, `compiling`, `warming-up`) and does not fabricate a percentage.
4. The mapping to the §6.3 states: process not started or weights not loaded = `installed`; `model.load` in progress = `loading`; load complete = `ready`; a job running = `busy`; unloading = `unloading`.

**Execution.** The Worker runs only one job at a time. Queueing, fairness and the depth limit are all handled by the Runtime's JobManager and `ResourceScheduler` (§7.6, §7.7). The Worker keeps no second job ledger. A job request carries `jobId`, `runGeneration`, frozen parameters, input paths and the expected output contract. The Worker reports stages, progress, segmented results and warnings as an event stream, and finishes with a single `job.result` that gives the path, sha256 and length of the staging file. The Runtime validates these before publishing (§7.3). Local synthesis and local image generation share the same process management and the same model package queue. Their outputs (`speech.wav` and `image.png` in staging) are validated and published under the rules for generation jobs (§6.4, Model Worker protocol specification §5.1). Local separation works the same way, and its outputs are `vocals.wav` and `background.wav` in staging (protocol specification §2.5.4), which are validated by the dubbing flow or by checks.

**Priority and resources.** The Worker runs at a scheduling priority below the foreground. On macOS it uses utility QoS; on Windows the inference thread uses `THREAD_PRIORITY_BELOW_NORMAL`. The Runtime passes the thread count in from the admission result. Each Worker's resident memory and GPU memory is one lease (§7.7). For recognition and separation model packages, the post-load resident size is estimated from the components the job will load (the required components plus any optional components already installed). For MLX and Core ML, it is the weights' stored bytes. Separation model packages store their weights as 16-bit and upcast them to 32-bit at load, so they are counted at twice the stored bytes. HTDemucs-FT measured 673 MB resident for a 336 MB file. GPU memory is that estimate plus 1 GiB, with a minimum of 2 GiB. Process memory is 1 GiB. candle converts the weights to the compute precision, so resident size is the parameter count × 4 (f32 on CPU) or × 2 (half precision on CUDA). On CUDA it counts toward GPU memory, and on CPU it counts toward process memory (the resident size plus 1 GiB, minimum 1 GiB, and it does not use GPU memory). The candle separation model packages are the exception: HTDemucs-FT computes in f32 on both CPU and CUDA (the same as v2), so its resident size is again twice the stored bytes, and its pool follows the device. GGML's resident size is the stored bytes of the GGML weight file (when the size is unknown, the manifest's estimate is used), plus the resident size of VAD and the aligner on candle CPU (parameter count × 4). When the device is `cpu`, this counts toward process memory. When the device is a GPU (`cuda` or `vulkan`), all of it counts toward GPU memory. (VAD and the aligner actually live in process memory, so this part is booked to the wrong pool; it will be split once measured.) Parameter counts are recorded on the component (`parameters`, counted from the safetensors header). When they are absent, the estimate is stored bytes × compute bit width / storage bit width (`weightBits`). For quantized packages, parts that are not quantized make this estimate too large. Measured on CPU, Qwen3-ASR 0.6B is about 3.7 GiB after loading and about 6.3 GiB after using the aligner. The 1.7B is about 6.4 GiB, and MOSS is about 2.8 GiB. The candle variant of HTDemucs-FT took 42 seconds to separate 3.7 seconds of a mix on this machine's CPU (`--release`; candle was compiled on macOS only for measurement). That is about 1.9 cores, and each sub-model takes about 10 seconds per 7.8-second window. Its peak process footprint (phys_footprint) was 2.6 GB. By extrapolation (not measured), half an hour of material would take over three hours on CPU, so practical use depends on CUDA (not verified on this machine). The candle synthesis model packages are also estimated from parameter counts (every component records `parameters`). The codec shared by Qwen3-TTS is stored as f32 and has 170 million parameters. The MLX synthesis model packages, and model packages whose weight size is unknown, use fixed values (1 GiB memory, 2 GiB GPU memory). The candle synthesis measurements below were made on this machine's CPU (`--release`; candle was compiled on macOS only for measurement; system memory was tight during the measurements, with about 10 GB in swap, so the resident set is lower than normal and the times are longer). Resident size is the process's maximum resident set, including the mapped weight-file pages. Qwen3-TTS 0.6B Base is estimated at 4.3 GB. Worker load took 5 seconds, 2.1 GB after loading, 2.6 GB at peak. Cloning 2.9 seconds of English took 162 seconds. The 0.6B CustomVoice is estimated at 4.3 GB with a 3.5 GB peak, and 5.0 seconds of Chinese took 268 seconds. GPT-SoVITS is estimated at 2.2 GB with a 3.8 GB peak, and two sentences totaling 9 seconds took 56 seconds (including load). OmniVoice is estimated at 3.3 GB with a 4.2 GB peak, and five sentences totaling 22 seconds took 646 seconds. IndexTTS 2.5 is estimated at 6.7 GB with a 5.2 GB peak, and each 3.4–4.9-second sentence took 420–625 seconds (it was swapping). IndexTTS2 (estimated 7.7 GB) and VoxCPM2 (9.5 GB) have not been measured on this 16 GiB machine. Apart from GPT-SoVITS, peaks stayed within the estimate plus 1 GiB. GPT-SoVITS exceeds it by about 0.5 GB. Its maximum resident set also includes the mapped 16-bit weight-file pages (about 1.1 GB). On CPU, synthesis uses only one to two cores (as in v2) and runs slower than real time. Qwen3-TTS is about 55 times slower, IndexTTS 2.5 about 120–145 times, OmniVoice about 30 times, and GPT-SoVITS about 6 times. CUDA has not been verified on this machine. So, as with text-to-image, the model description of a candle synthesis model package carries `notes` when the device is `cpu`. The notes state honestly how much slower than real time it is. Models that have not been measured (the three variants of Qwen3-TTS 1.7B, IndexTTS2 and VoxCPM2) are marked "not measured". With CUDA the expected speed is much faster (unmeasured). The Worker omits these notes when it reports CUDA, the same as for the MLX version of the same model. The one sentence used for the preview before playback in Settings uses this path first.

Text-to-image model packages have three weight segments (about 9.8 GB in total). They are read in layer by layer as a stream and never all reside in memory at once. GPU memory is counted from the measured peak registered on the model package (`image.peakBytes`, which is 2 GiB for Qwen-Image-2.1), not from the total weight size. The resident-size estimate above does not apply to them, and the Worker does not refuse a load against a budget. Measured process peaks on this machine: about 0.7 GB at 256², and about 1.0 GB at 1024² (MLX peaks were 0.6–0.9 GB, consistent with v2). Weights that have been read stay in the system file cache, which can be reclaimed, so they do not count toward process memory. When the cache is evicted, the DiT segment (about 4 GB) is re-read from disk at every step. This makes it slower, but it does not fail.

The candle variant (`qwen-image-2.1@candle`, for platforms other than Apple Silicon) reads the same repository, and streams it as v2 does. The text encoder is loaded layer by layer. Each projection is dequantized when it is used and discarded afterward (the word embeddings first pick the rows for each token and then dequantize them). The DiT keeps only one dequantized layer of matrices (about 220 million parameters, about 0.9 GB in f32), and it re-dequantizes the whole DiT at every step. The VAE convolutions are read on demand and decoded by row bands. Compute precision is f32 on CPU and bf16 on CUDA. Peaks are registered per device. On CPU they count toward process memory (`image.peakBytes`, 6 GiB, and no GPU memory). On CUDA they count toward GPU memory (`image.devicePeakBytes.cuda`, 4 GiB). `ModelCatalog.imagePeak` takes the value and the pool from the device the Worker reports in the handshake. The peaks already include activations, so 1 GiB is not added on top. The CPU figures are measured process peaks (phys_footprint) on this machine (Apple M-series, 8 cores, 16 GiB; `--release`; candle was compiled on macOS only for measurement): 2.4 GB at 256² and 4.7 GB at 1024². The 1024² peak occurs during VAE decoding, while the DiT segment peaks at 3.3 GB. The resident set also includes the mapped weight pages (4.9 GB at 1024²). As with MLX, those pages are reclaimable file cache and are not counted. The difference is that candle re-reads the whole DiT at every step, so each step slows down when the cache is evicted. The 6 GiB budget leaves room for long side 1536 and for fluctuation. A 16 GiB machine can run it in both foreground and background priority. An 8 GiB machine can run it only at interactive priority. The 4 GiB CUDA figure is not measured on this machine. The v2 version of the same pipeline, measured on an NVIDIA GPU (compute capability 8.6), peaked at 3.57 GiB of total GPU memory for 1024×576 with 20 steps (including about 1.2 GB used by the desktop). 1024² was not measured. CPU is very slow. Text encoding takes 70–150 seconds. At 256², each step takes about 75 seconds (the first step, with a cold read, takes 120–160 seconds), and nearly all of that is dequantization and transposition. At 1024², one step takes 614 seconds (with a cold read and under memory pressure), and VAE decoding takes 165 seconds. By extrapolation (not measured), 1024² with 20 steps would take about three hours, 512² about one hour, and the self-check (256², 4 steps) about 7 minutes. At 256², the process uses only about 1.3–1.6 cores. At 1024², it uses 2.4–4.7 cores. Dequantization and the copy in `weight.t().contiguous()` are single-threaded, and additional cores only speed up the matrix multiplication and convolution parts. So the model description's `notes` on CPU honestly state that generation takes hours. On CUDA, v2 measured 1024×576 with 20 steps at about 11 minutes (debug build). The same seed produces different images on candle and MLX, because each backend uses its own random number generator for noise. Within one backend the output is reproducible byte for byte (on this machine, two 256² generations were byte-identical). For dubbing separation, a single step accounts for its own memory and staging (2 GiB each, for the decoded full-length audio and the two output tracks). Before loading a new model package, confirm that the Worker to be released has actually exited, not merely that an unload request was sent.

**Failure classification and recovery**.

| Failure | Detection | Handling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Load failure | `model.load` returns an error | No retry. Error code `MODEL_LOAD_FAILED`, classified by cause as `not-installed` (validation fails), `unsupported` (backend or device not supported), or `error` (insufficient resources). The Job fails and offers actionable options: install, switch model, switch backend |
| Runtime crash | Process exits abnormally, stdout closes, heartbeat timeout | Mark the current Job `interrupted` and retry it once automatically, keyed by `model package + input hash + Worker version`. If it crashes again, the Job fails with error code `MODEL_WORKER_CRASHED`, with the tail of stderr attached. If the same model package crashes N times within M minutes, the capability is marked unavailable (reason `resource` or `unsupported`) and is not started automatically again until the user re-enables it |
| Output violates contract | Runtime validation of the staging result fails | No retry. The Job fails with error code `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID`; the raw file is moved to a diagnostics directory for reporting |
| Cancellation | `jobs.cancel` or a stop barrier (§7.4) | Send `job.cancel`. The Worker stops at a segment boundary and acks; it then remains `ready` and the weights are kept. If the ack deadline (default 5 seconds) is exceeded, the process is killed, the model package returns to `installed`, and it is started on demand for the next task |

**Orphans and self-protection**. The Worker exits when stdin closes, and periodically checks that the parent process still exists and is the same as at startup; otherwise it exits on its own (Unix compares `getppid`; on Windows it opens a handle to the parent process and waits for it to end. The handle pins the process object, so the PID cannot be reused, and a process created later than the Worker is not treated as its parent). A new Runtime does not claim Workers from the previous generation and does not rely on PIDs (§7.7). When the Runtime stops normally, a Worker that falls under the "executor" in §2.4 first cancels its tasks, then closes stdin, and is sent SIGKILL if it exceeds the grace period.

**Idle and memory pressure**. A model is not unloaded immediately after a task completes. The Runtime keeps an idle timer (default 10 minutes); on expiry it sends `model.unload`, and the Worker unloads and exits. The Runtime subscribes to system memory pressure (on macOS, `kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level`; other platforms use an equivalent signal). When pressure rises, it unloads idle Workers in least-recently-used order. A task in progress is not interrupted, but that Worker accepts no new tasks. Users can pin a model to stay resident in settings.

**Staging and cleanup**. Each Job's decoded audio, segment results and final result are written under `<runtime-home>/staging/jobs/<jobId>/`. The Worker writes only to this directory, never to `artifacts/`, `models/` or video directories. After the Runtime validates the result and publishes it as an Artifact, the directory is deleted; it is also deleted when the Job fails or is cancelled. On a crash or validation failure, only the stderr tail, task metadata and the non-conforming raw files are moved to `logs/diagnostics/<jobId>/`. These files contain transcript text, so when diagnostics are exported they are previewed and redacted per §12.6, and they are not sent to product analytics by default. At Runtime startup, `staging/jobs/` is scanned: directories that are absent from the Job Ledger or already terminated are deleted once the grace period has passed; directories with a valid checkpoint are kept for resumption (§7.5).

### 6.6 Speech recognition pipeline

**Capabilities**. `transcribe` (media → transcript with word-level timing) and `align` (media + known text → word-level timing) are two independent capabilities. When a cloud service returns a transcript without word timing, the gap is filled with local `align`; after the user corrects the text, re-alignment also goes through `align`.

**Local pipeline**. A `transcribe` Job runs inside the Worker in the following order. Steps 1 to 3 are streamed, so memory does not grow linearly with media length. Models that segment on their own and identify speakers (MOSS) skip steps 2 and 3, and the model produces the segmentation in one pass. Step 5 aligns only lines longer than 5 seconds. Step 7 uses the speaker model to merge per-chunk speakers into global ones; without a speaker model, per-chunk labels are kept and marked `diarization-unavailable` (Model Worker protocol spec §2.5.1). In `worker.hello`, the Worker declares per model family what it can recognize (`transcribeFamilies`); the Runtime does not load a model family that is not declared.

1. Decode: the Worker uses an ffmpeg pipe (`model-runtime`'s `audio.rs`; 16 kHz PCM WAV takes the pure-Rust path and does not use ffmpeg) to decode the selected audio track of the source into 16 kHz mono f32. Chunks are written to staging and read on demand. Demuxing allows only whitelisted demuxers (consistent with the Runtime's media analysis) and reads local files only. Media with no audio track ends here with `no-audio-track`.
2. VAD: streaming voice activity detection, which outputs speech intervals. Media with no speech throughout ends with `no-speech`. Both outcomes are facts reported to the user, not empty successes.
3. Segmentation: split by speech intervals and the model's maximum segment length. Segments are the unit of progress, cancellation, checkpoints and retries.
4. Recognition: feed each segment to the ASR model. Language is set by the task parameter: **assert** (specified by the user; the model must not change it) or **prefer** (auto-detect; the result carries the detected language). Auto-detection fixes the task's language on the first segment that yields text: it prefers the language name the model self-reports, otherwise infers from the accumulated text. Empty segments do not count, and a language the model self-reports on such a segment is not trusted. Once fixed, `job.language` is pushed once, and the result's `language` is that value. It is not imposed on later segments, though: each segment is still judged by the model itself. (v2 locked it for later segments. Whisper given a fixed language translates speech in another language into it, so mixed Chinese–English material came out entirely in one language.) Alignment and word-time estimation use the language the model self-reports for that segment, falling back to the task language when none is reported. The cost is that very short, ambiguous segments may be misidentified on their own. When a speaker uses one language and then, without a pause, switches to another within the same segment, both recognition models decode in only one language and may drop the other (a model limitation; v2 is the same). Local models provide no language confidence, so `confidence` is null.
5. Forced alignment: when ASR itself cannot give reliable word timing, an aligner fills it in (per segment, aligning the recognized text against that segment's audio; words are marked `aligned`). If the model package has no aligner, words are interpolated within the segment by character length and marked `estimated`. If alignment of a segment errors or yields no words, that segment likewise falls back to estimation, with an `alignment-failed` warning, and the task does not fail.
6. Monotonic correction: word times are monotonic within a segment, do not cross segment boundaries, and do not exceed the media duration. Corrected words keep their original `timingQuality` and get a warning.
7. Speakers: when speaker diarization is requested (`diarize`), it runs over the whole audio after recognition and alignment finish (`diarizing` stage, progress unit `steps`). Pyannote segmentation-3.0 judges, frame by frame with 10-second windows and 5-second steps, up to 3 local speakers in each window. Each local speaker gets a voice embedding from WeSpeaker. Constrained agglomerative clustering (merge threshold 0.715) synthesizes global speakers, which are then projected onto words: each word is scored by overlap; within continuous runs of speech, votes are weighted by duration to unify the speaker; sufficiently long interjections keep their own speaker; a segment takes the speaker with the most speaking time among its words. `speakerId` is numbered in order of first appearance as `spk-1`, `spk-2`, and so on. It is stable within the same Job and not guaranteed consistent across Jobs. Segments in `job.segment` and `segments.jsonl` do not yet carry speakers. The algorithm, thresholds and tests are ported unchanged from v2. Pyannote has two builds, MLX and candle (the MLX and Core ML backends use the MLX build; the candle and GGML backends use the candle build). If the model package has no `segmentation` and `speaker` components (the speaker diarization model package is not installed, §6.3), or loading or inference errors, no diarization is done. A `diarization-unavailable` warning is attached and the transcript is still published (v2 failed the entire task). Time spent on diarization counts toward `asrMs`.

Internally the Worker computes in f32 seconds, and output is always converted to integer ticks of the declared `timescale`. The boundary between seconds and ticks lies only inside the Worker.

**Diarizing an existing transcript** (AI tool "identify speakers"). This does not re-transcribe. A fixed flow, `speakers` (§7.9), hands the word timings of a `speech` document in the video, together with its source, to a Worker that has only the speaker diarization model package installed (`diarize` of `job.run`, Model Worker protocol spec §2.5.5). The algorithm is the same as step 7, and the result is a speaker for each word. The Runtime then assembles a proposal: existing speakers are reused by overlap duration (keeping their IDs and names, so the library's voice bindings and the dubbing speaker bindings stay intact), and new speakers are created for those that cannot be reused. A trial application is run once through the caption and translation core, yielding each speaker's cue count and preview clips, plus the number of translation entries that would be re-split. The proposal is stored as an artifact, and a summary goes to the confirmation page in the UI. The flow itself does not change the video. After the user confirms (and may rename), it is applied via `edits.applySpeakers` as a single undoable edit.
