When the Runtime starts, unsettled reservations from the last run are settled as `conservative`, because they may have started before the previous process exited. Automatic retries also consume budget. The previous attempt is settled as `conservative` and recorded in `grant.retries`; the retry re-reserves on the same Grant. If quota is insufficient, no further retry is made and the Job fails with `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. Therefore a “只这一次” (this time only) Grant does not cover automatic retries. Per-batch translation in a fixed pipeline (§7.9) is an in-process call that creates no Job. It reserves and settles each batch through the same ledger, and checks coverage and quota once before the pipeline starts.

**Task budget.** The task contract's `budgetPolicyRef` points to a task budget: the total call count (`maxCalls`) and amount (`cap`) of all outbound calls in this task across Providers. `null` means this item is unlimited. “Calls in the task” are the Jobs the agent submits within the task, the fixed pipelines started within the task, and those pipelines' sub-steps and in-process calls (found through the parent Job's submitter). Submissions from the UI, CLI, external services and nodes are not in the task and are not subject to the task budget.

- Each call first passes Grant admission and then the task budget. Both reserve synchronously in the same step. If either fails, the Job is not created and does not enter approval. The task budget does not loosen Grants: anything a Grant does not cover still requires authorization.
- Counts are calls already settled plus calls in reservation.
- Starting a pipeline does not pre-check the task budget. The pipeline is admitted at each outbound step; when the budget is exceeded, it stops at that step and fails with `TASK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, with `details.step` giving the step where it stopped. Steps before it that do not go outbound (download, import) complete normally and their results are kept. The agent can only start imports from links (§7.9), whose only outbound step is transcription.
- An amount cap accepts only calls whose upper bound can be estimated in the capped currency. The following cases cannot be guaranteed (`TASK_BUDGET_UNVERIFIABLE`): this call's amount cannot be estimated or its currency differs; calls with unknown amounts already exist, or calls were settled in another currency; a reserved call has an amount that cannot be estimated or a different currency. A service-reported cost is recorded in the reported currency; once a currency differs from the cap, the amount cap can no longer be guaranteed. Otherwise, used plus reserved plus this call's estimate is compared against the cap. Exceeding it is `TASK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` (`details.measure` is `calls` or `amount`).
- Reservation, start and settlement reuse the Grant ledger's durable records. A task-budget reservation and a Grant reservation are the same entry, written to disk together, and “started” is durably recorded before the start. At settlement the task budget is recorded only once: a reservation that never started is released; one that completed with a service-reported cost is counted as reported, otherwise counted as the reserved estimate, and if the estimate cannot be determined it counts calls only and is recorded as amount unknown; one that started but failed, was cancelled or was interrupted is counted as one call plus the estimate. After a crash, settling orphaned reservations at startup also records only once, and reservations already settled are not counted again.
- Task budgets are recorded in `taskBudgets` in `store/grants.json`, one entry per task, keeping at most the most recent 1000.

| Error code | Category | Meaning | Remedy |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TASK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` | `conflict` | The task budget's count or amount is insufficient for this call | User raises the budget in the task contract, or waits for in-flight calls to settle |
| `TASK_BUDGET_UNVERIFIABLE` | `conflict` | The task budget has an amount cap, but the total cannot be guaranteed in one currency | User removes the amount cap (keeping only the count cap), or switches to a model with a unit price |

`details` carries the `taskId` and this task budget (cap and usage), plus the CLI command for viewing the contract. When the user edits the task budget, only the cap changes and usage stays as it is. The agent cannot modify it.

### 7.9 Jobs without a Task and fixed pipelines

**Download video tool (updated):** the UI entry name and icon are “下载视频” (Download video) / S2 Download. It reuses the `link-import` fixed pipeline. Without a target, no video is created. `transcribe: true` can submit a file-transcription Job directly, publishing TXT/SRT; the selected model and transcription directory are frozen at submission. `projectId` is optional and only associates the transcript and subtitles with a project; it does not change paths. Video, TXT and SRT are all saved to `downloads.directory` or the host's Downloads folder. Space keeps the project association, and it is not lost when task records are trimmed. The legacy `videoId` / `target` parameters are kept for existing flows. `cookieBrowsers` accepts only supported browser names (in attempt order) and is not passed by default. Both resolution and download pass `--cookies-from-browser`; raw cookies, arbitrary arguments or config files are not accepted. Categorized errors guide the user to check detection, update according to install source, and log in or repair cookie reading. When several browsers are checked, the next one is tried only when cookies cannot be read or the site still requires login (see “从链接导入” (Import from link) below). After a successful download, if transcription fails, only transcription can be retried, and existing files are kept.

The `JobSpec` in §7.2 describes computation that an Agent initiates within a Task. Jobs have three other initiators that have no Task. The other rules are unchanged:

| Initiator | Example | `submitter` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Direct operation from the UI or CLI | Tool page's speech generation, image generation, music generation, text generation; model installation and checks (§6.3) | `connection` |
| External service | MCP service's `start_transcribe`; a request to the model API service; remote tasks received by a node | `service` |
| Fixed pipeline | Transcription, subtitle translation, dubbing translation, import from link (the same-named operations on the tool page and in the editor all go through the pipeline) | `pipeline` |

- `taskId`, `runId` and `runGeneration` exist only when the initiator is an Agent. Jobs an agent submits through tools are recorded with `submitter: { kind: 'agent', id: <session ID>, taskId }`. Later tasks in the same session can also view and cancel them (§3.5), and a main stop cancels them too (§7.4). Jobs from other initiators record `submitter` plus the connection, service or pipeline that started them. `permissionGrantRef` must exist for all initiators. A direct operation is authorized by the user's click plus an enabled Provider. An external service is authorized by access policy plus service approval (§4.8).
- **Jobs without a video.** The input is a file or a piece of text, and the output is not applied to any video. After publishing, the product enters Space (§5.7). Together with its parameters, seed, model and source, it forms a **generation record** (the Job record's `generation` and `result.outputs`). Afterwards, “add to video” is an ordinary asset import (the agent uses `importAsset` with `artifactId`; the source is still this generation record, §3.5), and “create a new video from this” goes through `space.openForEdit`. Remote tasks received by a node are an exception: their results do not enter the artifact library (§6.7).
- **Call facts in progress.** Jobs that call a model per clip or per sentence report the call count, retry count and failure count in their progress events. These are factual counts, not substitutes for a percentage.

**Fixed pipelines** are multi-step work with fixed steps that does not need model planning. A pipeline is a deterministic orchestration inside the Runtime, executed by the principal `system:pipeline`, and it does not create an AgentSession:

- A pipeline is one parent Job (`kind: 'pipeline'`). Each step is a child Job (`kind: 'pipeline-step'`, with `parentJobId`), each with its own frozen input, outputs and failure state. The parent Job's `submitter` is the connection or service that started it; child Jobs record `submitter: { kind: 'pipeline', id: <parent Job> }`. The parent Job's progress is reported per step, and phase and call counts follow the step currently running. Parameters are validated and frozen at submission. Execution and retries read only this one copy.
- **Child Job folding.** `jobs.list` lists only parent Jobs by default, and steps fold into the parent Job's `pipeline.steps` (each step's status, latest child Job, execution count and outputs). With `children: true`, child Jobs are listed too. The `jobs` topic pushes all records, and the client decides how to fold them.
- **Record retention.** The task ledger keeps only 200 finished top-level Jobs, evicted from oldest to newest by end time. Child Jobs are not counted; they are kept and evicted together with their parent. Unfinished Jobs and their children are not evicted. Pipelines that can still be retried (failed, cancelled or interrupted), the latest 50, are kept additionally and do not count against the 200. Older ones are evicted like ordinary Jobs, and after eviction they can no longer be retried.
- A step may carry a condition (recorded as `skipped` when unmet, e.g. optional vocal separation). A step may also spawn several child Jobs (e.g. per-sentence synthesis), so the step shape can accommodate dubbing translation and import from link.
- Between steps, results pass only through published artifacts and the video's committed versions. When a step fails, completed steps are kept, the pipeline stops at that step, and it can be retried from that step. Cancellation follows the barrier in §7.4: first stop starting new steps, then cancel the running child Jobs.
- **Retry semantics.** `pipelines.retry` accepts only failed, cancelled or interrupted pipelines. It reuses the same parent Job (`attempt` plus one) and the frozen parameters, and first re-checks the prerequisites from submission time (text model configuration, ffmpeg). Retry starts from the first step whose output is no longer valid. Earlier completed steps are reused when their output artifacts still exist and the inputs they depended on have not changed (the source document is still the frozen version; the input file's length and modification time are unchanged). Otherwise that step is redone, and every step after it is redone. Redone steps get new child Jobs; the old ones stay in the list.
- **Interruption.** When the Runtime stops or crashes, unfinished pipelines are recorded as `interrupted` (`JOB_INTERRUPTED`, stopped at the running step). After restart they do not resume automatically; the user retries.
- When a pipeline writes to a video, it does so the same way an Agent does: it submits an edit transaction through Application, subject to version checks and protection scope. A pipeline cannot do what only a TaskContract can authorize, such as automatically overwriting the user's manual edits.
- **The entry point decides the executor.** The same task (translation, polishing, chaptering, writing) has two possible executors: an agent, or a pipeline calling a text model (`generateText`). Their outputs and write methods are the same. Requests raised in a session are handled by the agent: the agent is itself a text model and translates and polishes on its own without going through `generateText`, and a missing text model configuration does not affect this. When the session does not belong to a project, the files it produces (e.g. a translated subtitle) are placed in the downloads directory with `downloads_save` for the user. Tool entry points (the Rail's tool page, the CLI) go only through the pipeline and do not start an agent. Editor tool entry points, by default, send this set of parameters as one message to the session of this video, to be completed by the agent. When the user explicitly switches to “直接调模型” (call the model directly), it goes through the pipeline (product design §5.10). The executor is fixed at initiation and does not change afterward. A pipeline returns `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED` when the text model is not configured, and does not hand off to the agent instead. On failure it also does not hand off automatically. When no agent is available, the editor entry explains why and does not silently switch to the pipeline.


**Tool** is the product-level catalog of fixed pipelines and direct tasks (product design §2.7). A tool is not a separate execution mechanism. Each tool corresponds to one fixed pipeline or one directly submitted Job type; the catalog only declares what input it accepts and what output it produces.

- **Catalog.** Each tool declares: a stable ID; its group (`speech` speech and subtitles, `text-image` text and images, `music` music, `video-file` video files; product design §2.7); input kinds (`file`, `link`, `text`, `video`, `document`); result kind (`video` or `artifact`); execution method (a pipeline name, or a direct task's method and capability); capabilities and external tools that are required, and those used only for some inputs; and whether it needs a network download. The declaration is a static registry (`TOOL_CATALOGUE` in `@cuccut/jobs`). `tools.list` adds current availability: capabilities are judged by the same selection used at submission time, external tools use the last probe result, and under strict offline mode (`offline.strict`) network need is checked. Reasons reuse existing error codes. A tool is unavailable when a required dependency is unmet. A dependency used only for some inputs is recorded as a limitation, and the tool remains available. The interface reads this catalog. The CLI has no separate command to list it; on the CLI, fixed pipelines are first-level verbs derived from the tool catalog (§3.5).
- **Three ways to write a target.** A video tool's pipeline parameters use the same `target`: `{ videoId }` (an already-open video), `{ entryId }` (a video entry in Space, which does not need to be open), and `{ create: { projectId | conversationId, name?, media } }` (create a new video, either in a project or in the session's source directory; give one of the two. `media` is the absolute path of a local media file, or the output of a previous download step). A top-level `videoId` is equivalent to `{ videoId }`; if both are given they must refer to the same video. `entryId` is resolved to a `videoId` at submission and recorded as the pipeline's first step `target` (the resolved target). `create` is performed by the pipeline that accepts it, in its own step. Import from link creates it after publishing, with `media` being the file it downloaded, not a parameter; transcription must be given `media`, and the file's existence is confirmed at submission. Later steps recognize only `videoId`. Each pipeline declares which forms it accepts. Translation and dubbing do not create videos, so `create` is rejected for them with `PIPELINE_TARGET_UNSUPPORTED`.
- **Opening a video on the user's behalf.** When the target is not open, the pipeline opens it with a Runtime lease (the same path used for restart recovery, §7.5). It holds the lease throughout the pipeline and releases it on completion, cancellation or failure. If another connection already has it open, that copy is used directly, with version checks as usual. If another process has it locked, `pipelines.start` rejects with `VIDEO_LOCKED`, and no task or anything else is created. Videos in the trash (`SPACE_ENTRY_TRASHED`) and entries that are not videos (`SPACE_ENTRY_NOT_VIDEO`) are likewise rejected at submission. Leases exist only in memory. After a Runtime crash or stop the pipeline is `interrupted`, and no video stays open indefinitely. On retry, the video is first reopened at the recorded location (`STALE_JOB_INPUT` if that location no longer holds the original video), and then the pipeline continues from the step where it stopped.
- **Creating a new video is a pipeline step.** `create` makes a new video in the project directory (same path as `videos.create`; the name defaults to the media's title). When a session is given, it goes where the agent's `videos_create` puts it: in the project if the session belongs to one, otherwise in the session's working directory, without creating a project for it (§3.10). Targets that only download and do not create a video are likewise unbound, and are resolved at runtime from the session's source at that moment. The step imports `media` as a link and places it on the timeline (first track of the same kind, starting at 0, covering the whole media, in the same transaction as the import). Once this step completes, the video is in Space and the pipeline holds its lease. If a later step fails, the video is kept, and a retry starts from the failed step. Once the creation step has completed, it is not run again, even if earlier steps are redone. Creation and import are also not duplicated after a crash: the pipeline first reserves a directory in the project (or the session's source directory) and records it in the pipeline's staging. A retry recognizes that directory (opening the video if one is already there, creating it there if not). The import uses a fixed `commandId` and recognizes an already-applied import by its receipt.
- **Candidate inputs.** The facts the input picker needs come from the content index (§5.11) and the Space directory (§5.7), without opening videos: each video's transcripts (language, whether word-level timing exists, whether it is on the timeline), translations (target language, the transcript translated from, unit count and stale unit count), and dubbing groups (language, the translation and transcript used). `tools.candidates` returns the eligible video entries under the tool's rules. Transcription and import from link cover all videos outside the trash. Translation and dubbing list only videos that have transcripts, with each transcript and any existing translation or dubbing shown. Scope rules are the same as `space.list`. Videos not yet indexed are still listed and marked (it cannot be concluded that they have no transcript). As with `space.search`, the result states whether it is complete.
- **Result sources.** The documents and instances the tool writes into a video, and the artifacts it publishes, record this run's parent Job and the tool ID in Provenance. The source of a newly created video entry is this run. Space's artifact index keeps the pipeline record for a completed creation step, so the source remains even after the Job Ledger evicts this run. Space uses this to display sources and to offer the “next step” tools that can take the result.
- **Save location.** Results without a video (file-to-file pipelines, transcription and download without a target, direct-task outputs) all land in one directory. It is the pipeline parameter `outDir` if given; otherwise the setting `downloads.directory`; when that is `null`, it is the Downloads folder of the host running the Runtime (a Runtime started by the desktop app uses the system-provided Downloads folder; one started another way uses `~/Downloads`). `tools.list` returns the resolved directory to the desktop interface as `saveDirectory` (browsers do not get it). Direct tasks (`synthesizeSpeech`, `generateImage`, `generateMusic`, `generateText`) save an extra copy only when `saveDir` is given. The tool page always passes it (the save location shown in the UI). Agents and other callers that do not pass it only enter the artifact library and write no file. The directory is determined and frozen at submission and is created if missing. If it cannot be written, the error is `OUTPUT_DESTINATION_UNAVAILABLE` (import from link keeps `LINK_DESTINATION_UNAVAILABLE`). File names are readable: taken from the title, the source file name, or the start of the prompt, cleaned with the same rules as downloaded files. Existing files are not overwritten; name clashes get a sequence number. Pipelines write their result files directly into that directory, and the file is the output (after it is moved or deleted, the entry becomes `missing`). For direct tasks the artifact library remains the authority for the bytes. After publishing, a copy with a readable name is written to `saveDir`, and its path is recorded at `result.outputs[].path` (text generation with `saveDir` also returns `outputs` in its result). “Show in folder” uses that path. If the copy cannot be written, only a `save-copy-failed` warning is recorded and the task does not fail. If the copy is missing, the UI says it is gone and the entry is not counted as missing. The Space directory gives the location (`entryPath`) for results in the save location, based on `result.outputs[].path`.
- **Space entries as input.** Parameters that accept a `file` (transcription's `file`, transcode's `inputs`, the subtitle translation file's `input`, and the materials for speech generation and text generation) all accept `{ entryId }` in place of an absolute path. At submission, the Space directory resolves it to that entry's file (a file in a source directory, a published artifact, or a result in the save location). A trashed entry gives `SPACE_ENTRY_TRASHED`; an entry with no file (placeholder, missing, or editable video) gives `SPACE_ENTRY_NO_FILE`; the wrong kind gives `SPACE_ENTRY_UNSUPPORTED`. The resolved path is frozen into the parameters and is thereafter treated the same as a given path. The candidate-entry list is simply `space.list` filtered by kind; no separate method exists for it.
- **No overwriting.** Translations, subtitle layers and dubbing groups are only added. When the target video already has a result of the same kind, a new copy is still added and the result says so. Which one to use is a decision made in the editor. A newly added original-language subtitle layer is not stacked on the screen over an original subtitle of the same material that is already displayed. It is added disabled, and the user switches to it in the editor. For transcripts: if a video that already has a transcript is transcribed again, the result goes into a newly created video, or replaces the current transcript by switching to it (see “转录” (Transcription) below, §6.6). A video never holds two transcripts side by side.
- **Authorization on the spot.** When a present user (a desktop UI connection on the main gateway) starts a pipeline or directly submits a task, rejections for missing authorization, revoked authorization, insufficient quota, or an unestimable amount (`GRANT_REQUIRED`, `GRANT_REVOKED`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_UNVERIFIABLE`) also carry pending items for approval (`details.pendingGrants`, the `GrantRequestItem` of §12.5: capability, recipient, data kind, video, reason, cost and estimate). These cover all of the pipeline's outbound transfers at once (e.g. dubbing's translation and synthesis). The `remedy` command stays the same. After the user confirms, the client issues each item through `grants.create`, then resubmits with the same `commandId`. The rejection happens before the task is created, so the resubmission is the first submission. The Runtime does not issue grants automatically and does not loosen any authorization. When the task budget is insufficient, no pending items are offered, because approving outbound transfers cannot loosen the task budget. The CLI, agents and browser sessions still receive only the rejection and the command to run. External services follow service approval (§4.8).
- **Entry points.** The desktop UI, the CLI, and same-named operations in the editor all go through the same pipeline. The web service can read directories and candidate inputs. Tools whose execution method is not on the allowlist are marked unavailable. Web `tools.list` does not give a save location, so the tool page has no save-location row, and direct tasks carry no `saveDir` (they go only to the artifact library and write no copy). When the save location is not inside a registered project directory, pipeline tools whose results land only at the save location are marked unavailable (`PATH_OUTSIDE_PROJECT`); tools whose results can be written into the video are marked restricted (video only); direct tasks are unaffected. MCP services provide no directory and only the subset they already have. The results page and the task detail's “hand to Agent” use `space.continueInConversation` to put a reference to the result entry into the session (§5.7). The draft in the input box is pre-filled by the interface and sent by the user; no new method is added.

Tool-to-flow mapping:

| Tool | Input | Result | Execution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Transcribe | `file`, `video` | `artifact` (default), `video` | `transcribe` flow. With only `file` (a local media file or a media entry in the Space) and no `target`: transcribe → publish a TXT and an SRT to the save location; the result is two outputs, a document and a subtitle file (the same shape as `transcribe` when imported from a link, `downloadTranscript`). With `target`: resolve the target (creating a video if needed) → transcribe → create the caption layer. A link first goes through link import |
| Translate subtitles | `video` (with a transcript), `file` (SRT, VTT: a local file or a subtitle entry in the Space) | `video`, `artifact` | `translate` flow; for a video target, the caption layer in the target language is created after the translation is written. Subtitle files go through the `translate-subtitles` flow (the catalog declares a different execution for this input kind, `executionByInput`) |
| Dub | `video` (with a transcript) | `video` | `dub` flow |
| Download video | `link` | `artifact` | `link-import` flow, optionally generating TXT / SRT |
| Extract audio | `file` | `artifact` | `transcode` flow (`action: 'extract-audio'`) |
| Compress, merge video | `file` | `artifact` | `transcode` flow |
| Generate speech, generate text | `text`, `document` (a document or subtitle in the Space: speech generation takes its text; text generation attaches it as material after the prompt) | `artifact` | Job without a video (`models.synthesizeSpeech`, `models.generateText`); the result is published to the save location |
| Generate image | `text` | `artifact` | Job without a video (`models.generateImage`); the result is published to the save location |
| Generate music | `text` | `artifact` | Job without a video (`models.generateMusic`, `generate-music`, group `music`); only the locally installed music model pack, no network; the result is published to the save location |

**Laying down the score.** When the editor's "Generate score" (product design §2.7 "Generate score") is submitted, it is still the direct task above: `targetDurationSec` is the video's duration; if it exceeds the model's `maxDurationSec`, `maxDurationSec` is used. `generateMusic` itself does not loop or concatenate. When a video is given, the task only imports the result as a candidate asset and does not place it on the timeline (§6.1). Putting the music track on the timeline is a later `edits.apply` submitted by the UI (or by the agent), which writes it with existing operations in **loop-fill** form:

- A music piece of P seconds filling a video of T seconds: segment k (k from 0) is an `audio` instance with `role: 'music'`, playing from the start of the asset. Its sequence start is `k·(P − X)` and its length is `min(P, T − start)`, where X is the seam crossfade (2 seconds). A next segment is needed only when the previous one has not finished before T, so the last segment's total length exceeds X. If the music is not shorter than the video, there is only one segment.
- Instances on the same track cannot overlap (video format spec §3.4). On each seam the segments alternate across two music tracks (or a new one is created with `addTrack` if none exists), and each segment is placed in turn with `addItem`. The UI writes the whole thing as **one transaction**: `addTrack` (the one or two music tracks used, referenced by `ref`) plus one `insertItems`, with one `audio` instance per segment. `playDuration` is the truncation, and the fades are written directly into `mix` (`addItem` cannot reference a track created in the same transaction, and `setAudioMix` needs the instance ID first). One undo reverts the whole transaction. An agent may instead write it in several steps with `addItem` and `setAudioMix`; the result is the same.
- Fades use the instance mix (video format spec §3.9 `AudioMix`, `setAudioMix`): `fadeIn = X` for every segment except the first, and `fadeOut = X` for every segment except the last. The last segment's `fadeOut` is the end-credits fade-out (3 seconds, not longer than the length after its fade-in). All values are within [0, 5] seconds. Instance fades are linear, so a seam is the sum of two linear fades, not an equal-power crossfade (`audioCrossfade` is only for transitions between video instances).
- Timing of the fill: task completion only imports the asset. If the video is open in the editor, the UI submits this transaction right away; otherwise it is submitted the next time the video is opened. Filled task IDs are recorded in the local UI preferences. A task whose asset is already on the timeline (filled before, or placed by the user) is not filled again, so deleting the filled music and reopening the video does not fill it again.
- No speed change and no stretching: each segment's `timeMap` is 1× speed. When the music should be lowered under narration, add `setDucking` as usual, targeting these two music tracks.
- When the agent has a score emotion arc, it may instead generate several segments, one per row of the arc, placed at shot transitions and joined with the same fades. This is also ordinary editing and needs no separate operation.


First-batch flows:

| Flow | Steps |
| --- | --- |
| Transcribe | With a target: resolve target → optionally create a video and import the local media → transcribe (`transcribe` Job; the Job applies the speech document itself) → create the caption layer (skipped if a caption layer for the same transcript already exists). File only: transcribe (`jobs.submitFile`) → publish TXT and SRT to the save location; no video is created. The flow name `transcribe` shares its name with its transcription step: the flow is the parent Job (`kind: 'pipeline'`), and transcription is one of its child steps |
| Translate subtitles | freeze the source text → translate in `speech-worker` (each model call goes through `text.generate`) → check the translation → apply the translation document → create the target-language caption layer from the caption cues projected by the Worker |
| Translate subtitle file | read and strictly parse → translate in batches (`generateText`) → write out, read back, check cue count and timecodes → publish. File to file; does not touch video (`translate-subtitles`) |
| Dub | if the translation is missing, translate (same as "Translate subtitles", in `speech-worker`) → check the translation → optional vocal/background separation (`separateAudio`) → synthesize sentence by sentence (`synthesizeSpeech`) → time alignment → apply as a new dub group in one transaction |
| Link import | resolve target → resolve link → download media and optional subtitles → verify decodable → place in the download directory → optional new video → optional import (into a new or existing video) → optional transcription and caption layer |
| File transcode | compress or merge video files, or extract audio. File to file; no video is created |
| Identify speakers | freeze the transcript and version → in a Model Worker that has only the speaker-diarization model pack installed, distinguish speakers by voiceprint (`diarize`) → organize into a proposal (reuse existing speakers, trial sentence counts, re-split by translation). The video is not changed; after confirmation, one undoable edit is applied by `edits.applySpeakers` (`speakers`, §6.6) |

**The core of subtitles and translation** comes from CucCut v2 and is not rewritten for v3. Sentence splitting, source subtitle line breaking, translation pagination and validation, display splitting and alignment of translations, time projection of translated subtitles, subtitle export and quality checks are all implemented by the `speech-doc` crate (ported as-is from v2's `ccut-flow-core`, together with its test suite; design rationale in [docs/design/subtitle](../design/subtitle/)). There is only one set of rules; each side does not write its own:

- **Deterministic parts** (deriving sentences and cues, time projection, export, checks) are pure functions. VideoEngine links `speech-doc` directly; Node and the UI use the same implementation via WASM (`bindings/editor-wasm`; the TS side is `@cuccut/editor-wasm`). Source-caption line breaking was still hand-ported in Node (`caption-cues.ts`, and the UI's `speech-cues.ts`); it is deleted when the crate takes over. The parameters of the two versions match the crate's `CueParams::default()` except that they lack a minimum display duration. However, they have line breaks that the crate's `derive_cues` does not have: a cue longer than 7 seconds is split at stored sentence boundaries or at pauses longer than 1 second, and a whole segment without word timing is split into several blocks marked `~n`. Switching to the crate would change the caption cues and their IDs, so it is switched together with the source-caption layer.
- **The parts that call models** (polishing and segmentation, translation, alignment, targeted repair) are executed by a separate Worker process, `speech-worker`, in the same way as the Render Worker: one step of the flow starts one process, which exits when the step ends. Location: environment variable `CUCCUT_SPEECH_WORKER`; if it is not set, the `speech-worker` next to `engine-host` is used; if that is not found, the cargo output directory is searched (built together by `npm run build:engine`). The protocol is JSON lines `speech-worker/1` over stdio (command protocol spec §4.5): after `hello`, a `translate` request carries the frozen input and this step's staging. The Worker emits `llm.request`, `progress`, `checkpoint` and `resumed` events, and ends with the result of `translate` or an error. Closing stdin is equivalent to cancellation, and the process exits after cleanup. The Worker reads only the frozen input and writes only staging; it does not write the video directory. The crate itself drives multi-round calls and degradation (the whole page twice, splitting a page in half, redoing single units), and this retry semantics is kept as is. Currently only translation exists: the brief (skipped when the document fits in one page), paginated translation, the quality gate and redo, alignment, a final round, and then projecting the target-language caption cues back onto the transcript clock.
- **The model call path.** The Worker has no network access and holds no credentials; its environment does not contain them either. One call is one `llm.request` (`kind`, `attempt`, `system`, `user`, `temperature`, `maxOutputTokens`; the prompt and the file-contract envelope belong to the Worker, §13.6). The Runtime executes it via the flow's `text.generate`. Capability and Provider selection, authorization and budget reservation and settlement, call records, and the stop barrier all sit on this path. The Runtime removes temperatures the frozen model does not accept, clamps the output limit to the model's limit, and returns text or a classified failure as `llm.reply`. Output that does not meet the contract or is truncated is `malformed` (the core resends). Cancellation is `cancelled`. Everything else (missing authorization, over budget, task stopped, Provider refusal, and still unavailable after the bounded HTTP-layer retries, §6.4) is `terminal`. After a terminal failure the core makes no further calls and wraps up at once; this step fails with the Runtime-side error object as is, so details such as `pendingGrants` are not lost. One `llm.request` counts as one call; those with `attempt` greater than 0 are also counted as a retry; those returned as failures are counted as failures.
- **Checkpoints.** The Worker writes checkpoints in this step's staging (`checkpoint.json`, the crate's `PipelineStatus` wrapper). The keys are fingerprints of the source words, target language, style hints and glossary; if any of them changes, the whole checkpoint is discarded. The content is the target-language translation and alignment tables plus the settled brief, keyed by sentence ID. Each is written atomically after each completed page, after main alignment, and after the final round. After a failure, cancellation or interruption, the retry puts the tables back into the document before calling the engine again: sentences already translated whose source is unchanged are not resent, the brief is not redone, and an interruption after main alignment resumes from the final round (the fingerprint check is built into the crate). On cancellation the Runtime sends `cancel` and aborts in-flight calls; the Worker stops before the next call or backoff; then stdin is closed, and after a grace period the process is killed. Staging is deleted on completion and cancellation, per the flow's rules; it is kept on failure and interruption.
- **Wiring status.** The translation step of the `translate` flow (together with translation when dubbing lacks a translation) runs in the Worker through `runSpeechTranslate` in `@cuccut/jobs`. The `captions` step uses caption cues that the Worker projects back onto the transcript clock (cue IDs are `q-<unit ID>`, with timing matching the transcript); writing goes only through VideoEngine. Translating subtitle files (`translate-subtitles`) and the source-language caption layer have not yet moved to the crate.
- **Sentences and source fingerprints.** Only the crate's version is used (video format spec §5.3). `source_sentences` in `speech-doc-bridge` derives sentences from the words of the transcript body: a pause of at least 1.8 seconds or a semicolon breaks a sentence, and the `sentences` stored in the transcript body are not used. The fingerprint is `<word count>:<first word ID>:<last word ID>:<FNV-1a>`. The Worker links it directly. The translate flow freezes the source and checks whether the source has changed. Dubbing judges which units are stale. The content index counts stale units (Node), and the UI's sentence-by-sentence comparison (browser) calls the same implementation through `bindings/editor-wasm`. The WASM boundary is the JSON body of `cuccut.speech/1`; sentence start and end are integer ticks at the body's `timescale`, never floating-point seconds. When WASM is not built, the step in the translate and dub flows that uses sentences fails with `EDITOR_WASM_UNAVAILABLE` (run `npm run build:wasm` first). The content index only marks units stale according to each unit's own state count, and the UI treats the source text as unreadable.
- **Data model.** The crate internally uses v2's `TranscriptDoc`. It is a lossless two-way mapping with v3's `speech` and `translation` documents (`speech-doc-bridge`, pure functions; `speech-doc` itself does not depend on v3 crates). It is converted at the boundary between the Worker and VideoEngine. On read, the transcript is converted to `TranscriptDoc` together with the asset's facts. On write-back, the transcript that was read out is the base, and fields that `TranscriptDoc` cannot hold are carried back from the base by ID. The v3 document format gained the fields the mapping needs (video format spec §5.2, §5.3); the conversion between seconds and ticks follows video format spec §2.10.

**Dubbing** (`dub`) dubs a `speech` document in the video into another language. One dub group is a new dubbing track, the per-sentence dubbing instances on that track, and one dubbing plan (`dubbing-plan` document, video format spec §7.2), applied in one editing transaction. The flow calls the model itself and does not start an agent:

- **Parameters.** `videoId` (already open), or `target` (`{ videoId }`, `{ entryId }`; see "Tools" above). At least one of `targetLanguage` and `translationId` is given. When an existing translation is given, the language is taken from the translation; if a language is also given, it must match. In this case the translation parameters `textProvider`, `textModel`, `style`, `glossary`, `glossaries` and `batchSize` do not apply, and if they are given the request is rejected with `invalid-request`. Optional `documentId`: when omitted, the only `speech` document is used; when a translation is given, the translation's source is used. Synthesis uses `voice` (a preset voice, a voice ID in the account, or `library:<id>`; it applies only to speakers with no voice bound in the video, see "Sentence-by-sentence synthesis"), `provider` and `model`. Original audio `originalAudio`: `duck` (default; lowers it by `duckDb` dB, 1–60, default 18), `mute`, or `keep`. There is also an optional `separateBackground`.
- **At submission.** The speech synthesis Provider and model are selected and frozen, and the voice, language and format are checked against the model. WAV is preferred because alignment is computed in samples. `library:<id>` without a valid clone on this Provider gives `VOICE_CLONE_REQUIRED`; it is not swapped for another voice. A translation that is not `cuccut.translation/2`, that was translated from a different transcript, or whose language does not match is rejected. Outbound transfer needs a covering Grant; otherwise the request is rejected with `forbidden` and no task is created (a user present can grant it on the tools page on the spot, then resubmit; see "Tools" above). This covers sending the translation outbound to synthesis (data kind `transcript`), and, when the translation is missing, sending the source outbound for translation (also `transcript`). When the speech synthesis Provider is enabled, its default Grant does not include `transcript` (§12.5), so the first dub is usually rejected here. The rejection's `details.remedy` states the next step; `hint` says that dubbing must send the transcript outbound, that the default authorization does not include it, and to re-run after granting; `commands` is the command to run: `cuccut grants create --recipient <Provider> --data transcript --video <videoId> --purpose "<purpose>"`. `cuccut dub` prints it as is. The voice bound in the video to this transcript's speaker is read at submission and frozen with the parameters (bindings to another Provider are not used).
- **Translation.** When the translation is missing, the first four steps (read the source, translate, assemble, write) are the `translate` flow's steps (see "Translation" below), adding a new translation document. When `translationId` is given, it is used directly and not changed. Afterwards each sentence is checked. The following kinds are stale: they are not synthesized, and they are listed honestly in the result and in the dubbing plan (`DUB_UNITS_STALE`):
  - the unit is marked `stale` (`marked-stale`);
  - the source sentence is no longer in the transcript (`sentence-gone`);
  - the source sentence's fingerprint differs from the unit's `sourceFingerprint` (`source-changed`);
  - the library glossary recorded by the translation (`glossaryRef`, video format spec §5.3) has changed in a way that involves a term appearing in this source sentence (`glossary-changed`): the recorded rendering changed or was deleted, or a newly added term appears in the sentence. When the whole glossary is deleted, sentences containing its terms are stale. The comparison uses the current version in the library; glossaries passed directly in the call are not affected by the library;
  - the translation is empty (`empty`).

  When no sentences remain, `DUB_NOTHING_TO_DUB`.
- **Separation** (optional, §6.1). At submission, the separation model pack is selected and frozen per §6.2. When there is no executor, this step is `skipped`: the result is `separation: 'not-configured'` with the warning `DUB_SEPARATION_NOT_CONFIGURED`. If a separator was present at submission but is gone at execution, this step fails with `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`. The editor's dubbing settings have a "Separate background sound" switch (on by default when a usable separation model is available locally); like the CLI's `--separate-background`, it passes `separateBackground: true`. With `keep`, the original audio is left untouched and the two separated stems would have nowhere to go, so no separation is done (result `not-requested`). After successful separation, both `duck` and `mute` use the separated background; see "Application" below. Without separation and with `mute`, the music and ambient sound in the original audio are muted along with the speech, warning `DUB_BACKGROUND_MUTED`. Without separation and with `duck`, the whole original audio is lowered.
- **Sentence-by-sentence synthesis.** Each sentence is one sub-Job with one output, with at most 2 calls at the same time. Each call (including resends) goes through Grant and budget reservation and settlement (§7.8, §12.5). `library:<id>` is re-exchanged for the clone valid at that time on every call. Failures are handled by kind:
  - Failures that every following sentence would hit: authorization, budget, credentials, quota, capability or voice (`GRANT_*`, `BUDGET_*`, `PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED`, `PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, `CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `VOICE_*`, etc.; except a speaker-bound voice becoming invalid, see below). Once one occurs, no new calls are started and in-flight calls finish as usual. This step fails with that error code, and `details` carries the number of sentences synthesized and the number remaining.
  - Unavailability, timeout, rate limiting, and output that does not meet the contract: the sentence is resent once.
  - Other per-sentence failures are recorded first. After all sentences have run, the step fails with `DUB_SYNTHESIS_FAILED`.

  Successfully synthesized sentences are recorded in the staging manifest by digest of text, language and voice. A retry only synthesizes sentences not yet synthesized.
- **Choosing voice by speaker.** Each sentence's voice is taken in this order: the voice bound in the video to that sentence's speaker (`speakerVoices` of `library-selection`; only this transcript's bindings are considered, §5.9), the `voice` parameter, then the model's default voice. A bound voice is converted and checked at the synthesis step (including retries) one by one. `library:<id>` goes through the same checks as the parameter: the speaker's own declaration (`VOICE_CONSENT_REQUIRED`), a valid clone on this Provider (`VOICE_CLONE_REQUIRED`, `missing`, `stale`), and that it is still in the library (`VOICE_NOT_FOUND`). An unavailable binding is not swapped for another voice: that speaker's sentences are not synthesized and are listed one by one in the result (error code and reason). In the dubbing plan these units are `failed` (video format spec §7.2), with the warning `DUB_VOICE_UNAVAILABLE`. If a voice becomes invalid partway through synthesis, the remaining sentences of that speaker are not called. Other speakers are synthesized as usual; if no sentence can be synthesized, this step fails with the error code of the first cause. After the voice is fixed (re-cloned, declaration added), `pipelines.retry` re-checks and only synthesizes the sentences not yet synthesized. Muting and ducking of the original audio do not apply to instances that contain only such sentences. With `mute`, such sentences sit in the same instance as voiced sentences, so the original audio is muted along with them, warning `DUB_MUTED_UNVOICED`.
- **Time alignment** (`fit-fixed-slot`, video format spec §7.3). Each sentence's time window is one occurrence of the original sentence on the sequence, taken from the projection of the engine's text plan. When the same sentence has been cut into several adjacent segments, it counts as one occurrence; when it reappears after other sentences, it is dubbed again. A sentence entirely absent from the timeline is not placed (`DUB_UNITS_OFF_TIMELINE`). Checks run in order:
  1. Not longer than the window: play from the start of the original sentence, with no speed change.
  2. Longer than the window: speed up (ffmpeg `atempo`, preserving pitch), with the factor rounded up to the thousandth, at most 1.25.
  3. Still does not fit at the speed-up limit: it may occupy time up to the start of the next sentence (any sentence) or the end of the sequence.
  4. Still does not fit: not placed (`overlong`, `DUB_UNITS_OVERLONG`), reporting how many seconds it exceeds.

  It never truncates and never squeezes onto the next sentence; comparisons use measured sample counts. When no sentence can be placed, `DUB_NOTHING_PLACED`.
- **Application.** One transaction does the following:
  - Each audio is imported as `managed`. Provenance is `origin: 'generated'`, recording the flow and `jobId`, Provider, model, voice, language, units and factor.
  - A new audio track, "Dubbing (<language>)", is created.
  - Instances are inserted with `role: 'dub'`; `extensions['cuccut.dub']` records `groupId`, language and dub units; the asset references the import in the same transaction via `assetImportRef` (command and protocol spec §4.2).
  - The dubbing plan is written.
  - Original-sentence instances that carry sound are handled: `duck` adds a ducking, triggered by the dubbing track (referencing the new track via `trackRefs`) and targeting these instances; `mute` mutes them and records in the dubbing plan the instances muted by this action (`mutedItemIds`; instances already muted beforehand are not counted); `keep` leaves them alone.
  - When separation is in effect (`duck` or `mute`), original-sentence instances whose source is the transcript and that are voiced are replaced by the two separated stems. These instances are muted and recorded in `mutedItemIds`. A new track, "Background (<language>)", is created with a background segment at each instance's position, with source range, time map and volume identical to the original instance and not lowered, so the background, music and ambient sound between and during dubs are as loud as before. `duck` additionally creates a track, "Vocals (<language>)", placing the vocals the same way; the ducking target is this track (via `trackRefs`), not the original instance, so only the original vocals are lowered. The original instance is not lowered with background layered on top, because ducking is only effective while dubbing speaks, and background between sentences would be doubled. Embedded sound in video instances also cannot be replaced by other material, so the vocals go on a separate track. The `extensions['cuccut.dub']` of background and vocal instances is `{ groupId, stem }`; export of "original audio only", undo, and switching back to the original audio in the editor all follow the dubbing plan's `mutedItemIds` and this marker. Original-sentence instances whose source is not the transcript (for example, a separately placed recording) are handled as they are when there is no separation.

  On a version conflict, re-read and retry, at most 3 times. If the video is changed after alignment, the job ends with `STALE_JOB_INPUT`; a retry re-aligns against the current timeline, and synthesis is reused as before.
- **One dub group.** `groupId` is `dub_<parent Job ID with the job_ prefix removed>`. Each execution adds a new group; it does not replace existing dubbing tracks or given dubbing, nor swap given dubbing for speech synthesis. Export selects "only one dub group" by `groupId` (§9.13).
- **Result.** `summary` contains:
  - the language and the translation used (new or existing);
  - the dubbing plan, the dubbing track and the `groupId`;
  - sentence counts: total, placed, stale, off timeline, sped up, muted occupying, did not fit, voice unavailable;
  - stale, did-not-fit and voice-unavailable units;
  - the voice used per speaker, its source (`video`, `params`, `default`), availability and sentence count;
  - synthesis calls, resends, failures and reuses;
  - the glossary used when translation was needed;
  - the handling of the original audio and the separation status.
- **Entry points.** `pipelines.start` (`pipeline: 'dub'`) and the CLI `cuccut dub`. The agent and MCP service submit the whole flow in one step through the top-level verb `dub` (Agent surface design §4.2, §11). Scattered dubbing within a session is done by the agent with `speak` and editing transactions ("the entry decides the executor", §14). The web service does not provide it.

**Link import** (`link-import`) downloads run through the managed external tool yt-dlp (§12.9), outside the video transaction (§5.2):

- **Parameters**. `url`. The download target is one of a project, a conversation or a video (`projectId`, `conversationId`, `videoId` or `target`; none is required). `target.create` creates a new editable video in the source directory of its project or conversation; the download still uses the specified directory. If top-level `projectId` and `conversationId` are also given, they must match it. Optional: `audioOnly`, `subtitleLanguages`, `transcribe` (without a video target, generates a standalone transcript and subtitles). The transcription `language`, `provider`, `model` and `hint` are given only together with `transcribe`. `diarize` and `captions` (default false; builds the caption layer after transcription) also require a video target, with the same meaning as the same-named parameters in the `transcribe` flow. The provider and model are selected and frozen at submission. When transcribed into a video, the video's enabled transcription glossary is applied (not applied for externally served starts). The download directory is determined and frozen at submission: if `downloads.directory` is set, it is always used; otherwise it is the host's Downloads folder. A Runtime started by the desktop app uses the Downloads folder the system reports (recognising the known folder moved by Windows and the XDG directory on Linux); a Runtime started another way uses `~/Downloads` under the home directory. The project default only determines ownership and does not affect the save directory. With `saveTo: 'project'` (default `downloads`), files are saved to `downloads/` in the owning project (the project of `projectId`, `videoId`, or `target.create`); when the owner is a conversation that does not belong to a project, it is still the download directory. The UI and CLI do not provide `saveTo`. For the agent, `download` gives `saveTo` only when only `project` (download to project only) is given; for external service, `download` gives it whenever there is a destination (external service can only write into a project, §12.8). A nonexistent project or conversation is rejected with `not-found`.
- **Preconditions**. At startup and before each step, confirm that yt-dlp is installed, that its version is new enough, and that the user's consent is valid. After consent is withdrawn, retries are also rejected (`TOOL_*`). Rejected under strict offline mode (`OFFLINE_STRICT`). `transcribe` is rejected when transcription is not configured (`CAPABILITY_NOT_CONFIGURED`). Rejections at submission do not create a task.
- **Link checks**. Only `http(s)://` is accepted. Links that start with `-`, contain a username or password, or have no hostname are rejected with `LINK_UNSUPPORTED`. Hosts that are local, link-local or private-network addresses are rejected with `LINK_PRIVATE_ADDRESS`: literal IPv4 and IPv6 (including IPv4-mapped and NAT64 forms, and integer notation), `localhost`, local suffixes such as `.local`, and single-label hostnames without a dot. At submission the hostname is resolved again; if any address falls in these ranges it is rejected too. Resolution failure is `LINK_NETWORK_ERROR`. **These checks do not cover**: DNS changes between the resolution at submission and yt-dlp's own resolution; redirects that yt-dlp follows; and the interfaces and CDN addresses the extractor requests separately. These requests are made directly by yt-dlp and the Runtime does not handle them, so the link checks guard against typos and obvious misuse, not network isolation (§14).
- **Redaction**. Only the normalised link appears in storage, logs, task records, events, error details and Provenance: the hostname is lowercased, user info and fragments are removed, and of the query parameters only the identifying ones are kept: `v`, `list`, `index`, `t`, `start`, `p`, `page`, `id`, `bvid`, `aid` (sorted by name). All others (tracking parameters, signatures, tokens) are removed. The path is kept as is. Page addresses reported by yt-dlp and links in its error output are handled the same way. When normalisation changes the link, the original link exists only in `store/link-sources.json` (0600) under the Runtime Home. Frozen parameters reference it by `sha256:<digest>`, and the original link is what is given to yt-dlp. When the flow completes, that entry is deleted. At Runtime startup, entries no longer referenced by an unfinished flow that can still be retried are cleared; afterwards, retries fail with `LINK_SOURCE_EXPIRED`.
- **Execution**. Arguments are passed to the process as an array, not through a shell; the link comes after `--` as the last argument. Always pass `--ignore-config`, `--no-plugin-dirs` and `--no-playlist`; playlists and live streams are rejected with `LINK_UNSUPPORTED`. The output template is fixed to `dl/media.%(ext)s` in staging; the title does not go into the path. MP4 is the default: video and audio prefer MP4 and M4A streams, merged into MP4 (`-f bv*[ext=mp4]+ba[ext=m4a]/b[ext=mp4]/bv*+ba/b`). When only audio is wanted, M4A is preferred (`ba[ext=m4a]/ba/b`), so browsers and Electron can play it directly without first preparing a compatible copy. If the site offers none of these, it falls back to the best available format of any kind. The merge container tries MP4, WebM, MKV in turn (`--merge-output-format mp4/webm/mkv`), taking the first that can hold these codecs. Progress is the downloaded and total bytes yt-dlp reports using the template CucCut gives (`progress.unit: 'bytes'`), accumulated across several streams; when the total is unknown, no percentage is shown. yt-dlp runs in its own process group. On cancel, the whole group gets SIGTERM first and SIGKILL after a grace period, and the process is waited on until it exits.
- **Publish**. Only recognised media and subtitle extensions are published. File names come from the title: path separators, control and reserved characters are removed; the name does not start with a dot or `-`; maximum 120 characters; an empty name becomes `download`. Existing files are not overwritten; on a name clash a sequence number is added. The real path is checked to confirm it lies in the download directory. The download directory is created if missing; if it cannot be written, the error is `LINK_DESTINATION_UNAVAILABLE`. The result is a generated record without a video (`result.outputs`). `summary` carries the redacted link, title, platform, author, duration, files, tool and version, the browser cookie used (`cookieBrowser`, null when anonymous), download time, the imported video and assets, and the transcription task.
- **Import and transcription**. When `target` is given, the download is imported as a link asset, and the file stays in the save directory (the download directory by default, or the project's `downloads/` when `saveTo: 'project'`). `target` is the same as for the video tools (see “Tools” above): `{ videoId }`, `{ entryId }` or `{ create: { projectId | conversationId, name? } }`. `create` makes a new video and places the downloaded media on its timeline; the name defaults to the page title. The download-video UI offers no video target and no new video. The agent's and external service's `download` set the destination as one of three options (agent surface design §4.2): `video` (import into an existing video, the flow's `videoId`; if its timeline is still empty, with no clips on the root sequence, the same transaction places the asset on the main track, as for a new video; if it already has clips, only the asset is imported), `newVideo` (create new, the flow's `target.create`; may give `project` and `name`), and `project` (download to project only). When the agent imports into a video (`video`, `newVideo`), the download stays in the download directory, as in the UI and CLI, and the video's asset links there. When only `project` is given, `saveTo: 'project'` is given and the file goes into that project's `downloads/`. The destination for external service always gives `saveTo: 'project'`; the file goes into the owning project's `downloads/`. The agent's target is limited to the source directory of the conversation. `newVideo` and `videos_create` build in the same place: if the conversation belongs to a project, in that project (`projectId` defaults to it; another project answers `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND` and generates no approval); otherwise in the conversation's working directory (the flow parameter is `create: { conversationId }`), after which `videos_list` and `videos_inspect` can see it. Files of a conversation that does not belong to a project still go to the download directory. When the agent gives no destination it may also `transcribe`, generating a standalone transcript and subtitles. To place a download in a video afterwards, import it with `importAsset` in `edits_apply`, using the media's `artifactId` from the result (§3.5). Provenance records `origin: 'link-import'` and `source`: the redacted link and page address, platform, media ID, title, author, publish date, duration, the page description (`description`, up to 20000 characters) and the chapters the platform gives (`chapters`, cleaned `[{ start, end?, title }]`, up to 400 entries; omitted when absent), the tool's name, version and source, the download time, and the flow's `jobId` (video format spec §4.5). `summary` also carries `description` truncated to 1200 characters and the chapter count `sourceChapters`. Subtitles are kept only as files beside the media and are not imported into the video. When no `target` is given, the download is made and optionally transcribed directly; the result is a video file, a transcript and subtitles in Space. When a target is given, `transcribe` submits transcription after the import and waits for it to finish; the summary's `documentId` is the speech document written. When `captions` is true, the final `captions` step builds the caption layer from it (same step as in the `transcribe` flow; the summary's `captions` has the same shape).
- **Adopting source chapters**. The platform's chapters and the timestamp outline in the description were written by the author. `videos_inspect` lists them in the asset's `source` (description truncated to 1200 characters; chapters not snapped). The agent uses `chapters_adopt` (§3.5) to turn them into the video's chapters. Parsing and snapping are in the subtitle and translation core (`bc_source_chapters`, same rules as the older version), and all times are source times of the asset:
  - Source chapters: an explicit outline (`outline`) takes priority. Otherwise take structured `chapters[]` first (recognising `start_time | start`, `end_time | end`, `title`); when absent, parse a timestamp outline from the description (at least two lines).
  - Snapping: the author's timestamps are only accurate to the second and often come a few seconds before the topic really starts, so each t snaps to the nearest structural start in the transcript, with levels paragraph > sentence > cue > word. First search, in order, for paragraph, sentence, then cue starts within the near window `[t-1, t+2]` seconds (both ends included). The first level with a candidate decides the result: one candidate is `matched`; several is `ambiguous` (take the one nearest t, with ties going to the one after t; keep at most 4 candidates). If none is found, widen to `[t-4, t+6]` seconds, accepting only paragraph and sentence starts, nearest first (`snapped`). If still none, look for word starts in the near window (again `matched` or `ambiguous`). If none, keep the original time (`unanchored`). With no transcript, or when snapping is off, everything is `unanchored`.
  - Cleaning: drop empty titles, sort by start, keep one entry per start time, clamp the first chapter to 0, and drop any that are not strictly increasing. The end time is the start of the next chapter; the last chapter ends at the asset's duration.
  - Projection (Runtime): source times are converted to timeline seconds through the asset's enabled clips on the root sequence (only linear time mapping is recognised). When the same asset segment is placed several times, take the earliest on the timeline. Chapters falling in cut-away parts are marked `offTimeline` and recorded as `unanchored`, and fall to the nearest clip start after the source time (or, if there is none after, the nearest one before). Align to frames; clamp the first chapter to 0; drop any that land on the same frame as the previous chapter (the receipt's `dropped`).
  - Compiled into a single `setChapters` (titles and times, no summaries), replacing all existing chapters in one undoable change; `dryRun` returns only the plan. `NO_SOURCE_CHAPTERS` when the source has no chapters; `ASSET_NOT_PLACED` when the asset is not on the timeline (command and protocol spec §4.2).
- **Failures and retries**. The failure reasons form a closed set, each with a `remedy`: `LINK_UNSUPPORTED`, `LINK_LOGIN_REQUIRED`, `LINK_COOKIES_UNAVAILABLE`, `LINK_TOOL_UPDATE_REQUIRED`, `LINK_UNAVAILABLE` (deleted, region-restricted), `LINK_NETWORK_ERROR`, `LINK_DISK_FULL`, `LINK_DOWNLOAD_FAILED`, `LINK_DOWNLOAD_UNREADABLE`, `LINK_DESTINATION_UNAVAILABLE`, `LINK_SOURCE_EXPIRED`, plus the tool's `TOOL_*` (command and protocol spec §11.3). On cancel, staging is deleted. On failure and interruption it is kept, and `pipelines.retry` lets yt-dlp resume from `.part`.
- The download tool is not distributed with the app. On first use, explain the tool's name, source, version, size and licence; it is downloaded only after the user consents (§12.9). Cookies are off by default. The user ticks one or more browsers for this download (`cookieBrowsers`, in the order to try), which allows yt-dlp to read those browsers' cookies on this machine and use them to access the target site. The UI lists only browsers detected on this machine (`externalTools.cookieBrowsers`). Following yt-dlp's lookup rules, it checks whether each browser's cookie store exists (for Chromium-based browsers, `[<Profile>/][Network/]Cookies` under the data directory; Firefox, `cookies.sqlite`; Safari, `Cookies.binarycookies` at a fixed location; if permission to look is missing, the browser is still listed, with unknown time) and its modification time, most recently changed first. It only `stat`s; it does not open or read contents and does not run the download tool. When resolving a link, each browser is tried in order: if that browser's cookies cannot be read (`LINK_COOKIES_UNAVAILABLE`) or the site still requires login (`LINK_LOGIN_REQUIRED`), the next one is tried; other failures fail directly. When none succeeds, `LINK_LOGIN_REQUIRED` is reported if any browser reported a login requirement, otherwise `LINK_COOKIES_UNAVAILABLE`; `details.attempts` holds each browser's result. The browser that is used is recorded in the resolve step's result, and download reuses it. A download failure does not switch browsers; a retry continues from download, still using it. Finally, no extra attempt without cookies is made: ticking a browser means the user wants the login information used. Only the browser name is stored, not cookie contents; unticked browsers are never read. The earlier form that gave a single `cookieBrowser`, and frozen parameters in that form, are still accepted, equivalent to a list containing only that browser. Downloading does not mean the user has a licence to use the content; the UI shows the notice per product design §5.9.

**File transcoding** does not go through the video render pipeline. Input and output are both media files, executed by `media-core` or the managed ffmpeg; the executor is reported in the result. Stream copy is used only when the clips' encoding parameters truly match; otherwise the media is re-encoded and this is stated.

- Steps: read input (ffprobe) → encode → verify output (duration, video track; when re-encoding, also the codec and height) → publish. The executor is ffmpeg on PATH (`CUCCUT_FFMPEG` / `CUCCUT_FFPROBE` may specify it). The result reports its version and a parameter summary of the actual command (paths reduced to file names). The output reports the final published file name, including any sequence number added on a clash, not the temporary name in staging.
- **Stream-copy criteria**. Stream copy is possible only for merging. Every clip must have video, with matching video codec, resolution, frame rate and pixel format. Either all or none have audio, and where they do, audio codec, sample rate and channels must match. The codecs must also go directly into MP4 (video H.264, HEVC; audio AAC, MP3, ALAC). If any item does not match, re-encode; `reason` lists which parameters differ. The picture is scaled to the first clip's size with letterboxing, frame rate and sample rate are unified, and clips without audio get silence. Compression always re-encodes.
- Output is written to the chosen directory (`outDir`), by default the save location (see “Save location” above). It never overwrites existing files; on a clash it adds a sequence number. The output is recorded as a generated record without a video (`result.outputs` carries the output file path).
- **Extract audio** (`action: 'extract-audio'`). One output per input. When there is an audio track, the video is dropped. Streams whose audio codec is AAC, MP3, Opus or FLAC are stream-copied into the matching container (`.m4a`, `.mp3`, `.ogg`, `.flac`); others are re-encoded to AAC (`.m4a`) and this is stated. An input with no audio track fails with `TRANSCODE_NO_AUDIO`. Verification checks only the duration and the audio track.

**Translation** turns a `speech` document into a new `translation` document (video format spec §5.3), sentence by sentence, executed by `speech-worker` (see “Subtitle and translation core” above). Paging, resending, back-filling and alignment belong to the crate. The parameter `batchSize` is still accepted but no longer used. When the source and target languages share the same primary subtag, the request is rejected at start (for example `zh` to `zh-Hant`). If sentences are still missing after back-filling, this step fails with `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID` (`details` carries the missing and translated sentence counts). The checkpoint is kept and no half-finished document is written; a retry does not redo translated pages. Each execution adds a new translation document and does not replace existing ones, since they may have manual edits (§10.3). If the source document changes during execution, the write step fails with `STALE_JOB_INPUT`; a retry restarts from the current source.

- **In-place refresh** (parameter `translationId`, optional `sentences`). No new translation is made. Only the sentences to be updated in an existing translation are re-translated, and the result is written as a new version of the same translation document. The source is this translation's `sourceDocumentId`. `targetLanguage` must match the translation's language, and it cannot be given together with `captions`, `captionStyle` or `bilingual`. Sentences to re-translate: when none are named, those marked stale (`status: 'stale'`), those whose source fingerprint differs from the unit's, empty translations, and sentences that are in the transcript but have no unit in the translation yet (the same rule the editor uses to judge staleness). When sentences are named, those are re-translated even if fresh (up to 500 sentences). Units whose source sentence is no longer in the transcript are all removed when none are named; when named, only units sharing source words with the named sentences are removed. When there is nothing to do, or named sentences are not in the transcript, the request is rejected at start with `invalid-request`. The read of the source at this step also freezes the translation's version. The Worker uses it as the base and sends only these sentences; no translation brief is made, and `glossaryRef` is carried over from the base (command protocol spec §4.5). The assembly step merges these sentences back into the original translation: unit IDs and order stay the same, new sentences' units are inserted after the preceding sentence's unit, other units are not changed at all, and only the re-translated sentences are checked. The write is one transaction. It writes the new version of the translation, plus, in each caption document generated from this translation (`cuccut.translationCues`), the caption cues for the re-translated and removed sentences. The cues are cut by the Worker, with times taken from the member words of the original sentence; other cues are untouched (the same rule as the editor's in-place change of one translation sentence). Undo reverts both together. If the transcript or this translation changes during execution, the write fails with `STALE_JOB_INPUT`, the video is unchanged, and a retry picks sentences again from the current version. The summary gains a `refresh` item: the version written, the re-translated sentences, the removed units, the caption documents updated along with it, and that transaction. When the agent translates itself and uses `documents_put` to change an existing translation, the Runtime likewise replaces the caption cues of the changed sentences in the same edit, cutting them by sentence-level alignment. The receipt's `syncedCaptions` is the number of caption documents updated.

- **Glossaries**. Terms come, in order, from the `glossary` given directly at call time, from the glossaries in the library given at call time (`glossaries: [{ id, version? }]`, up to 20), and from the translation glossary enabled in the video (§5.9). For the same source spelling (case-insensitive), only the first occurrence is taken. When a library glossary given at call time does not fit (not in the library: `not-found`; one used for transcription, or a language mismatch or duplicate: `LIBRARY_ENTRY_NOT_APPLICABLE`), the request is rejected at start and no task is created. When the video's enabled glossary has been deleted or its language does not match, it is skipped and listed in the summary's `skipped` (`removed`, `language`). Languages match by BCP 47 subtag prefix (`en` matches `en-US`; `zh-Hans` does not match `zh-Hant`). Clients of external service cannot use library glossaries, and the video's enabled glossary is not read for them.
- **Freezing and pinning**. At start, each glossary's version and `contentHash` are frozen and recorded on the parent task's `library.entries`, and pinned to the parent task until the flow ends. The glossary contents are written to a snapshot artifact before the first step. Afterwards the library is not read again; retries also read this snapshot. After a Runtime restart the glossaries are pinned again, skipping versions already cleaned up.
- **Into the prompt**. After de-duplication, all terms are passed to the Worker and written into the system message of each translation call, as preferred renderings (not locked). They are not truncated by batch; the summary's `cappedBatches` is always 0. The prompt and file-contract version is the Worker protocol version `speech-worker/1`, recorded in the translation's `engine.promptVersion`.
- **Recording**. The translation document's `glossaryRef` (video format spec §5.3) records the library glossaries used with their versions, the digest and count of terms given directly at call time, and the terms that appear in the source with the renderings used at the time. Voice-over checking uses this to tell which sentences are stale because the glossary changed (`glossary-changed`). The summary's `glossary` lists the glossaries used (source `explicit` or `video`), those skipped, the term counts and `cappedBatches`.
- **Subtitle files** (`translate-subtitles`). Input is an SRT or WebVTT file. Output is a new subtitle file with the same number of entries and the same timecodes, recorded as a generated record without a video and entering Space (§5.7). No video is created and no document is written. The data category sent out is again `transcript` (authorisation without a video); terms come only from those given at call time, not from any glossary enabled in a video. A file that cannot be read correctly is rejected at start, before a task is created (`SUBTITLE_FILE_INVALID`, `SUBTITLE_FILE_TOO_LARGE`). Parsing is strict: rejecting is preferred to guessing times. Encodings (BOM, UTF-16, GBK), line endings, WebVTT headers and blocks, cue settings, multi-line text and style tags are tolerated; there are limits (4 MiB, 10000 entries, 1000 characters per entry). Each entry only has its text replaced; nothing is merged, split or re-segmented. One entry is one model sentence, matched back by ID. After writing, the output is re-parsed and its entry count and start and end times are compared with the original file (for the same format, the timing lines are compared verbatim). On a mismatch this step fails with `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID` and nothing is published. Inline markup is not carried into the translation. When converting WebVTT to SRT, cue settings and blocks that do not fit are counted and warned about. Bilingual output puts the original above and the translation below in the same entry. Output is written to the chosen directory (`outDir`), by default the save location (see “Save location” above). The name is `<source file name>.<target language>[.bilingual].<format>`, and existing files are never overwritten. The contract is in the `pipelines` section of the command protocol spec.
- **Caption layer**. After the translation is written, the `captions` step, when the parameter `captions: true` is given, builds a caption layer in the target language, following the rules in the caption-layer part of “Transcription” below. `bilingual` (default false) can be given only when `captions: true`. The flow parameter does not build by default. The tool entry (the video input of `translate-subtitles` in the tool directory carries it in `execution.params`), the CLI, and the editor's “Translate into…” (together with the user's chosen `bilingual`) pass `captions: true`. When it is not given, this step is skipped, as it is when the step does not exist. The summary's `captions` states whether a layer was built and why. When newly built, it carries the `transactionId` of the transaction that built the layer, which the editor uses to undo. For a flow record created before this step existed, retries align steps by name: the new step is back-filled as `pending` and decided by the frozen parameters. CLI: `cuccut translate` builds by default; `--no-captions` skips it; `--bilingual` makes it bilingual.

**Transcription** (`transcribe`) is the backend of the transcription tool. It shares its name with the transcription Job submitted by `models.transcribe`, but they are not the same thing: the flow is the parent Job, and its “Transcribe” step submits a `transcribe` Job (`submitter` is this flow) and waits for it to finish.
