| Coverage of task protection (§3.2) | Whether results of app background Jobs (transcript writes, generated-asset imports) and flows writing video are also checked against their task's protection | Decided: the same checks apply; rejected Job results are kept as candidates, and the user decides via `jobs.reconcile apply`; the flow stops at the video-writing step (§3.2) |
| Scope of entity protection (§3.2) | Whether entity protection extends to child entities (instances on a track, paragraphs in a document) | Does not include child entities; to protect content on a track, use `interval` plus `trackIds` |
| Budget for in-process text generation (§7.8) | Whether calls inside the agent Provider, or other in-process `generateText` calls later, go through the ledger each time | Only fixed-flow translation (each model call of the Speech Worker) goes through the ledger; all Jobs go through the ledger |
| Authorization records for fixed-flow steps (§7.9) | Whether flow step records carry `grant` as Jobs do | No: usage is recorded on the Grant, and step records carry none |
| Service-reported costs (§7.8) | Which online Provider responses carry costs and how to convert them to amounts | None report in the first release: completed calls settle as estimated or amount unknown |
| `context` data kind (§12.5) | How outbound agent conversation context is authorized through a Grant, and how the Driver reports it | Kept only in the model; not wired to the Driver |
| Long-term allow rules (§3.12) | In the prototype, the approval card's "always allow this command prefix": what rules match on, scope (session, project, global), storage and revocation | Access modes decided (§3.12); long-term rules undecided |
| Details of access modes (§3.12) | When the Driver's own permission settings catch up after a mid-task switch; whether local-model generation counts as `edit` or `command`; whether a tool's "don't ask again this task" persists; time limit for Codex tool calls; whether the old UI sends one of three levels with every message; the Web service's browser subscribes to the `tasks` topic and sees approvals for the external service (client name and summary) | The Driver switches only when the native session is rebuilt at the next message; local-model generation counts as `edit`; kept in memory only; `tool_timeout_sec` one day (per the Codex docs config key, not verified against the real binary); sessions that sent messages in the old UI are fixed to the level chosen then (default `ask`), removed when the UI migrates to four levels; the browser sees external-service approvals but cannot act on them (`approvals.*` is not on the Web allowlist), so before the external-service admin surface moves into the browser, it must be filtered by connection |
| Export draft `exports.updateDraft` (§9.11, §9.13) | Whether the draft lives in the video or the Runtime; per video or per sequence; sync with the UI export panel | Deferred: the caller supplies everything in `exports.create`. The output frame rate is in the `video` kind's `ExportSettings` |
| Other forms of portable package (§5.8) | Folder form; pax extensions (over-long paths, files over 8 GiB), compression and signing; linked packages (manifest `linked` / `excluded-license`, receiver relinks by digest) | Only a single-file, uncompressed, unsigned ustar `.cuccut`, with all assets packed in; preflight rejects anything that cannot be written as ustar |
| Portable packages and local records (§5.8) | Documents whose body contains local paths (e.g., future export records, import records): strip, rewrite, or reject when packing | Preflight rejects item by item (`EXPORT_PACKAGE_LOCAL_PATH`); these two kinds of record are not yet implemented |
| Staging when opening a portable package (§5.8) | The staging directory sits in the source directory, and if the process is force-killed it leaves dot-prefixed hidden directories; whether to move it to the Runtime Home and clean up at startup | Decided: it stays in the source directory (on the same volume as the new video, so it is renamed in place on completion), and Space does not list it; startup residue cleanup deletes staging directories that are old enough and not in use, plus hidden temp files from export (§5.8 "Residue cleanup") |
| Remainder of project export (§9.13) | Copy-style (assets copied beside the project); FCPXML and CapCut drafts; text, subtitles, code frames and effects rendered to media first and then referenced; mapping of transitions and volume envelopes | Only `xmeml`, reference-only; items it cannot express produce a per-item warning `PROJECT_ITEM_OMITTED` |
| Fonts and typesetting for final-cut export (§9.11) | Whether fonts go into the frozen snapshot (checked by digest); whether Chinese and Japanese line breaking needs dictionary segmentation to match the preview; colour emoji and bidirectional text | Decided: fonts shipped with the render kernel travel with the binary and do not enter the snapshot; local fonts in use are frozen per face: preflight runs through the text and reports the faces used, the Runtime picks them on the local machine and records in the frozen snapshot the file, the face index, and the digest of the whole file; at execution the Worker verifies the digest and loads only those faces; if they changed it fails with `FONT_MISSING`; faces not found at freeze time are drawn with a fallback font and a warning is issued (§9.11 "Fonts"). Preview and export use the same parsing and face-extraction method (§9.1). Shaping, line breaking and bidirectional text live in the kernel and are identical on both sides. On-demand font download (§9.1): families in the font catalog but not present locally are downloaded during the export job and frozen the same way; if they are not downloaded or the download fails, they are drawn with the fallback font and a `FONT_NOT_DOWNLOADED` warning is issued. On opening a video, the fonts to download are inventoried across the whole film (`fonts.usage`, same single typesetting pass as export preflight, §9.1); if the download fails, export proceeds directly with the fallback font and gives a warning. Undecided: whether to include colour emoji fonts (none in the kernel's bundled fonts now); how brand-library fonts are injected; the browser session has no `fonts.*`, so preview uses only the bundled fonts |
| Output aspect ratio for final export (§9.11) | When the export aspect ratio differs from the sequence canvas: add black bars, crop, or re-lay out to the new ratio | Decided: add black bars. The picture is scaled to the largest size that fits the canvas ratio, centred, with the remainder black; layout scales with the canvas and is not re-laid out (rules in the command and protocol spec §4.4). The UI export panel offers 16:9, 9:16, 1:1, 4:5, defaulting to the canvas; the CLI and agent export tools both accept width and height |
| Content final export cannot render (§9.11) | When built-in generators (waveforms, progress bars, etc.) and code-package compositions get native drawing | Refused and listed item by item; skipped if the user agrees; GIF animation and SVG are already drawn in the kernel (§9.1) |
| Child processes when the Render Worker is force-killed (§9.11) | No process groups on Windows; when Runtime and Worker are both force-killed, who reaps ffmpeg | On POSIX the Runtime kills the Worker's process group (tested); on Windows only the Worker is killed, and ffmpeg lives on until it fails on reading or writing the pipe; when both are force-killed, ffmpeg likewise exits on pipe breakage, with no separate watchdog. The same applies to links imported via yt-dlp: on Windows, cancelling only ends yt-dlp, and the ffmpeg it started to merge audio and video may linger a little longer |
| Publishing exports on volumes without hard links (§9.11) | On volumes such as exFAT, "confirm absent, then rename" has a short race window; whether to switch to exclusive creation followed by streamed copy | Accept the window; simultaneous exports of the same filename into the same directory are rare |
| Audio export vs. preview differences (§9.11) | When the volume envelope is applied to both; whether preview also amplifies when gain is above 0 dB; how to fill when crossfade handles are insufficient (asset shorter than registered) | Decided: volume envelope, fades, crossfades and ducking use one shared plan on both sides (§9.11). With gain above 0 dB, export applies the plan and preview does not amplify; the part without handles is muted; the difference is written into the task's warnings |
| Remote download and remote export (§6.7) | In the prototype, nodes could also download videos and export videos on behalf. Download requires the node to actively access the external network; export requires sending the video's frozen inputs to another machine | Not done; nodes share only model capabilities, each capability toggled separately by the node owner (node protocol spec §10) |
| First batch of text-model Providers (§6.1) | Which providers `generateText` supports first | Decided: OpenAI, Google, and the OpenAI-compatible endpoint `custom:<slug>` |
| Web service (§4.8) | Capability set of the first browser client release; whether editing and export are allowed, or read-only and sessions only | Decided: in product scope; listens on loopback only, default port 47622. The first-release capability set is the desktop UI protocol minus changes to settings, model-service configuration and credentials, local model-package install management, external-service management (including issuing access links), node pairing and sharing, and arbitrary-path `projects.open` (may only open registered projects or create new ones in the default project directory); query, sessions, editing, transcription and generation, and export are available. The allowlist lives in the service config and can be tightened to read-only. Writes from the browser count as the user themselves (`user_local`), not `external:web`. Choosing directories, importing assets by local path, and "show in folder" are unavailable in the browser; session image and file attachments are served via a same-origin upload address bound to the session (§4.8), and no arbitrary local path is exposed. Access links are issued only by the CLI (`cuccut web open`); an entry point from the desktop UI will be added later |
| Routing of the model-API service (§4.8) | Whether external requests may be forwarded to online Providers or nodes; default contents of the alias table | Decided: by default only to `local`; online Providers, nodes and agent Providers each have a switch, default off; a category that is off does not appear in `/v1/models`, and requests by name get 404; the alias table by default only maps `whisper-1` → the default local transcription model package; loopback `127.0.0.1:47621`; tokens are issued per client and are not interchangeable with the MCP service's; `stream: true` returns as SSE with one content block; images only `b64_json` |
| MCP service tokens, approvals and defaults (§4.8, §12.8) | Whether tokens are issued per service or per client, and whether they survive restarts; approval time limit; default port, level and scope; in-flight Jobs when the service stops | Decided: loopback `127.0.0.1:47620`; tokens issued per client, plaintext shown once, only a salted hash stored, valid across restarts, individually revocable; approval 50 s, timeout counts as reject; default `ask` and all videos; stopping the service disconnects and cancels pending approvals, while submitted Jobs run to completion. Connections without a token are undecided and not provided in the first release |
| Retention of fixed-flow records (§7.9) | When the task ledger trims old records by count, whether child Jobs count; whether a failed flow can be pushed out of the ledger, making it impossible to retry later | Decided: only top-level Jobs are counted (200 entries), and child Jobs follow their parent; the latest 50 retryable flows are kept in addition |
| Engine-side stop barrier (§7.4) | How the `runGeneration` invalidation control is passed to the Engine Host via a private channel, and checked at the video's serial commit entry | Decided: on cancel, `runs.invalidate` invalidates this execution. The Engine Host rejects at the commit entry with `TASK_STOPPED`, without opening a transaction; Node records it as application `cancelled`. `tasks.stop` waits for the barrier to confirm, at most 5 s. Remaining gaps: if the Engine Host restarts, invalidation records are lost; per video, when over 1024 executions, the oldest is dropped; both fall back to the Node-side barrier, and the new Runtime does not re-register them. Task and Run are not implemented; the execution generation is the task's attempt sequence number. Transcription tasks submitted by fixed flows run separately; cancelling the parent task invalidates them by cancelling the child task |
| Queryable remote tasks (§7.5) | After an online Provider gives a remote task ID, how to fetch results later | Interface only (`RemoteTaskQuery`): existing online adapters are all one-shot synchronous requests with no remote task ID. Any `pending`, `running` or `succeeded` result is treated as `needs-reconciliation`; no fetching |
| Online calls still running on normal stop (§2.4, §7.5) | Whether to wait for online requests to return before exit | Do not wait: mark `needs-reconciliation`, and the budget is conservatively charged. Each time a user exits with an online generation still running, reconciliation is required |
| Crash window between publish and apply (§7.3) | If it crashes after the artifact is written to the artifact store but before results and application are booked, how to claim the artifact | Decided: before writing the artifact, the publish intent is first recorded in the task ledger. On restart, the artifact is checked and claimed: no re-inference, no re-sent requests, budget settled once. Remaining gaps: exports and fixed flows' own artifacts do not go through publish intent; if the intent is recorded but the artifact has not finished writing when it crashes, the task still recovers as "running". Files in the artifact store with no reference are deleted by the background sweep (§7.3) |
| Release of artifact references (§5.7, §7.3) | Artifact references left by tasks the ledger trimmed (dubbing sentence audio etc., which video documents still point to): when they may be released; with a continuous queue the sweep is postponed forever; cache evicts by modification time, hits do not refresh; `content-index/`, which is not deleted, also counts toward the cache cap | Undecided. For now references only grow, and artifacts before the sweep starting point are all kept |
| Durability of the ledger (§7.3, §7.8) | Whether task, application and grant ledgers fsync after writes; whether a reserved "started" is persisted before execution begins | Decided: all three ledgers fsync files and directories; "started" is persisted before data is handed out. Queued tasks that were reserved as already started at restart are reconciled by outcome as unknown. Remaining gaps: on macOS Node's fsync is not `F_FULLFSYNC`, so on power loss data may still be in the disk cache; the artifact store and other files are not fsynced |
| Scope of retry and resume (§7.5) | Which tasks may be re-queued or `retry`; whether local segmented tasks can resume from a checkpoint | Only transcription and generation with video. Not possible for: transcription without video (model-API service), node-executed tasks, fixed-flow steps (use `pipelines.retry`), and tasks that do not go through a model. No resume from staging; local tasks always restart from scratch |
| Video not open during reconciliation (§7.5) | Whether `retry` and `apply` open the video on the user's behalf | No: rejected with `VIDEO_NOT_OPEN`; the user opens it first. Only restart recovery reopens at the recorded position |
| Duplicate writes after an interrupted fixed flow (§7.9) | If interrupted while writing the video or publishing a file, how a retry recognises what was already written | Not recognised: a retry redoes the step, which may produce an extra translated document or a numbered output file |
| Scope of link import (§7.9) | Supported platform list; whether consent for login state is given per use or remembered per site | Decided: in product scope; the download tool is not distributed with the app and is downloaded after user consent; reading the browser login state needs user consent. Each download explicitly selects a browser; it is not auto-enabled per site |
| Release manifest for managed external tools (§12.9) | yt-dlp version, size and sha256 per platform file, licence wording | Version 2026.07.04 taken from the stable release installed with Homebrew on the development machine, not verified against upstream; file names and licence follow its bundled README. Size and sha256 were not verified offline and are left empty, so under the built-in manifest `externalTools.install` always rejects with `TOOL_MANIFEST_INCOMPLETE`; no digests are fabricated; before release, a person fills them in by checking against upstream checksum files |
| Signing of managed external tools (§12.9) | Whether to verify upstream release signatures | First release only checks the size and sha256 in the manifest; signatures are not verified |
| Link import in the external service (§4.8, §12.9) | Whether the MCP service offers tool installation and link import | Decided: tool installation and consent are not offered, because they download and execute third-party programs and external-service approval is insufficient to establish source, version and licence; provided only in the desktop UI, the CLI, and the agent tools in sessions. From interface version 2 onward, download from link (`download`) is offered: only after the user has installed yt-dlp and consented, it downloads an existing video within scope or a registered project, subject to service approval (`ask`) by tool risk; if not installed or not consented, it is rejected outright. Later, `newVideo` (and `url` for `transcribe`) will be opened up: they must also give a registered `project`, and a video created by the flow is registered into the service's scope (Agent surface design §11) |
| First-release scope of link import (§7.9) | Whether to create new videos; when reading the browser login state is implemented | Decided: opened up. `link-import` accepts `target` and can create a new video, write into an existing video, or only download; the Runtime and the `download` tool in the catalogue (shared by agent and CLI) already support this; after transcription into a video, a caption layer (`captions`, created by default when `transcribe` is given `url`) can be built. The download UI only downloads files, with optional transcription; it supports ticking one or more locally detected browser cookie sources, tried in order one by one; a read failure is reported as `LINK_COOKIES_UNAVAILABLE` for handling |
| Network requests of the download tool itself (§7.9) | Whether to restrict yt-dlp's requests to the public internet (pin the resolution, check each request via a local proxy) | Only the link and its resolution are checked at submission time; DNS changes, redirects, and addresses requested separately by extractors are outside the check's scope |
| Sticker assets fetched from the network | The prototype pulls the dynamic sticker catalogue and files from a community site. Licensing, verification and caching of the source | Only use those shipped with the app and those the user uploads themselves |
| Cloud account | The prototype's privacy page has "delete cloud account data"; this document has no CucCut cloud account of its own | No cloud account exists; this item is not done |
| Exchange format of user-library entries (§5.9) | Concrete formats and versions of voice packages and glossary files | Decided: a glossary is a Markdown table with front matter (`cuccut.glossary` version 1); a voice package is JSON with embedded recordings (`cuccut.voice-package` version 1, ≤ 32 MiB); colours and caption styles are `cuccut.library-item` version 1 (§5.9) |
| Overlay templates in the brand library (§5.9) | Content format, parameters for templates, and what entity they become once copied into the video | Only the kind `overlayTemplate` is kept: `library.put` rejects it with `LIBRARY_KIND_RESERVED`; opened once the video format defines templates |
| Enabling user-library entries in a video (§5.9) | At which step of the video which glossaries are used, and which speaker uses which voice, recorded where in the video; how new videos adopt `defaultEnabled` | Decided: a `library-selection` document in the video (video format spec §4.6) records entry IDs, not content, read and written via `library.getVideoSelection` / `setVideoSelection`; new videos get the default-enabled glossary written by `system:library`, and it is not on the undo stack. Transcription, translation and dubbing use it when a call does not explicitly give one; the glossary version used for translation is recorded in the translation's `glossaryRef` (§5.3) |
| Creation and deletion of voice clones (§5.9) | When and via which method reference recordings are uploaded to the Provider to create a clone; after the entry is deleted or the recording replaced, how the provider is asked to delete the old clone | Decided: `library.createVoiceClone` is a `voiceClone` task; `library.removeVoiceClone` deletes remotely first, then clears the record; after re-cloning, deletion of the replaced old clone is requested, with a warning if it cannot be deleted. Deleting an entry does not automatically delete the remote clone; the user calls `removeVoiceClone` |
| Details of the ElevenLabs cloning API (§6.4) | Written from public docs and could not be verified offline: the status code returned when deleting a voice the remote no longer has; whether a clone with `requires_verification` true can be used for synthesis immediately; per-file and total upload size limits | A 404 on delete is treated as "remote no longer has it"; clones needing verification are still recorded as valid, and synthesis failure is reported honestly; reference recordings are bound only by the library limit (20 MiB) |
| Crash during clone upload (§5.9, §7.5) | If the Runtime crashes after upload but before recording `voiceId`, the remote may hold an unrecorded extra clone | Task interrupted, not re-queued, and the remote is not queried; the user deletes the extra clone on the provider's side |
| Default authorization for dubbing outbound (§7.9, §12.5) | When a speech-synthesis Provider is enabled, whether the default Grant covers `transcript` | Decided: it does not. ElevenLabs' default Grant has only `document`; dubbing hands the user's transcript outside, so under outbound minimisation and explicit consent the user issues a separate Grant covering `transcript`. Without it, `pipelines.start` and `cuccut dub` reject with `forbidden` (`GRANT_REQUIRED`), with `remedy` giving the `cuccut grants create … --data transcript` command to run and a note to re-run after granting; a desktop UI rejection also carries pending items to approve (`pendingGrants`, see "on-the-spot authorization in the tools page"); CLI and external services are unchanged |
| Executor for vocal/background separation (§6.1) | Which local model or online service implements `separateAudio`; how the capability view shows "not configured" | Decided: port v2's local vocal separation (HTDemucs-FT) as the executor, wired into the existing separation interface for dubbing. Current status: MLX and candle paths are both wired (§6.1); with `htdemucs-ft@mlx` (Apple Silicon) or `htdemucs-ft@candle` (other platforms) installed, dubbing's separation is run by it; without it, the step is skipped, and the result carries `separation: 'not-configured'` plus a warning; `separateAudio` is in the capability view, with factory defaults and user defaults (the "Automatic selection" option and per-model packages under "Audio separation" in Settings), and the editor's dubbing settings can open separation. Not implemented: a standalone separation command and tool; handing separation to a LAN node; the whole clip is decoded into memory, so memory use grows with length for very long material |
| Speaker-to-voice binding for dubbing (§7.9) | How a multi-speaker video chooses a voice per speaker; what the dubbing plan's `speakerBindingId` points to | Decided: the binding in the video (`speakerVoices` of `library-selection`) takes precedence, then parameter `voice`, finally the model's default voice; when a bound voice is unavailable, that speaker's sentences are not synthesised and are reported sentence by sentence, not swapped for another voice. `speakerBindingId` is the speaker ID that uses a binding in the video; otherwise `null` (video format spec §7.2) |
| Entry point for dubbing (§7.9, §4.8) | Whether agents have a separate tool for translated dubbing; whether MCP and Web services offer it | Decided: no separate tool; within a session the agent completes it with speech synthesis and editing transactions ("the entry point decides the executor"); neither the MCP service nor the Web service offers `pipelines.*` |
| Language of dub tracks (§7.9) | Whether the track needs a language field | Decided: format not changed. The language is written in the track name "Dubbing (<language>)", in the instance's `extensions['cuccut.dub'].language`, and in the dubbing plan |
| Re-dubbing (§7.9) | When dubbing again in the same language, replace the old set or add a new set | Add a new set; the old one is left alone and removed by the user; export selects by `groupId` |
| Source of audio export in other entry points (§9.13) | Whether the agent's export tool and video export accept `source` | Decided: video export and agent `export` (risk tiering unchanged) both accept the same `source` as audio export, changing only the sound plan for this export and not the video. Multi-language audio tracks (one per language) are not implemented |
| Pending objects for the video format | Persistable objects that existed in the prototype but are not yet defined in the video format spec, reviewed in batches and then brought into the format. Later batches: per-word motion and per-caption style overrides, cross-sentence dynamic typesetting, short-film delivery markers, separated music stems, multiple samples for per-sentence synthesis, pronunciation annotation | Decided: only those that render deterministically enter the format. The first batch (segment transitions, element effect stacks and cropping, chapters, sound ducking) has entered the format (video format spec §3.5, §3.9, §3.13). The element-model batch entered the format with fifteen rulings: whole-clip motion (keyframes and element animation, §3.15), whiteboard hand-drawn and particle elements, visualisation elements (waveforms, progress, timers) (§3.7), template aspect lock and caption avoidance (§3.17), and talking-head cut points (§6.7). Items not in the format are implemented first by code synthesis |
| Porting the render core (§9.1, §9.3) | Which side is authoritative for colour and consistency; whether preview shares the implementation; batching | Decided: v2 is authoritative; preview and export share one ported implementation, and v2's reference images are carried over. Batches: base libraries (audio processing, waveforms, media probing); motion library and deterministic primitives; CPU render core and fonts; element layer and the v3 adapter layer; subtitle rendering; native codec and export loop; GPU compositing and preview WASM; loudness mastering and project export. Current status: the pure base libraries `audio-dsp`, `waveform`, `media-probe`, the motion library `motion`, deterministic primitives `scene-primitives`, the CPU render core `render-raster` (together with the bundled fonts), the element layer `timeline-render` and subtitle rendering `subtitle-render` (together with subtitle style data) have been ported verbatim from v2 (algorithms, constants, public interfaces and tests unchanged; internally the algorithms still use floating-point seconds; fixtures are in each crate's `tests/fixtures/`, and built-in recipes are in `motion/presets/`). The adapter-layer batch is wired: `frame-render` draws frame plans into frames, shared by export and preview (via `preview-wasm`); `render-core` and the Canvas drawing in the UI have been deleted (§9.1); `audio-dsp` is referenced only by the Render Worker's loudness mastering (§9.13), `media-probe` still has no process reference, and asset probing still goes through `media-core`'s ffprobe subprocess. Tests against v2's preview shell, v2 editor output and the BCF spec are kept as source but marked ignore. The native codec `media-native` is likewise ported verbatim and is wired into the export loop: the Render Worker's video decoding and H.264 MP4 encoding prefer native, falling back to ffmpeg per asset and per output (§9.11); the Windows native path has not been measured on Windows. `render-raster` enables `media` by default (frame supply and final encoding are native-first with ffmpeg fallback), and v2's tests and reference images pass under v2's criteria; the only things not compiled are the program-gated items (`ccut-compile` is not ported) and tests behind the GPU feature (§13.6). Tests that need ffmpeg are skipped when ffmpeg is absent; `media-native` write tests are skipped when no native encoder is present, and `render-raster`'s final-encoding tests fall back to ffmpeg, skipped only if neither exists; the Lottie corpus fingerprint test needs `CCUT_LOTTIE_CORPUS` pointing to a corpus outside the repository and is skipped without it. `timeline-render` and `subtitle-render` tests pass under v2's feature combinations; subtitle reference images, frame hashes, contracts and comparisons against the prototype (`model-subanim.js` in `designs/`, which needs node) all follow v2's criteria; marked ignore are five tests that read v2's sticker library (`apps/cuccut/assets/stickers`, element assets not brought over) and one that names v2's style gallery source file; the two entries in the "no second subtitle-typesetting implementation" gate that scanned `render-core` are re-enabled, since `render-core` was deleted. The loudness mastering batch is wired: v2's export mastering chain (integrated loudness normalisation alternating with true-peak limiting over three rounds, with a static true-peak fallback) is ported into `export-worker`'s `master`, and its tests compare bit-for-bit with v2's whole-clip algorithm (§9.13). Not implemented: the GPU compositing and project export batches |
| Element model (§13.4) | How videos newly created natively in v3 line up with v2 elements | Decided: the video format adopts v2's element kinds and parameters directly, with no compatibility for existing v3 videos. The fields are written into the video format spec §3.4–§3.9, §3.15–§3.17 and §6.7; `schemaVersion` is raised to 3, and versions 1–2 are refused on open (spec §1.4). Not implemented: `video-model`, `packages/protocol`, VideoEngine and the editor must change together, as a separate batch placed before the element layer of the render core. Field mapping, the fifteen rulings and batching are in [Element model mapping](../design/timeline/element-model-mapping.md) |
| Porting the timeline semantics (§13.6) | What `ccut-timeline` ported; how v2's element model maps onto v3's video format; the order of wiring | Decided: `crates/timeline` is a verbatim port of v2's `ccut-timeline`; algorithms, constants and public names are unchanged, and the tests and the fixtures they read come along in full (fixtures in `crates/timeline/tests/fixtures/`), depending on `motion` and `scene-primitives`. The template layer used by `ccut-protocol`, the four brand types that the watermark conversion reads, and the version constant for `timeline.json` are gathered into an internal `protocol` module; the JSON Schema export of the template layer was not brought over. On-screen text (`screentext`) comes with the crate but is not wired up (see “On-screen text”). The item-by-item comparison of the v2 element model with the v3 video format, time conversion and quantization, the fate of old data, affected modules and batching, and open questions are in [`docs/design/timeline/element-model-mapping.md`](../design/timeline/element-model-mapping.md); v2's design records are in the same directory, and data model names in the text refer to v2. Batches: crate port; format ruling; element model into the video format (see “Element model”); legacy project import rewritten and re-imported; render adapter layer; wiring of cut points, follow, keyframes and ducking; UI calls via WASM. Questions in section 7 of the mapping doc have been ruled (mapping doc §7.1), and the format-ruling batch has been written into the video format spec. Legacy project import has been rewritten: it writes version-3 videos, element fields land in the formal fields, the cut set is stored as a document, and the rules and fields that have no counterpart are in section 5 of the mapping doc. The editing-semantics batch is wired: VideoEngine's manual cuts and restore (`addCuts`, `restoreCut`) call `timeline::cuts`; the keyframe conversion for split, trim, ripple and merge (`joinItems`) calls `timeline::keyframes`; the ducking gain curve (including ducking triggered from the transcript) is computed by `render-graph` calling `timeline::duck`; `follow-cuts`, `sequence-fixed`, to-sequence-end, `item-local` and `speech-anchor` are implemented; cut-suggestion detection and acceptance (`proposeCuts`, `acceptCutSuggestions`) call `timeline::detect`; the UI's transcript cutting, restore and cut-band range change send `addCuts` and `restoreCut`. What remains is in “Open items in editing semantics”. The render adapter layer batch is wired (`frame-render`, §9.1). The UI batch is wired: through `bindings/editor-wasm` it calls the stage frame, the engine's value ranges and defaults, and the style catalogs for the audio waveform, progress bar and confetti (§13.2); what remains is in “Open items in editing semantics” |
| Open items in editing semantics (§13.6) | The parts of the editing-semantics batch that are not finished, or were done on assumption | Decided: instances placed by time default to `follow-cuts` from creation, whether or not a cut set exists; `sequence-fixed` must be chosen explicitly (`updateItem` can also change it); instances that were created earlier without a strategy are read as the default, with no migration (video format spec §3.16). Decided: the source duration for `addCuts` is read by the engine from the probe data of the current media version; callers do not pass it, and the call is refused when no known duration exists. Decided: a cut proposal is the minimal document `cuccut.editorial-proposal/1` (video format spec §6.2): the source intervals suggested for cutting, category, text and confidence. `proposeCuts` ports v2's filler-word and pause detection (`timeline::detect`, default parameters as in v2); v2 has no duplicate-segment detection, so none is proposed. `acceptCutSuggestions` compiles into `addCuts` with a `ref` and marks the proposal `accepted` in the same transaction, and is refused after the transcript has been changed (`proposal-stale`). Decided: `speech-anchor` is wired (`anchors` in `video-engine`): at the end of every transaction, anchors are re-solved and placed using the same word projection as transcript-driven ducking (`render_graph::text_plan`) and the rules of `timeline::anchors`; occurrences follow v2 (a midpoint mapping to more than one place is ambiguous, and `occurrenceId` names the instance that acts, or the instance before it was split). Anchors that cannot be solved or cannot be placed stay where they are, are listed in the receipt's `orphanedAnchors` and in `videos.inspect`, and are not written to disk (video format spec §3.16). Decided: `item-local` is wired (`item_local` in `video-engine`): at the end of every transaction, the target is recomputed from the changes before and after the target and from the split lineage. A target with a source clock follows by source time, and when split it follows the segment that contains its anchor; when a start trim crosses the anchor, the start moves to the new start. If the target is deleted, the anchor is deleted with it and listed in the receipt's `removedWithTarget` (video format spec §3.16). Decided: `explicit-link-group` is outside the scope of this version; only its shape is stored and validated, and it does not move when edited. It is v3's own, and its linkage rules are not decided. When one cut maps to partially overlapping intervals on the audio and video tracks, the whole transaction is refused (`scope-misaligned`); a user who places audio and video out of alignment must align them first. Instances locked to sequence end also update along with it (spec §3.16). The `fixed` value of `durationPolicy` has no operation that can write it yet. Decided: the UI's transcript cutting, restore and cut-band range change all go through the media's cut set: the selected words are converted to media seconds and sent as one `addCuts`; restore finds the cut in the cut set that covers the selected words (or the seam on the timeline) and compiles it to `restoreCut`, putting the whole cut back; a range change is `restoreCut` plus `addCuts` in the same transaction, and undo goes back one step. The `removeRange` path, which computed intervals from instance placement, and the `moveItems` plus `trimItem` path, have been removed along with their tests. Cuts that were not made as cuts (trimmed by dragging a segment edge, or made before the cut set existed) are refused as-is on restore (`untracked`); a range change also requires that the cut covers exactly the seam within half a frame (otherwise `partial`). Differences from before: restore granularity is the whole cut, and no longer only up to the selected words; `addCuts` ripple-deletes only on the track where the instance playing this media sits, and other tracks only move their `follow-cuts` instances, so an instance linked to the cut instance (`linkGroupId`) that also explicitly chose `sequence-fixed` will drift out of step with it; captions that cross a cut are shortened under `follow-cuts`, not split. Chapter cutting (`chapters.ts`) is still `removeRange` plus a delete marker, moving later chapters forward, and is not recorded in the cut set. Caption line breaking has not been unified onto the crate's `derive_cues`: switching would change caption cues and their IDs; the differences are in §7.9. The upper limit on transition duration (10 seconds) and the range for dub speed change (0.1–10) are still literals in `video-engine`; `editor-wasm` does not depend on VideoEngine, so the UI copies each one. They will move into `timeline` or `video-model` constants and then be switched over. The 51 text presets are still UI data: the v2 recipe IDs and style fields differ from the UI's, so they must be compared one by one first, and resolving text presets also pulls in the whole registry of animations and graphics. When creating new text, the text-box measurement still uses Canvas 2D: the code that renders measured text lives inside `render-raster`, and to move it into `preview-wasm` (where the fonts are) it must first be made public |
| Quick fix (§9.11) | Where the baseline artifacts are stored, how long they are kept, and when they become invalid | Decided: keep this capability, low priority, placed after the render core port. Storage rules are not decided |
| Licence for ported code (§13.6) | v2 is under a proprietary licence; v3's original code and ported code are distributed under the repository's current licence | Decided: v3 uses the [CucCut Community License 1.0](../../LICENSE); the licence boundary is in the [root README](../../README.md#许可). v2 remains private. Ported files do not carry v2's licence notice; third-party components keep their own licences, and rights already granted under Apache-2.0 for historical material are not withdrawn |
| Built-in fonts and elements (§13.6) | Whether fonts (about 25 MB) and element assets ship with the application; where the attribution for CC BY assets goes | Decided: fonts and element assets come with the render core and ship with the application, together with their own licence files and attribution. Current state: fonts come with the CPU render core (`render-raster/assets/fonts/`, including `LICENSES/`, `NOTICE.md`, `README.md` and the source manifests `verified-sources.json` and `subtitle-design-fonts.json`). The caption styles read by caption rendering (`core/assets/captions/styles`, the recipe textures and their licence notes, `subtitle-designs.json`) come with `subtitle-render` under its `assets/`; fonts are not carried separately. Element assets (v2 `core/assets/elements` and the sticker library `apps/cuccut/assets/stickers` in the app) and the style gallery's `subtitle-gallery.json` have no module reading them, and are left to the batches that use them. Undecided: where the attribution appears in the application |
| On-screen text (§13.6) | v2's on-screen text scanning and translation (about 3700 lines) has no corresponding feature in v3's product design | Decided: this feature is wanted, low priority, and placed after the porting tracks; its place in the product design and video format is filled in before porting. Not implemented |
| Tool catalog and video targets (§7.9) | `tools.list`, `tools.candidates`, the `target` pipeline parameter (`entryId`, `create`), and opening a video on behalf of the user | Decided: `tools.list` is the static registry plus current availability; `tools.candidates` gives, according to each tool's rules and from the content index and Space directory, the videos and transcripts to choose from, and does not open videos; the contract is in command protocol spec §4.1. The content index cache (version 2) records the facts of transcripts, translations and dub groups (§5.11); caches of older versions are queued for re-reading when read back. `translate`, `dub`, `link-import` and `transcribe` accept `target` (`videoId`, `entryId`; `link-import` and `transcribe` also accept `create`, and `transcribe`'s `create.media` is a local media file) and open the video on their behalf; the contract is in the `pipelines` section of the command protocol spec. The `transcribe` pipeline is implemented; its inputs in the catalog are `file` and `video`; links go through `link-import`. Subtitle-file input for translation goes through `translate-subtitles` (see “Translating subtitle files”). Not implemented: judging whether a translation in the index is stale when the glossary changes |
| Transcription tool's caption track (§7.9, product design S01) | Who creates the caption track after transcription; how to avoid duplicates when a caption track already exists | Decided: the last step of the `transcribe` and `translate` pipelines, `captions`, creates the caption document and caption instances in one transaction; if the same transcript (or translation) already has a caption track, it is skipped. A new source-language caption track is added in a disabled state when source-language captions are already showing for the same media. `transcribe` creates it by default; the `translate` pipeline parameter does not create it by default, but the tool entry (`execution.params` in the tool catalog), the CLI and the editor's “Translate into…” (`translate-run`, together with `bilingual`) pass `captions: true`; the editor's “Generate captions” and “Re-transcribe” (`transcribe-run`) use the `transcribe` pipeline. The editor reports results according to the summary's `captions`, and when newly created, undo uses its `transactionId`. After transcription, `link-import` also has a `captions` step, which the pipeline parameter does not create by default; when `transcribe` is given a `url` it passes `captions: true` (not passed under `noCaptions`), and `download --transcribe` does not create one |
| Translating subtitle files (§7.9) | When translating SRT or VTT directly, how timing and sentence splitting are handled; whether “create video from this” imports as a low-precision transcript or as a caption document | Decided: the file-to-file `translate-subtitles` pipeline translates cue by cue without re-splitting sentences; timecodes are kept as-is and read back to be checked before publishing; a file that cannot be read accurately is refused at submission. The contract is in the `pipelines` section of the command protocol spec. Not implemented: “create video from this” (planned to import as a low-precision transcript without per-word timing, product design S01); inline style markup is not preserved |
| Default project for tool-created videos (product design §2.7) | Where to put it the first time, when there is no project yet | Undecided: default to the last project used; when there is no project, first have the user choose a directory to create a project in, and do not create one automatically beside the media |
| On-the-spot authorisation on the tool page (§7.9, §12.5) | The return shape for pending approval items; one consent covering several outbound sends in a pipeline | Decided: `details.pendingGrants` is an array of `GrantRequestItem` (`reason` gains `exhausted`), given only to a user who is present (a desktop UI connection on the main gateway) for `pipelines.start` and direct tasks; one refusal covers all the outbound sends in the pipeline, and `remedy.commands` is merged and kept. The client calls `grants.create` item by item and then resubmits with the same `commandId`. The Runtime does not issue grants automatically and does not loosen anything; CLI, agents, browser sessions and external services are unchanged (§7.9). Not implemented: the desktop UI is not yet connected to pending approval items |
| Porting the subtitle and translation core (speech-doc) | What the first batch ported, and what stays in v2 | Decided: `crates/speech-doc` is a verbatim port of v2's `ccut-flow-core`; algorithms, thresholds, public types and function names are unchanged, and all of its tests come along: post-processing of transcription (word tokenization, sentence breaking, paragraphing, speakers), the carrier and engine for translation and alignment (`filepipe`, `engines`, `align_block`), and subtitle splitting and layout (`cue`, `split`, `layout`). In addition, v2 `ccut-speech-core`'s `RowIn`, `WordIn` and `word_breaks`, and `ccut-protocol`'s transcription version constants, are gathered into internal modules. `export` is v2 core's subtitle export (srt / vtt / ass / json-words; bilingual output is segmented by the union of source cue and translation-segment boundaries, and a missing translation does not fall back to the source text), and depends only on `TranscriptDoc` and plain data. `check` has only a report summary, and `translate_flow` is the pure decision for the final round of translate. The parts of v2 that read and write the project directory, go through the timeline projection, or call a Provider were not brought over (including `check_verdict`, `align_params_for`, `render_project_timeline_subtitles`, the `timeline_timed_words` used only by it, and reading `ass_play_res` from `project.json`). The two pure-function blocks that depend on subtitle rendering or the timeline schema are in `subtitle-render`: `ass_style_sheet`, which generates the ASS style header from project styles (producing this crate's `AssStyleSheet`, with the frame size passed in by the caller), and `place_out_of_canvas` for checking; this crate does not depend back on rendering. Test fixtures are in `crates/speech-doc/tests/fixtures/`; offline replay of model responses is in `crates/speech-doc/bench/` (no model calls); design records are in `docs/design/subtitle/`, and model names in the text refer to v2. Undecided: how the parts not brought over are filled in when wiring. `speech-worker` and `bindings/editor-wasm` (through `speech-doc-bridge`) now reference this crate; the mapping and wiring are in the next row |
| Wiring the subtitle and translation core (§7.9) | The order in which `speech-doc` is wired into the pipelines | Decided: the whole crate is ported verbatim and not rewritten in TypeScript; engines that call models live in the `speech-worker` process, and model calls are handed back to the Runtime. Four batches: porting the crate and its test set; mapping `TranscriptDoc` onto the v3 document and format supplements; switching `translate`, `translate-subtitles` and the caption track over to the crate (translation brief, reading budget, quality gate, alignment, timing projection of translated captions, checking), while letting the caption track be created for transcripts imported from links and the operation that creates caption tracks for agents; transcription post-processing (polishing, segmentation, speaker repair). The second batch is complete: `to_transcript_doc` and `from_transcript_doc` in `crates/speech-doc-bridge`; `cuccut.speech/1` gains `glue`, `userBreaks`, `autoBreaks`, `layoutProfileId`, `paragraphBreaks` and `stages`; `cuccut.translation/2` gains `displayRewrite.naturalFingerprint`, `alignment.split`, and block `confidence` and `flags` (video format spec §5.2, §5.3); `video-model` has DTOs for these two bodies, and the engine checks the shape of the added fields; v2's samples and bodies written by the existing pipeline round-trip losslessly. Decided: sentence derivation and the source-sentence fingerprint are unified onto the crate's version (v2's rules); in the third batch the translation pipeline and the UI are changed to call it, and no longer compute their own. `cuccut.translation/1` is not kept; legacy project import writes `/2` directly along with the supplemented fields; block-level alignment with no blocks is kept with v2's semantics, and the spec will be updated in the third batch. The first half of the third batch is complete: `crates/speech-worker` (protocol `speech-worker/1`, command protocol spec §4.5) runs translation in one process (brief, paged translation, quality gate and make-up pass, alignment, final round, and the timing projection of `derive_trans_cues`), and through the mapping writes the `cuccut.translation/2` body and target-language caption cues, with one checkpoint per page, so resuming does not redo work. `runSpeechTranslate` in `@cuccut/jobs` starts it, sends each model call out through `text.generate` and hands it back; `npm run build:engine` builds it along with the rest. Differences from the former one-step translation (now in effect): when the source and target languages have the same primary subtag, it is refused (v2's behaviour, for example `zh` into `zh-Hant`); sentences still missing after the make-up pass fail with `MODEL_OUTPUT_INVALID`, and the checkpoint is kept; glossary is not truncated per batch; v2's analysis round is not done. Decided: the UI and Node call the crate's sentence splitting and fingerprinting through `bindings/editor-wasm` (the boundary is the `cuccut.speech/1` body, and times are integer ticks). The one-step translation in the third batch is now wired: `translate` (including translation required when dubbing lacks a translation) goes through Worker translation, and `captions` uses the Worker's caption cues; the translation pipeline, the UI's translation checks, dub staleness checks and the content index all check against the crate's sentences and fingerprints; `cuccut.sentences/1` and `sha256:` fingerprints are removed and are not compatible. The operation that creates caption tracks for agents is complete: `captions_create` (§3.5) aligns translations by sentence and splits cues by display width, does not go through Worker, and Runtime fills in the alignment for translations written by agents by sentence. Not implemented: `translate-subtitles` and the source-language caption track have not switched to the crate (the differences in source caption line breaking are in §7.9); creating caption tracks for transcripts imported from links; checking; the fourth batch; the remaining callers of the mapping; legacy project import does not write supplemental fields (line breaks and segmentation are still in `legacy`), and `cuccut.translation/1` cannot be converted |
| Reading speed table (§7.9) | v2 has three tables with different bases: one for splitting, one for the translation budget, and one for layout by writing system | Decided: unified into the one used for splitting (zh 9; ja and ko 13; th 15; ar and hi 18; others 21). The existing test set does not cover the translation budget for Thai, Arabic and Hindi, so test cases for these languages must be added before unifying. Not implemented |
